# The evidence by question

Built by code from the nine readers and their verifiers (workflow A, 2026-11-06). Each answer is followed by its verifier's verdict; the verifier's correction governs where they differ. The record points, where a reader found the record contradicted or qualified, follow at the end.

## Q1: Identification

#### r1-priority: Q1 (reader confidence high)

Yes, as a proposal. Minic 2020 section 5 calls the duals of the Standard Model matter Lagrangian 'that could be interpreted as dark matter Lagrangian'. Section 6.2 says 'The proposal here is that the dual sector ... should be interpreted as the dark matter sector'. The abstract states that metaparticle quanta are 'the natural quanta of dark matter in this approach'. A related conditional reads 'If the quanta of modified dark matter are metaparticles, this may explain why ...'. The Belgrade slides (9 Sep 2019) state it as phenomenology, with a question mark on 'hidden sector?'.

Weaker forms: speculation in FLM 2017; 'relates to' in BHM 2019; 'may play the role of dark energy/matter' in FKGLM 2021.

The abstract's metaparticle is the particle plus its dual, entangled, so 'metaparticle quanta are dark matter' is a different and earlier claim (2017) than 'the dual of visible matter is dark matter' (2019-2020). Nothing is derived. Against #11 App. A item 4: the school's dark fields Ã(x, x̃) are integrated over x̃ and live on ordinary spacetime x, rather than being matter in the other polarization of one phase space.

Evidence: 2003.00318 abstract, section 5 eq. (49)-(50), section 6.2, section 4.3; talk:mphys10-2019-Minic slide 5; recid:1590124 p. 19; 1905.08269 conclusion; 2104.00802 introduction and last paragraph

**Verifier (r1-priority-verify): confirmed-in-part.** The identification is a proposal, as the answer says, and the metaparticle/dual distinction is right. The answer omits a passage from Minic 2020 §3. That section, which the paper ties to §5 ('see section 5'), says the doubled field formulation adds no degrees of freedom: in a chiral action the 'magnetic' duals φ̃ do not count separately. The paper does not say how the duals of §5 and §6.2 then form an additional dark sector (my reading).

Counter-evidence: 2003.00318 §3, after eq. (22): 'There is no overcounting here, because the action will turn out to be chiral. So even though one has formally doubled the number of variables, the chiral nature of the theory, keeps the total number of degrees of freedom the same as in the purely electric formulation.' (FOUND)

#### r2-dual-sm: Q1 Identification (reader confidence high)

Yes. All five dark-matter papers state it (2109.01122 has no dark-matter content), with varying firmness. Abstracts and conclusions state it as an outcome: 'dark matter ... comes from the degrees of freedom dual to the visible matter' (2010.15610 abstract, §8), a 'new understanding of dark matter in terms of metaparticles' (2109.12763 abstract), 'The dark matter degrees of freedom are thus tied to the dual particles to the visible SM particles' (2307.16712 §5). The body frames it as a proposal or candidacy: 'can be interpreted as' (2010.15610 §1), 'The proposal here is that the dual sector ... should be interpreted as the dark matter sector' (2010.15610 §3, 2109.12763 §5, 2212.06086 §6.3), 'which we propose' (2109.12763 §4), 'good candidates' (2212.06086 §4.3), 'a candidate dark matter companion' (2307.16712 §3). No paper derives it; the only observational support offered is MDM's fits. The closest wording to C1 is 2212.06086 §6.4: 'the dual particles are conjugate to the Standard Model particles in analogy with the canonical fact that momenta p are conjugate to coordinates q', glossed as the dual fields not commuting with SM fields. That is field-space conjugacy of each field with its dual, a different relation from #11 P4's separate p-polarized species (my reading).

Evidence: 2010.15610 abstract; 2010.15610 §1; 2010.15610 §3 after eq. (8); 2010.15610 §8; 2109.12763 abstract; 2109.12763 §4; 2212.06086 §4.3; 2212.06086 §6.4; 2212.06086 §6.5; 2307.16712 §3; 2307.16712 §5

**Verifier (r2-dual-sm-verify): confirmed-in-part.** Four of the six assigned papers state the identification in words, not five: 2010.15610, 2109.12763, 2212.06086 and 2307.16712. 2111.14205 calls the dual gauge fields '“dark”' but never writes 'dark matter', and 2109.01122 has no dark-matter content. 2212.06086 §6.3 does not say 'The proposal here'. It says 'The main point here is that the dual sector … should be interpreted as the dark matter sector' (lit.py quote: 'The proposal here is that the dual sector' NOT FOUND in 2212.06086; the 'main point' wording FOUND). The strength ranges from 'could be interpreted as the dark matter Lagrangian' (2109.12763 §4) and 'we argue' (2010.15610 §2; 2212.06086 §6.2) up to the framework's 'most important general predictions … the dual matter degrees of freedom naturally appear as dark matter candidates' (2010.15610 §5; similarly 2212.06086 §6.5). Two of the reader's conclusions stand: no paper derives the identification, and the §6.4 'conjugate' means field-space non-commutativity.

Counter-evidence: 2212.06086 §6.3: 'The main point here is that the dual sector (as already indicated in the previous section) should be interpreted as the dark matter sector,' (FOUND) | 2010.15610 §5: '(2) the dual matter degrees of freedom naturally appear as dark matter candidates, as discussed in the preceding section.' (FOUND) | 2212.06086 §6.5: 'The most important generic predictions of the generic string theory[242] is that the geometry of the dual spacetime determines the dark energy sector[264] and that the dual matter degrees of freedom naturally appear as dark matter degrees of freedom' (FOUND) | 2109.12763 §4: 'the effective action for the SM matter Lagrangian ( L_{m} ) and their duals (that could be interpreted as the dark matter Lagrangian L_{dm} ) takes the form' (FOUND) | 2010.15610 §2: 'In what follows, we argue the dual part of the matter sector appears as dark matter, which is in turn both sensitive to dark energy [18] and also dynamically correlated with the visible matter.' (FOUND)

#### r3-gravitized: Q1 (reader confidence high)

Yes, in four of the six texts read. The strength ranges from a parenthesis to a repeated proposal, and the latest text is the most hedged.
- 2203.17137 (2022) and 2303.15645 (2023): no mention of dark matter ('dark' occurs 0 times in either full text).
- Minic 2023 (2305.12593; the same text in Bulg. J. Phys. 51, 2024): one parenthesis. Metaparticles are 'correlated objects of visible particles and their duals, which, in turn, represent natural candidates for dark matter' (proposed; the antecedent of 'which' is open).
- Hübsch–Minic 2024 (2407.06207): the most frequent statement, as a proposal the authors 'will argue'. 'Metaparticles appear as natural dark matter quanta' (§3.1); 'dual particles, correlated to visible particles, represent dark matter' (§3.3); dual fields are 'natural candidates for dark matter' (§3.4); each SM particle's dual is 'the most obvious candidate dark matter' (§5.1); 'the dark matter sector can be seen as coming from a dual Standard Model' (§6).
- Hübsch–Minic April 2026 (2604.19418): repeats this nearly word for word. 'Dual particles and fields in this picture model (fuzzy) dark matter'; 'every Standard Model particle has a dual fuzzy counterpart'.
- Hübsch–Minic October 2026 (2610.19522): hedged. The dual field 'is a candidate for the invisible matter sector' (§3.2); the theory 'has the potential to describe' it (§5).
Two formulations alternate without comment: dark matter as the whole metaparticle (2407.06207 §3.1, §3.4; 2604.19418 §7), or as the dual partner alone (2407.06207 §3.3, §5.1; 2604.19418 §5.1, §7). In every case the dark field φ̃ is a second field on a doubled space that does not commute with φ, and it is the background of a dual momentum p̃. In #11, χ is matter in the other polarization of the one phase space (P4, P5).

Evidence: 2203.17137 full text: 'dark' 0 occurrences (executed grep); 2303.15645 full text v2: 'dark' 0 occurrences; 2305.12593 §2, end: 'metaparticles (correlated objects of visible particles and their duals, which, in turn, represent natural candidates for dark matter [10])'; recid:2778134 p. 65: same sentence; 2407.06207 §3.1: 'Metaparticles appear as natural dark matter quanta'; 2407.06207 §3.3: '(We will argue that dual particles, correlated to visible particles, represent dark matter.)'; 2407.06207 §3.4: 'dual “particles” (dual fields) are natural candidates for dark matter'; 2407.06207 §5.1: 'the latter of which furnishing the most obvious candidate dark matter[103, 104]'; 2407.06207 §6: 'the dark matter sector can be seen as coming from a dual Standard Model'; 2604.19418 §1.1 and §5.1; 2610.19522 §3.2: 'it is a candidate for the invisible matter sector'; 2610.19522 §5: 'has the potential to describe the dual (invisible) matter sector'

**Verifier (r3-gravitized-verify): confirmed-in-part.** The identification statements and their strengths are quoted and graded correctly. Four of the six texts state the identification; the other two, 2203.17137 and 2303.15645, have 0 matches for 'dark' (verifier's count). Two overstatements:
- 'In every case the dark field φ̃ ... is the background of a dual momentum p̃' holds only for 2407.06207 §3.3 and 2604.19418 §5.1. Minic 2023 makes no field-level statement at all. 2610.19522 §3.2 states that φ̃ does not commute with φ, but not the p̃-background.
- 'The latest text is the most hedged' is a judgment. Minic 2023's 'natural candidates for dark matter' is as hedged as 2610.19522's 'a candidate for the invisible matter sector'.
The answer also omits a restatement in 2604.19418 §5.2 (below).

Counter-evidence: 2305.12593 §2: 'metaparticles (correlated objects of visible particles and their duals, which, in turn, represent natural candidates for dark matter [10])'. No dual field and no p̃-background appear in the paper. | 2610.19522 §3.2: 'it is a candidate for the invisible matter sector (dual to the observed, visible, Standard Model sector)'. Non-commutation is stated; the p̃-background is not. | 2604.19418 §5.2: 'At the same time, metastring has the relevant structure to reproduce the SM degrees of freedom and their dual, “dark” counterparts, as well as naturally small vacuum energy.' (lit.py: FOUND)

#### r4-pheno: Q1 (reader confidence high)

Four of the nine papers restate the identification, always credited to Minic 2020 (arXiv:2003.00318) and Berglund–Hübsch–Minic, LHEP 2021 (arXiv:2010.15610), and always as a proposal. (1) The Snowmass paper 2202.05104 says 'dark matter should be viewed as a dual SM'. It states this as the group's approach and says it 'has been recently proposed [29, 30]'. (2) 2202.06890 and Addazi et al. §2.2.2.5 say each observed field's 'dual' 'could be associated with dark matter', a proposal. (3) Addazi §2.3.4 lists 'their “duals”, associated with dark matter degrees of freedom' as an observable hallmark. Its §2.2.2.3 calls 'dual matter and dual gravity sectors' a generic prediction of modular spacetime but does not name dark matter in that sentence. (4) 2011.08852 §6 says the dual part of the matter sector 'can be naturally related to the dark matter sector that is sensitive to dark energy', a proposal citing the modified-dark-matter papers. Five papers do not state it. 2202.05266 is agnostic about the theory. In 2503.20854 dark matter is part of the matter Lagrangian 'put in by hand'. 2212.00901 and 2303.17495 have no dark sector. 2312.00409 has only a bullet on the metaparticle and Born reciprocity. None of the nine says the dual is the momentum side; each describes it as 'the dual spacetime' of a non-commutative, T-duality covariant string theory.

Evidence: 2202.05104, paragraph 'Constraints on the nature of dark matter'; 2202.06890, paragraph 'Observations'; 2111.05659, §2.2.2.3, §2.2.2.5, §2.3.4; 2011.08852, §6; 2202.05266, §1; 2503.20854, §2 and App. B after eq. (100); 2312.00409, §2.1.2

**Verifier (r4-pheno-verify): confirmed-in-part.** The count holds: four of the nine state the identification and five do not. Three wordings are stronger than the papers. (a) 'always credited to Minic 2020 and Berglund–Hübsch–Minic 2021': only the Snowmass paper cites both ([29, 30]). 2202.06890 ([11]) and Addazi et al. ([408]) cite Minic 2020 alone. 2011.08852's sentence cites the modified-dark-matter papers [55] and [26–30], of which [29] includes Minic 2020; BHM 2021 is its [46], cited elsewhere in the paper. (b) 'always as a proposal': Addazi §2.3.4 lists the duals 'associated with dark matter degrees of freedom' among 'observable hallmarks', and the Snowmass paper states 'dark matter should be viewed as a dual SM' as the group's approach. The reader's own details (1) and (3) say this; its summary sentence does not. (c) 'each describes it as the dual spacetime' holds for the four that state the identification only: 2212.00901 and 2303.17495 use x̃ for the momentum coordinate of a modular cell, 2202.05266 has no dual, and 2312.00409 does not describe one.

Counter-evidence: 2202.06890, paragraph 'Observations': '…each observed quantum field has a “dual”, which could be associated with dark matter degrees of freedom [11].' ([11] is Minic 2020 only) | 2111.05659 §2.3.4: 'This approach also sheds new light on the GUP, and leads to observable hallmarks, such as:' … '(ii) their “duals”, associated with dark matter degrees of freedom [408]' ([408] is Minic 2020 only; both FOUND) | 2011.08852 §6: '…as predicted by the above generic non-commutative formulation of string theory/quantum gravity [26, 27, 28, 29, 30].' ([29] includes D. Minic, arXiv:2003.00318; FOUND) | 2212.00901, after eq. (10): 'Here we have split the integral over phase space into a sum over integrals in a finite cell, via p\to\varepsilon\tilde{x} , q\to\lambda x .' (FOUND)

#### r5-mdm: Q1 (reader confidence high)

No MDM paper (2010–2024) states that dark matter is the dual or conjugate counterpart of visible matter. MDM's 'duality' is the CDM–MOND duality, one force law read either as modified gravity or as dark matter with a proposed profile, and in 2017 the review says 'there is no specific candidate for the MDM quantum'. The steps toward the dual sector, each a pointer, proposal or speculation: (1) 4 Jan 2016 (1601.00662 §4), a pointer: such non-local quanta 'could be found' in the Freidel–Leigh–Minic reformulations. (2) 13 Sep 2017 (1709.04388 §5), a proposal: metastring excitations 'can serve as unusual dark matter quanta', and the zero-mode sector, 'two entangled particles', 'could be viewed as a model for modified dark matter quanta'. (3) 30 Dec 2017 (1801.00160 §5), a speculation: MDM quanta 'might be modeled as metaparticles ... two entangled particles in momentum space'; the record does not mention this paper. (4) 18 May 2020 (2005.08927), inside 'Let us speculate', metaparticles as 'A possible candidate'. The identification of MDM's quanta with the dual cousins of Standard Model fields is made outside the MDM papers, in Minic 2020 (2003.00318, 29 Feb 2020): 'the natural model for MDM quanta could be provided by the metaparticle realizations of the Standard Model particles', with the baryons' 'natural cousins (the Ã backgrounds ...)' in the dark sector; that is a proposal. Edmonds, Minic and Takeuchi repeat it in 2021 (2109.12763: 'Thus the entire SM would have a dual SM (which we propose, describes the dark matter sector)'). The later observational papers of the MDM line (2009.12915, Sep 2020; 2405.08744, May 2024) do not mention the dual sector, metaparticles or Born geometry (word counts 0). Relative to #11 App. A item 4, the MDM line is a precursor of the school's identification, not a statement of C1.

Evidence: 1005.3537 abstract; 1709.04388 §5 (no specific candidate; zero-mode sector as model); 1601.00662 §4; 1801.00160 §5 last paragraph; 2005.08927 final paragraphs (v1 PDF checked); 2003.00318 §5; 2109.12763 §4; 2009.12915 and 2405.08744 full texts (word counts)

**Verifier (r5-mdm-verify): confirmed.** No correction. Two additions. (1) The 2017 MDM texts point to metaparticle theory for their 'two entangled particles'. In that theory (Freidel, Kowalski-Glikman, Leigh, Minic, arXiv:1812.10821, posted 27 Dec 2018), the 'entanglement' joins the two chiral components of one metaparticle, with chiral momenta p± = ½(p ± h p̃), and it 'prevents an interpretation as two particle species'. That paper never mentions dark matter (my grep: 0). So the 2017 phrase models the dark quantum by itself, not a visible particle paired with a dark partner. This supports the reader's 'not yet the identification'. (2) 'No MDM paper' depends on where the line is drawn. The MDM authors Edmonds, Minic and Takeuchi state the dual-SM proposal in 2109.12763 (Sep 2021), whose §6.1 reviews MDM. Edmonds also co-signs the 2022 Snowmass paper 2202.05104, which the record lists as partial.

Counter-evidence: 1812.10821 §3.1: 'creates a fundamental ‘entanglement’ of these chiral particles which prevents an interpretation as two particle species' (FOUND) | 1812.10821 §3.1: 'it is possible to kinematically decouple the metaparticle system as a sum of two free relativistic particles' (FOUND) | 2109.12763 §4: 'The generic prediction here is the existence of a dual field' [Ã, 'which is correlated (entangled), with the original A field'] (FOUND)

#### r6-foundations: Q1 (reader confidence high)

Not addressed in these papers. None of the seven (posted 26 July 2013 to 27 December 2018) mentions dark matter: tools/lit.py text --grep 'dark' finds zero matches in each full text. None states, proposes or speculates that dark matter is the dual or conjugate counterpart of visible matter. The nearest content is the metaparticle's 'dual particle, dual anti-particle pair' (1812.10821 §3.2), two further poles of the same propagator related to the particle pair by E ↔ μ/E, which 'corresponds to the exchange of p with p̃'; ordinary particles are the sector with μ = 0 and vanishing dual momentum p̃, 'a consistent truncation' preserved by interactions (§2.1). Neither is given a dark-matter role. This agrees with the record's dating of the school's identification to Minic 2020 (arXiv:2003.00318), which these papers precede; I did not read Minic 2020.

Evidence: all seven full texts: zero matches for 'dark' (tools/lit.py text --grep); 1812.10821 §3.2 (dual particle, dual anti-particle pair; exchange of p with p̃); 1812.10821 §2.1 (ordinary particles: μ = 0, p̃ = 0, consistent truncation)

**Verifier (r6-foundations-verify): confirmed.**

#### r8-outside: Q1 (reader confidence medium)

I found no paper outside the school that states the identification in the school's form or the program's. One comes close, and it is independent of both:

- **Sheng Ran (Washington University in St. Louis), 2025.** A report first posted 24 December 2025 (doi:10.55277/researchhub.unu7fof1.1/.2; CC-BY), on his framework paper (Phys. Rev. A 2026, arXiv:2511.20907). One global quantum state has 'two conjugate dynamical descriptions—one on the familiar space–time manifold and one on a complementary momentum–energy manifold'. In the report's words, 'Here we show that dark matter arises naturally as a structural consequence of the coupling between these two dynamical sectors.' Strength: stated as a result, in a report that is not peer reviewed. His dark matter is 'not a new particle species' but 'an evanescent projection of a single quantum state across two dynamically conjugate manifolds'. So it originates in the momentum–energy description; it is not a counterpart copy of ordinary matter.

Other outside statements use other meanings of 'dual' or other ideas:
- Häuser and Dröscher 2019 (abstract only), stated as an assumption: 'Dark matter is assumed to reside in' a dual spacetime with imaginary time, 'and therefore is principally invisible'; the dark matter is negative-mass particles.
- Nishimura 2022, a proposal: a negative-norm sector of a dual 'isospin spacetime' 'responsible for the dark matter and the dark energy'.
- Bolognesi 2012 and 2015, a modelling choice: in a Born-reciprocal x↔p theory, the one matter field is 'to be identified with dark matter'. It is not the counterpart of visible matter.
- Castro Perelman 2018 (abstract only): dark matter is an effect of observing ordinary matter from accelerating frames.
- Hazboun and Wheeler 2013: geometric terms of a phase-space-like biconformal geometry as a dark-matter candidate.
- Vasconcellos and Hadjimichef 2014, tentative: dark-matter fermions charged under the electric–magnetic 'dual partner of the photon'.
- Biswas et al. 2005: winding modes among 'stringy cold dark matter'.

Papers that cite the school do not take up the identification:
- Maldonado and Mendez place ordinary dark matter 'only in one patch ... patch a'.
- Chouha cites FKLM 2021 for 'some application to dark matter/energy' without proposing it.
- Calmet and Kuipers, and Kuipers's thesis, cite Minic 2020 only for an infrared cutoff.
- Addazi et al. (with Minic among the 162 authors) state 'the existence of dual matter and dual gravity sectors' without the dark-matter link in that passage.

Evidence: doi:10.55277/researchhub.unu7fof1.2 abstract; §V; 2511.20907 abstract; doi:10.1515/zna-2018-0559 abstract; doi:10.1515/zna-2021-0147 abstract; 2212.13131 abstract and final section; 1506.02187 abstract; §4; 1207.5514 §1; doi:10.1139/cjp-2018-0097 abstract; 1305.6972 §7.4; 1404.0409 abstract; §II.3; hep-th/0507199 abstract; 2304.00620 §III; recid:2704811 §2; 2602.21407 §3.1; 2009.11575 main text; recid:2127250; 2111.05659 §2.2.2.3

**Verifier (r8-outside-verify): confirmed-in-part.** Four corrections. (1) Addazi et al. 2022 do take up the identification, as a possibility. Apart from the §2.2.2.3 passage the reader quotes, the review says (§2.2.2.5) that 'each observed quantum field has a “dual”, which could be associated with dark matter', and (§2.3.4) lists 'their “duals”, associated with dark matter degrees of freedom [408]'. It is also not outside the school, since Minic and Kowalski-Glikman are coauthors. That is the record's 'partial'. (2) Two more outside statements using other meanings of 'dual' were missed. Houlden 2015 (proceedings 'Unified Field Mechanics'; not in the record) proposes dark matter as massive vacuum states in Rowlands' 'dual space to the real space where Standard Model fundamental fermions (and we) reside', states that 'interacts gravitationally'. Huang and Huang 1999 put light dark matter 'in a dual SM or two-fold SM'; this is a phrase collision, already in the record as such. Round 3 retrieved it, verified its quotes and labeled it C1 related (downgraded from partial; findings.json, round3/oa-c1-c6-c7.verify.json). Only round 1 logged it as not found.

#### r9-sweep: Q1 (reader confidence high)

Yes, as a proposal or candidate, never derived, from February 2020 on and always at leading order in λ after integrating out x̃. The paper with the most explicit content is Berglund, Hübsch and Minic 2021: 'dark matter … comes from the degrees of freedom dual to the visible matter' (abstract) and 'the dual matter degrees of freedom naturally appear as dark matter candidates' (§5). Others: Minic 2020 §6.2, 'The proposal here is that the dual sector … should be interpreted as the dark matter sector'; EMT 2021 §4, 'which we propose'; BHM 2023 IJMPD §4.3 'good candidates' and §6.4 'a dual Standard Model describing dark matter'; BHM 2023 Symmetry, 'a candidate dark matter companion'; HM 2024 §3.3, 'We will argue'; HM April 2026, 'model (fuzzy) dark matter'; HM October 2026, 'a candidate for the invisible matter sector'. Weaker: Snowmass 2022, 'should be viewed as a dual SM'; BFHKLMM 2022, 'could be associated'; JKMT 2020, 'can be naturally related'; Minic 2023, 'natural candidates'. The nearest wording to 'conjugate matter' is BHM 2023 IJMPD §6.4: the dual particles are 'conjugate to the Standard Model particles in analogy with the canonical fact that momenta p are conjugate to coordinates q'. That is a conjugacy in field space. The school has other candidates too. FKLM 2021 proposes class II metaparticles as 'remnants', hedged as 'dark matter/energy'. The MDM papers of 2010–2018 have non-local quanta obeying infinite statistics, and in 2016–2017 suggest metaparticles as their model (precursors, Q11). The CDM–MOND 'duality' of 2010 and 2014 is a word collision.

Evidence: 2010.15610 abstract; §1; §5; 2003.00318 §5; §6.2; 2109.12763 §4; 2212.06086 §4.3; §6.4; 2307.16712 §3; §5; 2407.06207 §3.3; 2604.19418 §1.1; §5.1; 2610.19522 §3.2; 2202.05104 'Constraints on the nature of dark matter'; 2202.06890 'Observations'; 2011.08852 §6; 2305.12593 §2; 2104.00802 abstract; final paragraph; 1709.04388 §5; 1801.00160 §5; 1005.3537 abstract; 1308.3252 §I

**Verifier (r9-sweep-verify): confirmed-in-part.** Right in substance: in these 41 papers the identification is a proposal or a candidate from February 2020 on, never derived. Two imprecisions. Minic 2020's abstract states the clause as an implication of the formulation ('implies'); 'proposal' is the word of §6.2, and BHM 2021 §3 repeats that 'proposal' sentence verbatim. Snowmass 2022's 'dark matter should be viewed as a dual SM' is not weaker in wording than 'which we propose'. The record labels it partial because it does not say the dual is the momentum side.

Counter-evidence: 2003.00318, abstract: 'This generic formulation of string theory implies a radiatively stable positive cosmological constant' … 'as well as metaparticle quanta (the zero modes of the metastring) as the natural quanta of dark matter in this approach' (lit.py: FOUND, abstract) | 2010.15610, §3 after eq. (8): 'The proposal here is that the dual sector (as already indicated in the previous section) should be interpreted as the dark matter sector, which is correlated to the visible sector via the dark energy sector, as discussed in [18].' (FOUND)


## Q2: What dual is

#### r1-priority: Q2 (reader confidence high)

Both senses occur, and the dark-matter statements rest on the doubled one.

Quantum-mechanics sections: x̃ = p/ε with [x̂, x̃̂] = i and ħ = λε (Minic 2020 section 2; FLM 2017 eq. 29). This is the canonical conjugate in one phase space, the program's sense.

Metastring and metaparticle: x̃ is the T-dual coordinate. FLM 2017 eq. 5 has X̃ = X_L − X_R; BHM 2019 calls the 'canonically conjugate dual space, spanned by the difference of the left- and right-movers'. It is paired with winding (RR̃ = 2λ², Minic 2020 section 4.3) and has its own momentum. Minic 2020 eq. 27 gives [X^A, P_B] = iħδ and [X^A, X^B] = 2πiλ²ω^AB, 'a deformation of the doubled Heisenberg algebra involving the string length'. FKGLM 2021 gives {p,x} = δ, {p̃,x̃} = δ, {x̃,x} = λ², with p̃ read as 'internal' quantum numbers.

The commutator [x, x̃] appears as 2πiλ²δ (Minic 2020 eq. 28; BHM 2019 eq. 3), iλ² (Minic 2020 sections 4.3 and 6.3; Belgrade slides) and iπλ² (FKGLM 2021). λ is the string length (λ² = ħα′) or 'such as the Planck scale', with ε = ħ/λ (Minic 2020) or 1/λ (BHM 2019), and α′ = λ/ε. The x̃ integrated over in Minic 2020's dark-matter section (the 'hidden variable parameters') is this string-scale coordinate.

Evidence: 2003.00318 section 2, section 4.1 eq. (27)-(29), section 4.3; 1905.09463 footnote 4 and eq. (3); 2104.00802 eq. (1), (5); 1812.10821 section 2 eq. (9), footnote 4; recid:1590124 section 2 eq. (5), p. 4; talk:mphys10-2019-Minic slides 2, 3, 6

(1) The school names the dual half energy-momentum even in its metastring sections: Minic 2020 §4 defines 𝕏^A ≡ (X^μ/λ, P_μ/ε) on 'phase space'; FLM 2017 calls the second Lagrangian 'momentum space'; FKGLM 2021 puts (x, x̃) in a 'modular cell inside phase space'. (2) The scale of the commutator does not separate the two senses: the Belgrade talk writes quantum mechanics' own modular spacetime with [x, x̃] = iλ² (PDF p. 21), the same commutator as the metaparticle field. What separates the metaparticle's x̃ from the momentum p is that x̃ has its own conjugate p̃: {p,x} = δ, {p̃,x̃} = δ, {x̃,x} = πα′ (1812.10821 eq. 9), 'a particle in a doubled space-time'.

#### r2-dual-sm: Q2 What 'dual' is (reader confidence high)

These papers use x̃ in two senses. (a) In the modular-spacetime foundations x̃ is the momentum: x̂ ≡ q̂/λ, x̃̂ ≡ p̂/ε with λε = ℏ, so [x̂, x̃̂] = i (2109.12763 §2), and p → εx̃ in the phase-space count (2307.16712 §2.2). That is the canonical conjugate at ℏ, the program's sense. (b) In the metastring and metaparticle, which the dark-matter statements are built on, x̃ is the left-minus-right zero mode, 'the dual (winding) coordinates' (2212.06086 §6.1; 2010.15610 §2; 2111.14205; 2307.16712 eq. 2.45). There [x̂^a, x̃̂_b] = 2πiλ²δ^a_b and [x,x] = 0 = [x̃,x̃] at B = 0 (2010.15610 eq. 3; 2109.12763 eq. 14; 2212.06086 eqs. 6.1, 6.5), or iℓ_s² (2212.06086 §4.9; 2307.16712 eq. 3.1). x̃ has its own momentum: [X, P] = iℏ (2109.12763 §3.1), [X, Π] = iℏ = [X̃, Π̃] (2307.16712 eq. 3.2), [p, p̃] = 0 (2307.16712 §2.4), and the Poisson bracket {x̃, x} = πα′ (2212.06086 eq. 4.19). 2212.06086 §4.9 says outright that 'the metastring is endowed with two Heisenberg algebras', [x,p] = i and [x,x̃] = il_s². It calls the doubled space a phase space 'since the metastring action requires ... a background symplectic form' (§4.3). The scale of [x, x̃] varies: λ 'such as the fundamental Planck scale' (2010.15610 §2); λ ∼ (M_P^(10))⁻¹ ≲ 10⁻¹⁹ m, 'not to be confused with' the 4d Planck length (2212.06086 fn 38; my computation: ħc/λ = 1.97 TeV); a minimal length bound of O(10 TeV) (2109.12763 §3.1); ℓ_nc 'in principle contextual, and not necessarily the string length or the Planck length' (2307.16712 §4.1). The dual phase space is assumed Planck-sized (2212.06086 §4.9; 2307.16712 §3). The school also glosses x̃ as the 'long-distance (IR)' spacetime (2212.06086 §6.1) and the 'dual (momentum-like) spacetime' (2307.16712 §4.1). Dark fields Ã enter by minimally coupling the dual momentum p̃ (2010.15610 eq. 14; 2109.12763 §2.2). So in the dark-matter model x̃ is an independent T-dual coordinate, not the momentum of x. #11 places χ in the other polarization of one phase space (x, p), with ω rigid at ℏ (P2′, P4, P5).

Evidence: 2109.12763 §2; 2109.12763 §3, §3.1; 2010.15610 §2 eqs. (1)–(3), (13); 2212.06086 §4.3 eq. (4.19); 2212.06086 §4.9; 2212.06086 §6.1 and fn 38; 2307.16712 §2.2, §2.4, §3 eqs. (3.1)–(3.2), §4.1; 2111.14205 Introduction

Its excitations 'will be discussed below as metaparticles', and the dual fields φ̃ are introduced in this same section by minimal coupling to p̃. #### r3-gravitized: Q2 (reader confidence high)

Both, in the same papers, with nothing separating the two uses.
(a) The canonical conjugate, on one phase space. In the modular polarization of ordinary quantum mechanics, x̃ is the rescaled canonical momentum: 'x≡q/λ and x̃≡p/ε and λε=ħ', so '[x,x̃]=i' (2407.06207 §3.2–§3.3; 2604.19418 §5.1). Minic 2023 calls the momenta p 'dual coordinates conjugate to x', with [x̂, p̂] = i. 2610.19522 §3.2 derives X̃ as Y, the coordinate that 'naturally lives in momentum space', writes the symplectic form as (1/ħ) dx̃∧dx, and says ω is 'encoded in the canonical commutator between x and x̃ (or the canonical commutator between q and p in quantum theory)'. This is #11's single phase space with ω rigid at ħ.
(b) An independent coordinate of a doubled phase space. For the metaparticle and the metastring, on which every dark-matter statement rests: '[x,x̃]=iλ², ... [p,p̃]=0 ... [x,p]=iħ=[x̃,p̃]' (2407.06207 §3.3; 2604.19418 §5.1; 2610.19522 §3.2). 2604.19418 calls x 'canonically conjugate to dual spacetime x̃', and in §7 'conjugate, in the sense of canonical commutation relations'. Executed check: the metaparticle Poisson brackets (2407.06207 eq. 3.15) have rank 4 per dimension, so x̃ is not a function of (x, p); the modular change of variables has rank 2.
Scales: λ is 'contextual'.
- λ² = ħα′ (Minic 2023);
- ℓ_s (2407.06207 §3.5, §5.1; 2610.19522 eq. 3.10);
- l_P (2407.06207 §3.2 and §4.3; 2604.19418 §5.2). The same heuristic chain gives ℓ_s² in 2610.19522;
- l_eff (2407.06207 §3.5).
Normalizations vary: iλ², iπλ² (Minic 2023), 2πiλ² (2610.19522 eq. 3.3), 2πiℓ_s² (2407.06207 §5.1), πα′ (eq. 3.15). One more source of the non-commutativity is named: 'a constant and nonzero Kalb-Ramond B-field' (2407.06207 §5.1). Dark fields are backgrounds of p̃ ('p→p+φ and p̃→p̃+φ̃').

[p,\tilde{p}]=0 . (Also, [x,p]=i\hbar=[\tilde{x},\tilde{p}] .)'; 2407.06207 §3.2: '[q,\tilde{q}]\sim il_{P}^{2}'; §3.5 eq. 3.15: '\{\tilde{x}_{\mu},x^{\nu}\}=\pi\alpha^{\prime}'; §3.5: 'l_{s}\to l_{eff}'; §5.1: '2\pi i\ell_{s}^{2}'; 2604.19418 §5.1: 'So spacetime x is quantized, and it is canonically conjugate to dual spacetime \tilde{x}'; 2610.19522 §3.2: 'because Y naturally lives in momentum space' and '(where we are relabeling Y as the dual coordinate \tilde{X} )'; 2610.19522 §3.2: '(or the canonical commutator between q and p in quantum theory)'; 2305.12593 §2: 'while p_{\nu} are dual coordinates conjugate to x' and '[\hat{x}^{\mu},\hat{\tilde{x}}_{\nu}]=i\pi\lambda^{2}...'; work/dual-sector/scratch/r3-gravitized/scripts/bracket_rank.out (rank 2 vs rank 4)

**Verifier (r3-gravitized-verify): confirmed-in-part.** Right that x̃ is used both for a rescaled momentum on one phase space and for an independent coordinate of a doubled phase space. The verifier derived the doubling independently from the paper's Lagrangian (rank 4; brackets_v.out). Four corrections:
(1) The answer never gives the papers' own string-theoretic definition of x̃. They build it from left- and right-movers, x = x_L + x_R and x̃ = x̃_L − x̃_R (2407.06207 eq. 3.11; 2604.19418 eq. 5.10; 2610.19522 eq. 3.7). That is the closed string's T-dual coordinate, which the papers tie to double field theory. Its quasi-period is the string momentum, or 'the winding number in the usual treatment of T-duality on compact spaces' (2610.19522 §3.2). The brief asks for exactly this when x̃ is a winding-type coordinate.
(2) 2610.19522's derivation belongs to use (b), not (a). Its X̃ = Y 'lives in momentum space' because ∂_σY = P, the momentum density. But [X, X̃] = 2πiλ² (λ² ~ α′), and X̃ has its own conjugate Π̃ at ħ (eq. 3.11). So it is not the canonical conjugate of x on one phase space at ħ.
(3) 'Nothing separating the two uses' understates what the authors do: they explicitly identify the two uses. 2407.06207 §3.2 says the x̃ ≡ p/ε description 'will appear in what follows in the metastring formulation'. The modular field φ_λ(x, x̃) is obtained by second-quantizing the Zak-transformed wave function (2407.06207 §3.3; 2604.19418 §5.1). A transition sentence marks the step from 'covariant quantum phase space' to 'modular spacetime'. What is missing is any statement that x̃ changes status.
(4) The answer's list of scales omits one: 2407.06207 §5.1 assumes the dual phase space is of Planck size, with l̃ = ℓ_s²/√(l_Λ l_P).
A note for the reader's executed check: the single phase space alone also gives [X, X̃] = iλ² in dimensionful form (X = q, X̃ = λ²p/ħ; brackets_v.out). It is [x, p] = iħ = [x̃, p̃] with [p, p̃] = 0 that forces the doubling, not the [x, x̃] commutator.

#### r4-pheno: Q2 (reader confidence high)

These papers use x̃ in two senses. (a) In the dark-energy papers (2011.08852 §2; 2503.20854 §1 and App. B), x̃ is the string's left-minus-right coordinate in a doubled target space X^A = (x/λ, x̃/λ). The scale is λ = √α′, 'the string length scale' ('such as the Planck scale'). The commutators are [x̂^a, x̃̂_b] = 2πiλ²δ^a_b and [x, x] = [x̃, x̃] = 0 (2011.08852 eq. 8; 2503.20854 eq. 101); 2503.20854 §1–§2 write iλ², a factor 2π apart. They call the doubled space phase space ('works in phase space', 'doubled “phase-space” operators') and x̃-space 'canonically conjugate', because it does not commute with x. The metaparticle has separate momenta p and dual momenta p̃ with p p̃ = μ (2503.20854 §3.4): the space is doubled, and its scale is λ², not ℏ. No [x, p] or [x̃, p̃] is given in the nine papers. (b) In the vacuum-energy papers (2212.00901 eqs. 10–11; 2303.17495 eq. 5), (x, x̃) are the coordinates of a modular cell of ordinary phase space: q = λx, p = εx̃, with λε = 2πℏ. There x̃ is momentum at ℏ, the program's sense, but these papers have no dark sector. The Snowmass paper and Addazi et al. give no definition beyond 'dual spacetime' and 'T-duality covariant and intrinsically non-commutative'. This agrees with the record: the dark-sector model doubles phase space at the string scale where the program polarizes one phase space (#11 P2′, P4).

Evidence: 2011.08852, §2 after eq. (1) and eq. (8); 2503.20854, §1; §3.4 before eq. (45); App. B eq. (101); 2212.00901, eqs. (10)–(11); 2303.17495, eq. (5); 2111.05659, §2.2.2.2

**Verifier (r4-pheno-verify): confirmed-in-part.** The two senses of x̃ are correctly separated and the commutators and scales are right. Two additions. First, 2503.20854 §1 calls the x–x̃ relation 'the Heisenberg algebra [x,x̃]=iλ²'. This is the sense of the school's 'canonically conjugate dual x̃-space': a Heisenberg pair at the scale λ², not ℏ. Second, 'No [x, p] or [x̃, p̃] is given in the nine papers' holds for the school's passages, not for the nine: Addazi et al.'s generalized-uncertainty-principle section writes a deformed '[x,p]=iℏ(1+βp²+…)' for those models (not the metastring).

Counter-evidence: 2503.20854 §1: 'as well as the statistics of infinite matrices x and \tilde{x} (the so called infinite, or quantum distinguishable, statistics) that satisfy the Heisenberg algebra [x,\tilde{x}]=i\lambda^{2} ,' (FOUND) | 2111.05659 (GUP models): 'deformed Heisenberg commutators between space coordinates and spatial momenta, [x,p]=i\hbar(1+\beta p^{2}+\mathcal{O}(p^{4}))' (FOUND)

#### r5-mdm: Q2 (reader confidence high)

Not addressed in the MDM papers. They define no dual coordinate x̃ and give no commutators [x, x̃], [x, p] or [x̃, p̃]. 'Dual' means the CDM–MOND duality (1005.3537, 1105.2916, 1308.3252), a holographic dual (dS/CFT, 1005.3537), or T-duality in passing (1709.04388 §5). The 'Born-Infeld theory' of 1201.2365 is Born and Infeld's nonlinear electrodynamics, used as a gravitational dielectric analogy, not Born reciprocity. Momentum appears twice. 1801.00160 speaks of 'two entangled particles in momentum space' without defining it. 1709.04388 §5 has a heuristic δp ∼ αδx with the ordinary Heisenberg relation δpδx ∼ ħ, so momentum there is ordinary momentum at ħ. MDM's scales are ħ (Unruh temperature), G and a0 = c²√(Λ/3) = cH0; there is no string scale λ. λ (UV) and L (IR) enter only in the string-side papers, in Berglund–Hübsch–Minić 2021's mixing term ∝ λ⁸/L¹⁰. Nothing in MDM corresponds to the program's polarization of one phase space with ω rigid (#11 P2′, P5).

Evidence: 1005.3537 abstract and dS/CFT section; 1201.2365 abstract, §3 Eq. (6), ref. [26]; 1709.04388 §5 (T-duality; δp ∼ αδx); 1801.00160 §5; 2010.15610 §3 Eq. (19)

**Verifier (r5-mdm-verify): confirmed.** No correction. One addition: 1005.3537 also uses 'dual' for the holographic degrees of freedom of dS/CFT. The quanta's nature is said to be constrained by non-gravitational boundary degrees of freedom 'dual to the bulk modified theory of gravity'. None of the eleven texts gives a commutator [x, x̃] or a dual coordinate (my counts of 'commut': only infinite-statistics oscillator relations, a kernel that 'commutes with the isometries', and 'non-commutative' in 1709.04388 §5 and 2005.08927).

Counter-evidence: 1005.3537 Phenomenological Implications (1): 'The nature of MONDian dark matter quanta is constrained by the holographic non-gravitational degrees of freedom dual to the bulk modified theory of gravity.' (FOUND)

#### r6-foundations: Q2 (reader confidence high)

It depends on the paper. (a) Ordinary quantum mechanics, 1606.01829 §1.1: x ≡ q/λ and x̃ ≡ p/ε 'measuring position and momentum in natural units', with ε = 2πℏc/λ and [x̂, x̃̂] = i/2π, which is [q, p] = iℏ (checked, normalizations.py). Here x̃ is the canonical momentum of the same particle at ℏ, as in #11's one phase space (x, p). (b) The string papers: the target 'phase space' is the doubled space 𝕏 = (X/λ, Y/ε) (1502.08005 eq. 13; (X/√α′, Y√α′) in 1307.7080). Y is 'a momentum space coordinate' defined by ∂σY = P, the momentum density; its quasi-period around the string is the momentum (1307.7080); it has the dimension of momentum and is conjugate to ∂σX, not to X (1502.08005 eqs. 6–7). It is the T-dual field: 1706.03305 defines X̃ by ∂σX̃ = ∂τX, and Y = X̃/α′ up to a function of τ (dual_field.py). The zero modes x = x_R + x_L, x̃ = x_R − x_L, p, p̃ are independent, with [x̂, p̂] = iℏ = [x̃̂, p̃̂], [p̂, p̃̂] = 0 and [x̂, x̃̂] = 2πiλ², λ = √(ℏα′/2), i.e. iπℏα′ (1706.03305 eq. 73; 1707.00312 eqs. 5–6). The canonical pairs are (p, x) and (p̃, x̃ − πα′p) (1706.03305 eq. 69): x̃ = q̃ + πα′p, with q̃ conjugate to the winding p̃ and commuting with x. So x̃ is a coordinate of a doubled space, conjugate (through q̃) to winding, which contains πα′ times the ordinary momentum and therefore does not commute with x. In x̃'s length units the commutator is 'set by the string length scale' (1706.03305 abstract) and independent of the radius (§3); in Y's momentum units it is [x, Y₀] = iπℏ (units.py), and 1502.08005 fn. 32 gives the modular cell area ℏ. FLM read (x, x̃) as a Heisenberg pair: 'the double space P should be understood as a phase space' (1706.03305 §4.1); in 1502.08005 §7.2 they set q ≡ x, p = hỹ, [p, q] = iℏ. (c) The metaparticle, 1812.10821 §2: phase space {x, p, x̃, p̃} with {p, x} = 1, {p̃, x̃} = 1, {x̃, x} = πα′ (Darboux as α′ → 0); in the convention [x, p] = iℏ this is [x, x̃] = iπℏα′, the same as 1706.03305 (normalizations.py). Scales: ℏ = λε, α′ = λ/ε, λ² ≡ α′ℏ, G_N = ρα′ (1502.08005 eq. 4); ℏ = λε, G_N ∝ λ/ε in D = 4 (1405.3949 §1.3); ε = 2πℏc/λ (1606.01829 §1.1); λ = √(ℏα′/2) (1706.03305 eq. 5; 1707.00312 §2). These normalizations differ by factors 2 and 2π. Compared with the program: χ lives in the p-polarization of one phase space (x, p) with ω rigid (#11 P2′, P4, P5). In the string and metaparticle papers the dual polarization is that of x̃, a partner of x in a doubled space, while the ordinary p remains a separate variable; only 1606.01829 has the program's setting, and it is not about strings or dark matter.

(4), (6), (7), (13); §7.1 fn. 32; §7.2; 1706.03305 §2 eqs. (5), (7), (10), (11); end of §3; §4 eqs. (69), (73); §4.1; 1707.00312 §2 eqs. (5), (6); 1812.10821 §2 eqs. Two omissions tilt the conclusion. (a) 1706.03305 sec. 4.2, which the reader only searched, says 'xt/{2 pi lambda^2} plays the role of the variable conjugate to x', the topological coupling making (x, xt) 'into a phase space'. My computation: hbar xt/(2 pi lambda^2) = xt/(pi alpha') = p + qt/(pi alpha'), with [x, qt] = 0, so the conjugate role is carried by p. (b) FLM read the string foundations' dual coordinate as a momentum: 'Y can be thought of as a momentum coordinate', 'the Polyakov action for the momentum variable', X -> Y 'the string Fourier transformation' (1502.08005 sec. 2). They call the (X,Y) units, with cell area hbar, 'the natural metastring parameterization', and the length-and-radius reading of (x, xt) 'deeply misleading' (sec. 7.1). The answer's 'a coordinate of a doubled space, conjugate (through qt) to winding' is right as structure. FLM's own reading is closer to the record's 'momentum side' than the answer allows. Also, 1812.10821 keeps x, xt non-Darboux by choice ('x,xt are preferred coordinates'), so whether xt counts as x's conjugate depends on the coordinates chosen. Supporting the answer: 1502.08005 sec. 7.4 names 'P (resp. Pt) the momenta dual to x (resp. xt)', and 1707.00312 sec. 2 calls P_A 'the conjugate operators' of X^A = (x, xt).

#### r7-vacuum: Q2 (reader confidence medium)

Incidental to Q9, but it limits how far 2104.00802's vacuum statement anticipates P5. These papers use the dual coordinate in two ways.
- In the modular-variable foundations x̃ is momentum rescaled: 'x ≡ q/λ, x̃ ≡ p/ε, so [x,x̃]=i, λε=ℏ' (2407.06207 §3.3; same in 2604.19418 §5.1). 1307.7080 calls Y 'coordinates in momentum space'.
- In the metaparticle and metastring, x̃ is 'conjugate to x' with [x̂, x̂̃] = iπλ² and 'the string scale λ²=ℏα′', and the theory has separate momenta p and p̃ (2104.00802, eqs. 1 and 5). 2604.19418 calls x̃ 'a dual/conjugate partner' with [x,x̃]=iλ².
FKLM's vacuum-sector breaking is stated in the second, doubled setting.

Evidence: 2407.06207 §3.3; 2604.19418 §1.1; 2104.00802 after eq. (1); 1307.7080 (record's quotation; also 1812.10821 §2)

**Verifier (r7-vacuum-verify): confirmed.** 

#### r8-outside: Q2 (reader confidence high)

Outside the school, 'dual' has these meanings:

- **Ran.** The ordinary Fourier conjugate (k, E) of (x, t), at ℏ: the kernel is e^{i(kx−Et/ℏ)}, normalized by (2πℏ)^−2. 'At the kinematic level, these manifolds are related by a norm-preserving Fourier isomorphism', but 'each manifold carries its own intrinsic unitary dynamics', with iℏ∂_tψ = Hψ and iℏ∂_Eϕ = H̃ϕ. The result is a direct sum H_xt ⊕ H_kE, not a polarization, and has no new scale. This is the program's sense of 'conjugate'.
- **Chouha, restating the metaparticle, has both readings the record describes.** On one reading x̃ is an independent coordinate: '[x^μ, x̃_ν] = 2πiλ²δ^μ_ν', where 'x and x̃ denote the spacetime and dual-spacetime coordinates, with conjugate momenta p and p̃', λ is 'analogous to the string length', and λ → 0 recovers spacetime. On the other, in the modular foundations, x̃ is the momentum: 'We identify [q]:=[x] and [p]:=[x̃]', and [x̃] does not survive ℏ → 0. His 2πiλ² differs from Hübsch and Minic 2024's iλ² by a factor 2π, a convention.
- **Bolognesi.** 'two distinct manifolds, one for the coordinates' x and one for the momenta p, with fields related by a generalized Fourier transform of scale M; a dual metric g̃(p); and G̃_N = M⁴G_N.
- **Maldonado and Mendez.** No dual coordinate: a deformed bracket {π_a, π_b} = θ between the momenta conjugate to two scale factors.
- **Häuser and Dröscher.** A dual spacetime 'with imaginary time coordinate'; 'The three spatial coordinates are shared by the two spacetimes.'
- **Nishimura.** An internal 'isospin spacetime', which 'The duality operator T' interchanges with spacetime.
- **Hazboun and Wheeler.** Biconformal space with a symplectic form and 'orthogonal Lagrangian submanifolds'.
- **Vasconcellos and Hadjimichef.** The electric–magnetic duality rotation.

Evidence: doi:10.55277/researchhub.unu7fof1.2 introduction and §I; 2602.21407 §3.1 eq. (3.1); Appendix B; 2608.26413 §II.1; 1506.02187 §2; 2103.11235 §II; recid:2704811 §2; doi:10.1515/zna-2018-0559 abstract; 2212.13131 main text; 1305.6972 abstract; 1404.0409 §II

The framework it rests on gives the momentum–energy sector the dynamics iℏ∂_Eϕ = (−α∂_k² + V0)ϕ, 'with real constants α>0 setting the stiffness of E-evolution and V0 a reference level' (2511.20907 §I). Additions: Chouha's abstract calls the dual sector 'winding-like', which supports the independent-coordinate reading. Bolognesi's duality 'requires a new fundamental energy scale that we call M' (§1). He puts M of order the Planck scale in §1 and takes M ≃ M_GUT ≃ 10^16 GeV in §6.

#### r9-sweep: Q2 (reader confidence high)

The school's own texts give three senses. (i) Foundations, from the modular (Zak) representation of ordinary QM: x̃ is momentum rescaled at ℏ. Minic 2020 §3 and EMT 2021 §2.2 write x ≡ q/a, x̃ ≡ p/b with ab = 2πℏ; FLM 2016 §1.1 writes x̃ ≡ p/ε with λε = 2πℏ and [x̂, x̂̃] = (i/2π)δ; HM 2024 §3.3 writes x̃ ≡ p/ε with [x, x̃] = i and λε = ℏ; Minic 2023 §2 calls the momenta p 'dual coordinates conjugate to x'. This is the canonical conjugate in one phase space, as in #11. (ii) The metastring and metaparticle, where all the dark-matter statements are made: x̃ is an independent doubled coordinate, x̃ = x_L − x_R, the 'canonically conjugate dual space' of BHM 2019 footnote 4. The commutator is 2πiλ² (BHM 2019, 2021), iπλ² with λ² = ℏα′ (FKLM 2021, Minic 2023), iλ² (HM 2024, 2026), or il_P² and iℓ_s² (BHM 2023 Symmetry). Momenta are separate: [x, p] = iℏ = [x̃, p̃], [p, p̃] = 0 (HM 2024 §3.3; BHM 2023 eqs. 3.1–3.2), and (x, x̃, p, p̃) are independent combinations of left and right movers (FLM 2017). λ is the string length ('such as the Planck scale'; 'a new phenomenological scale'). (iii) Field space, surprising: 'the fields and their duals are “canonically conjugate” like q and p … the two form an effective Heisenberg algebra' (BHM 2023 IJMPD §6.4), induced from [x, x̃]; visible φ and dark φ̃ 'do not commute' (HM 2024, 2026). FLM 2014's Y is the string coordinate whose quasi-period is p, 'coordinates in momentum space'. 1801.00160 describes metaparticles as 'two entangled particles in momentum space'.

Evidence: 2003.00318 §3; 2109.12763 §2.2; 1606.01829 §1.1; 2407.06207 §3.3; 2305.12593 §2; 1905.09463 footnote 4, eq. (3); 2010.15610 §2 eq. (3); 2104.00802 eqs. (1), (5); 2307.16712 §2.4; eqs. (3.1)–(3.2); 1706.03305 §2; 1707.00312 §2, eq. (40); 2212.06086 §6.4; 1307.7080 'Quasi-Periodicity…' paragraph; 1801.00160 §5

**Verifier (r9-sweep-verify): confirmed.** No correction. Three passages strengthen sense (ii). Even where the school defines x̃ ≡ p/b, it takes the dual (later dark) fields from the dual momentum p̃ of the doubled phase space. HM 2024 takes its 'dark matter' fields from the shift p̃ → p̃ + φ̃. BHM 2023 (Symmetry) introduces the dual coordinate q̃ through p̃ and obtains [q, q̃] ~ i l_P² from a gravitational (Schwarzschild-radius) argument.

## Q3: Field content

#### r1-priority: Q3 (reader confidence high)

Yes, a dual partner for every Standard Model field, including a dual photon. Minic 2020 speaks of 'bi-local extensions of all Standard Model fields', 'the Ã are their duals', a dual particle at energy μ/E for each particle, a 'dual field Ã, which is entangled' with A, and the case 'say, for a photon and its dual'. The Belgrade slides list 'dual photon, dual neutrino, dual quark' and a 'dual Standard Model (hidden sector?)'.

Dual charges are not discussed. The metaparticle couples to A through ẋ and to Ã through its x̃-velocity (eqs. 45-46). Chang et al. 2016 differ: there, only chargeless non-local partners are dark, and charged ones would be visible and have decayed early.

Evidence: 2003.00318 section 5 eqs. (44)-(49), section 6.2; talk:mphys10-2019-Minic slide 5; 1812.10821 section 3.2; 1605.04361 section 5

**Verifier (r1-priority-verify): confirmed-in-part.** 'Yes' is stronger than the sources. The dual copy is proposed ('could be provided', 'would have natural cousins'). Slide 5 marks it 'hidden sector?'. §5 says the gauging that produces Ã depends on taking (x, x̃) as configuration variables. §3 says the doubled formulation keeps the number of degrees of freedom of the 'purely electric' one. The dual photon and the absence of dual charges are as stated.

Counter-evidence: 2003.00318 §5: 'There is a possible ambiguity in this gauging which depends on which configuration variables one decides to work with.' (FOUND) | 2003.00318 §3: 'keeps the total number of degrees of freedom the same as in the purely electric formulation.' (FOUND) | talk:mphys10-2019-Minic slide 5 (PDF p. 24): '(HIDDEN SECTOR?)'

#### r2-dual-sm: Q3 Field content (reader confidence high)

Yes: one dual per field. 'For every φ(x,x̃) there exists a dual φ̃(x,x̃)' (2010.15610 §3; 2212.06086 §6.3). 'The entire SM would have a dual SM' (2109.12763 §4), and 'every Standard Model field φ(x) is doubled' (2307.16712 §2.4). Named partners: a dual photon 'distinct from the dark photon of effective field theory', a dual Z distinct from a Z′, dual QCD (2010.15610 §5; 2212.06086 §6.5); a dual neutrino and dual Higgs, the latter a possible Starobinsky-like inflaton (2212.06086 §6.4–§6.5); a dual axion (§6.4); a dual gravity sector R̃(x̃) that gives dark energy (§4.3). Dual gauge fields 'have the usual Abelian gauge symmetries' (2010.15610 §4), so the dual photon is a separate gauge field (my reading; compare #11 §11.4's separate-photon form). Dual charges are not addressed. In the heterotic setting each E8 gauge field has a 'dark' dual in the same algebra (2111.14205; 2212.06086 §6.6), and the dark sector's dual SM comes 'from the other E8' (2307.16712 §4.3). There is also a kinematic dual: 'for each particle at energy E there exists a dual particle at energy μ/E' (2109.12763 §4), and metaparticles are 'correlated pairs of particles and their duals' (2307.16712 §3). The one-dual-per-field structure parallels #11 §11.4's one magnetic analog per charged particle, without magnetic charges (my comparison). #11 §12 itself names three candidates, not a dual SM.

Evidence: 2010.15610 §3 eq. (7), §4 eq. (14), §5; 2109.12763 §4; 2212.06086 §4.3, §6.3–§6.6; 2307.16712 §2.4, §3, §4.3; 2111.14205 item 3(b)

**Verifier (r2-dual-sm-verify): confirmed-in-part.** The answer reports the papers accurately, with two qualifications. First, the dual-field prediction rests on a choice that 2109.12763 §4 itself calls ambiguous: 'There is a possible ambiguity in this gauging which depends on which configuration variables one decides to work with'. Second, the heterotic placements disagree, and the answer lists both without noting it. 2111.14205 and 2212.06086 §6.6 item 3(b) give each E8 factor visible fields on x and '“dark”' duals on x̃ 'valued in the same E8 algebra', while 2307.16712 §4.3 puts the 'dual Standard Model of the dark sector' in 'the other E8'. 2212.06086 §6.4 adds that a dark field and its visible field 'both stem from the same bi-local function A(x,x̃)'. 'Dual charges are not addressed' holds: none of the six has a 'dual charge', and 'charge' occurs in 2212.06086 only in the swampland and gauge-gravity passages.

Counter-evidence: 2109.12763 §4: 'There is a possible ambiguity in this gauging which depends on which configuration variables one decides to work with.' (FOUND) | 2212.06086 §6.6 item 3(b): 'These originally bi-local fields then give rise to both an E_{8} -gauge field and its (“dark”) dual field valued in the same E_{8} algebra.' (FOUND) | 2307.16712 §4.3: 'This is different from the usual GUT logic, but it points to a possible robustness of the Standard Model group (and its dual Standard Model of the dark sector, coming from the other E_{8} in heterotic string theory).' (FOUND) | 2212.06086 §6.4: 'In our case, the “specialness” of \tilde{A}({\mathord{\tilde{x}}}) as related to A(x) is that they both stem from the same bi-local function A(x,{\mathord{\tilde{x}}}) .' (FOUND)

#### r3-gravitized: Q3 (reader confidence high)

A dual copy of the whole Standard Model, one partner per field.
- 'every Standard Model field φ(x) is doubled as φ(x,x̃) and φ̃(x,x̃)' (2407.06207 §3.5); 'the Ã are their duals' (§3.4);
- 'its dual Standard Model of the dark sector, coming from the other E8 in heterotic string theory' (§5.4);
- 'every Standard Model particle has a dual fuzzy counterpart' (2604.19418 §5.1).
Not every dual is dark matter. 'Some dual fields, like the dual Higgs boson, might be relevant for modeling other phenomena, such as the Starobinsky-like inflation' (2604.19418 §5.1). The conclusion also floats the axion's dual partner as 'more suited for a fuzzy dark matter excitation' (§7, speculative).
The dual SM also contributes CP-violating phases to the visible mixing matrices (2407.06207 §5.4).
These papers name no dual photon. Charges: one footnote associates the dual momentum p̃ with conserved (electric) charges and the dual spacetime x̃ with 'conserved dual (magnetic) charges', in the context of gravitational corner symmetries (2407.06207 §3.5, fn. 11). No dual charges of matter are assigned.

#### r5-mdm: Q3 (reader confidence high)

MDM has no dual copy of the Standard Model, no dual photon and no dual charges. What the papers say about the quanta: massless at galactic scales and massive (CDM-like) at cluster scales, and 'vector and scalar degrees of freedom may be preferred' (1005.3537, speculative); infinite statistics (Cuntz algebra), 'fundamentally non-local', with a toy 'neutral scalar field obeying infinite statistics' (1201.2365); 'not of a traditional particle type (i.e. it is not described by a local effective quantum field theory)' (1308.3252); 'most probably of a non-local, non-particle kind' (1709.04388). The 2012 top-down sketch is an infinite matrix 𝕄 with a phenomenological 'Yukawa' coupling g to a Standard Model operator. In 2024 (2405.08744) the dark matter is an ultralight boson of ∼10⁻²² eV, 'perhaps axion-like'. A partner for every Standard Model field appears only in the string-side papers: Minic 2020 (metaparticle realizations of SM particles, A and Ã, a photon and its dual), and Edmonds–Minic–Takeuchi 2021 ('the entire SM would have a dual SM'). MDM has no analog of #11 P4's p-polarized representations or of §11.4's magnetic mirror.

#### r6-foundations: Q3 (reader confidence high)

No dual Standard Model, no dual photon and no dual charges appear in these papers. What they have: the metaparticle's dual particle/dual anti-particle pair, two further poles of one propagator (1812.10821 §3.2, §6); string states carrying both momentum and winding, which on the worldsheet act as electric and magnetic charges ('2d electromagnetism with electric and magnetic charges (dyons)', 1307.7080; 'dyonic states', 1502.08005 §1); and fields Φ(x, x̃) on modular spacetime with two masses (m, m̃), the usual spacetime description holding when m ≫ m̃ (1502.08005 §7.3). Fields 'living in the dual space' (1707.00312 §3) are the same states in another basis: 'one should simply choose one or the other ... the difference being simply a change of basis'. The metaparticle's two chiral components are 'entangled' in a way that 'prevents an interpretation as two particle species' (1812.10821 §3.1). Nothing assigns a matter species to the dual polarization, as #11 P4 does (x-polarized Standard Model, p-polarized χ) or #11 §11.4's magnetic mirror would.

#### r8-outside: Q3 (reader confidence high)

No outside paper proposes a dual copy of the whole Standard Model as dark matter.

- **Addazi et al.** (school coauthors): 'dual matter and dual gravity sectors'.
- **Bolognesi.** Matter ⟺ matter~ and gauge/gravity ⟷ gauge~/gravity~. The minimal model has one scalar, one universal U(1) 'graviphoton' and gravity, each with a counterpart on momentum space.
- **Vasconcellos and Hadjimichef.** A dual photon C_μ, with dark-fermion currents coupled only to it.

- **Nishimura.** Negative-norm bosons and fermions, plus a 'gravino' and a 'gravion' that 'may also be the dark matter'.
- **Ran.** No particle species: his dark matter is a projected state.

No outside paper has dual charges or a dual Standard Model.

Nishimura's negative-norm sector would, conditionally, hold 'almost all the antiparticles'.

## Q4: Coupling

#### r1-priority: Q4 (reader confidence medium)

Three channels in Minic 2020:
- Gravity: after x̃ integration both sectors sit in one action on √g(x) (eqs. 50, 57).
- An α′-order Berry-phase-like 'entanglement' term, α′A_μ dÃ^μ/dt, 'uniquely different from the usual effective field theory interaction terms'. No strength is given.
- A correlation 'via the dark energy sector, as discussed in [12]': modified dark matter's empirical tie between dark and baryonic mass profiles through a0 ~ cH.

'Correlated' thus means the modified-dark-matter mass-profile relation, and 'entangled' means the Berry-phase term. My inference from eqs. (49)-(50): at zeroth order in λ the visible and dual Lagrangians enter as a sum, with no cross term, and share only the metric. Limits: the usual particle limit is μ → 0, p̃ → 0; local effective field theory appears only in 'a singular limit' that neglects the non-commutativity; in FKGLM 2021 the dual degrees of freedom decouple as the scale factor grows. Compare #11 P4 (gravity only, ε ≲ 1e-13).

Evidence: 2003.00318 section 5 eqs. (45)-(50), section 6.2 eq. (56)-(57), section 6.3, section 4.3; talk:mphys10-2019-Minic slide 5; 2104.00802 eq. (2), introduction

**Verifier (r1-priority-verify): confirmed-in-part.** Eq. (49) is written on a gravitational background held 'non-dynamical' and ends in '+ ...'. 'No cross term, the sectors share only the metric' is therefore an inference about the displayed zeroth-order terms on a fixed background. The Belgrade slide 6 says dark matter is 'sensitive to Λ and L_m(x)', i.e. the dark Lagrangian depends on the visible one, so 'correlated' covers more than the mass-profile relation. No mechanism or strength is given. The three channels and the limits are as stated.

#### r2-dual-sm: Q4 Coupling (reader confidence high)

The papers state four channels besides gravity. (1) Through dark energy: the dual sector 'is correlated to the visible sector via the dark energy sector', citing MDM (2010.15610 §3; 2109.12763 §5; 2212.06086 §6.3). (2) Bilinear mixing terms. These come from the symplectic term λ² p ṗ̃ of the metaparticle action once p → p + A and p̃ → p̃ + Ã. In the doubled space: ∫[F² − aλ²⟦A,Ã⟧ + F̃² + FF̃ + …] (2010.15610 eq. 15; 2212.06086 eq. 6.27, 'clearly not present in the usual effective field theory approach'). In x-space after integrating x̃, 2010.15610 has vector −(λ⁸/L¹⁰) f⟦A,Ã⟧, scalar λ⁸/L¹⁰ and fermion λ⁹/L¹⁰ ψ̄ψ̃ (eqs. 19–21). 2212.06086 rewrites these in 4d as λ₄²/L⁴ times dimensionless 'Berry phase-like background fluxes', and λ₄³/L⁴ for fermions (eqs. 6.32–6.35). The fermion term is 'quite explicitly a mass-mixing term': each fermion and its dual share a mass matrix [[m, λ⁹/L¹⁰],[λ⁹/L¹⁰, m̃]], with seesaw relations and possibly neutrino masses (2010.15610 §5; 2212.06086 eq. 6.36). 2212.06086 §6.6 adds gauge–gravity terms. The dual photon's correlation is 'finite even in the limit of zero momenta' and dominates ordinary dark-photon mixing (2010.15610 §5). (3) Entanglement: the metaparticle 'looks like two particles that are entangled through a Berry-phase-like p ṗ̃ factor' (2109.12763 §4). This is what 'correlated (entangled)' means. (4) Field-space non-commutativity: the dual fields 'do not commute with Standard Model fields, and the two form an effective Heisenberg algebra' (2212.06086 §6.4; 2307.16712 §5). Strength is not fixed. 2010.15610 §6 calls λ an effective scale 'to be empirically determined', and 2212.06086 fn 43 leaves λ₄ and L 'unspecified'. 2010.15610 §6 lets L lie 'within one or two orders of magnitude' of λ, 'resulting in a phenomenologically realistic scale'. 2212.06086 §6.5 says the mass gap 'may well be phenomenologically relevant', the details being 'model-dependent'. My computation (/home/paul/git/reinholdtsen/conjugate-matter/work/dual-sector/scratch/r2-dual-sm/scripts/mixing_scale.py, output mixing_scale.out in the same folder): the 2010.15610 coefficients are suppressed relative to the fundamental scale by (λ/L)¹⁰ = 1.0e-10 at L/λ = 10 and 1.0e-20 at L/λ = 100. With λ the Planck length and L the Hubble length the factor is 10^-609.3, a reading 2010.15610 §4 rejects. With λ = 1e-19 m, my illustrative choice, the fermion mass mixing would be 197 eV at L/λ = 10 and 2e-8 eV at L/λ = 100. Limits: the mixing 'vanishes as either λ → 0 or L → ∞' and is 'invisible in effective field theory', but 'with a particular double scaling, this term can be finite' (2010.15610 §4; 2212.06086 §6.4). Ordinary particles are the limit μ → 0, p̃ → 0 (2109.12763 §4). The dual degrees of freedom are 'completely decoupled' in the extensification where holography emerges (2010.15610 §7; 2212.06086 §6.1). 2212.06086 treats the sectors as independent 'to first order in the fundamental length' (§6.2–§6.3), while 2010.15610 §1 places the dark-matter interpretation itself at first order in λ. #11 P4 excludes every channel above: no gauge force crosses polarizations, ε ≲ 10⁻¹³, and the sectors share only gravity (my comparison).

#### r3-gravitized: Q4 (reader confidence high)

The coupling comes in three layers, stated differently across the papers.
(i) Kinematic correlation. The visible φ and dark φ̃ do not commute ('dark matter is fuzzy from the point of view of classical spacetime', 2407.06207 §3.3; 2604.19418 §5.1), and 'visible and dark matter degrees of freedom are correlated (via the minimal length λ)'. Metaparticles are 'correlated objects of visible particles and their duals' (Minic 2023), 'rigid length-ℓ_s strings that correlate each Standard Model particle with its dual' (2407.06207 §5.1).
(ii) Leading-order effective action. S_eff = −∫√(g g̃)[R + R̃ + L_m(A) + L̃_dm(Ã)] writes no cross term (2407.06207 eq. 3.8; 2604.19418 eq. 5.7). My reading: to this order the sectors share only the gravitational measure.
(iii) A mixing with a scale, in 2407.06207 §5.3 only. 'Dark and visible matter mix via a Berry phase-like correlation term[103]. The mass-scale of this correlation term defines the decoupling energy, below which the dark and visible matter interact as required, only gravitationally'. The scale is identified with M_SM ≈ 4.5×10^14 GeV; executed check from the paper's definition and Table 1 inputs: 4.48×10^14 GeV. The statement is absent from 2604.19418, 2610.19522 and 2307.16712 v2. Its cited source, LHEP 2021 (arXiv:2010.15610), has the Berry-phase-like [[A, Ã]] term but no decoupling energy (greps).
'Entangled/correlated' dynamics (2407.06207 §6, from LHEP 2021) is not defined further in these papers.
Limits in which the dual sector vanishes:
- b → ∞ shrinks the dual dimension, and metaparticles 'are reduced to canonical particles' (Minic 2023);
- the canonical propagator is the 'highly singular p̃ → 0 (and μ → 0) limit' (2407.06207 §3.4);
- 'In the limit in which the physics of dual spacetime is neglected, one recovers local quantum field theory' (2604.19418 §5.1).
Against #11: below ~10^14 GeV the 2024 restatement has gravity-only interaction, as under #11's strict P4. The differences left are the non-commutativity at O(λ), the dual SM's contribution to visible CP phases, and the claimed galaxy-scale correlation (Q5).

Evidence: 2407.06207 §5.3 'Neutrinos': 'The mass-scale of this correlation term defines the decoupling energy, below which the dark and visible matter interact as required, only gravitationally.'; 2407.06207 §5.3: 'M_{SM}\simeq 4.5{\times}10^{14}\,\text{GeV} is thus naturally identifiable with this dark-matter/visible-matter decoupling scale.'; 2407.06207 §5 introduction, item (3); work/dual-sector/scratch/r3-gravitized/scripts/msm_check.out: case (b) M_SM = 4.484e+14 GeV; 2407.06207 §3.3: 'the visible \phi and invisible (dark matter) {\tilde{\phi}} do not commute'; 2407.06207 §3.4, eq. 3.8 and 'In general, visible and dark matter degrees of freedom are correlated (via the minimal length \lambda )'; 2407.06207 §5.1: 'rigid length- \ell_{s} strings that correlate each Standard Model particle with its dual'; 2305.12593 §2: 'in the limit of large expansion b\to\infty , the dual spacetime dimension shrinks to zero'; 2604.19418 §5.1: 'In the limit in which the physics of dual spacetime is neglected, one recovers local quantum field theory.'; greps: 'decoupl|gravitationally' absent in this sense from 2604.19418, 2610.19522, 2307.16712 v2 and 2010.15610

(1) The layers are one structure in the school's own derivation. LHEP 2021, which 2407.06207 cites for the Berry-phase-like term, derives the λ²[[A, Ã]] mixing from the same λ²p·(dp̃/dτ) term that makes x and x̃ non-commute, by the minimal-coupling shift p → p + A, p̃ → p̃ + Ã. #### r4-pheno: Q4 (reader confidence medium)

None of the nine papers writes a coupling term, a strength or a correlation function between the dual and the visible sector, and 'entangled' never appears for the dark sector. 'Correlated' appears in two papers. The Snowmass paper names 'intriguing correlations between dark matter, dark energy, and visible matter on the galactic, and galaxy cluster scales [8, 9]', observed correlations it aims to explain ('we would like to understand whether'). 2202.06890 has '(Dark matter, dark energy and visible matter are naturally correlated in this description [45, 46].)', with refs [45, 46] the modified-dark-matter papers (Ho–Minic–Ng 2010; Edmonds et al. 2017). The second of these places the correlation 'in correlations between dark matter and baryonic matter in galaxy rotation curves', through a critical acceleration related to Λ (its abstract, read via lit.py show). Addazi et al. cite the same two papers for 'consistent with observations'. 2011.08852 ties the dual matter sector to 'the dark matter sector that is sensitive to dark energy', also modified dark matter. On limits, 2011.08852 and 2503.20854 say that to zeroth (2503: first) order in λ, x and x̃ commute and the action separates into R(x) + R̃(x̃). The dual geometry then reaches ours only through G_N and Λ, and x–x̃ mixing arises at higher order in λ. 2503.20854's argument that Λ changes with our scale factor rests on the non-commutativity. A larger dual space means weaker gravity (1/G_N ∼ dual volume). My reading: in these nine papers 'correlated' names the observed MOND-like scaling of halos with baryons and Λ, not a stated coupling beyond gravity. Whether the school's dual sector couples beyond gravity must be judged from Minic 2020 and Berglund–Hübsch–Minic 2021 and 2023, which are not among my papers. #11 P4 (strict) and §13 row 7 require gravity alone.

Evidence: 2202.05104, 'Constraints on the nature of dark matter'; 2202.06890, 'Observations' and refs [45], [46]; 2111.05659, §2.3.4 and refs [492], [493]; 2011.08852, §2 after eq. (10) and §6; 2503.20854, §2; 1709.04388, abstract (cited source, read via lit.py show)

**Verifier (r4-pheno-verify): confirmed-in-part.** Accurate for the nine. The full text of the modified-dark-matter review supports the reading of 'correlated': its §2 ties the dark-matter mass profile to the baryonic one through a₀. Two qualifications. (1) One of 2202.06890's two sources does speculate on couplings beyond gravity: Ho, Minic and Ng 2010 (its [45]) say couplings of MONDian dark matter to Standard Model particles 'could be nonstandard'. (2) The question the reader left to Berglund–Hübsch–Minic 2021 is answered in that paper (arXiv:2010.15610). A coupling (λ⁸/L¹⁰)[φ, φ̃] 'as predicted by the doubled/non-commutative set-up' gives 'an explicit correlation between the dark matter sector and a visible sector' (§5). The correlation 'becomes invisible in effective field theory, and it vanishes as either λ→0 or L→∞' (§4). So within the school, 'correlated' covers both the observed scaling and a non-gravitational coupling term suppressed by λ⁸/L¹⁰.

#### r5-mdm: Q4 (reader confidence high)

In MDM's own papers the tie between dark matter and baryons is not a written interaction. It has three layers. (1) An observed regularity: the radial acceleration (mass-discrepancy) relation and the baryonic Tully–Fisher and Faber–Jackson relations, with an acceleration of order 10⁻¹⁰ m/s² ≈ cH0/2π. The authors call it 'an observational fact, which is independent of any theoretical assumptions' and say 'Occam's razor would favor' its emergence within gravity-only ΛCDM (2005.08927); 2009.12915 calls that 'The simplest' explanation; the 2017 review allows that 'it may be possible to reconcile this result with CDM models'. (2) A phenomenological mass profile, M′ = f(a/a0)·M: (1/π)(a0/a)² in 2010–2014, (1/2)(a0/a)² from a Jacobson-type de Sitter argument in 2016–2017, times α/(1 + r/r_MDM) with α ≈ 1 (0.5) in galaxies and 50–100 in clusters. Einstein's equations are unchanged ('Despite its appearance, Eq. (18) does not modify General Relativity'; 'we have not changed Einstein's equations'). Λ reaches the dark matter 'via the temperature of the de Sitter horizon'. The profile is 'arbitrary and a case of reverse engineering', 'lacking a dynamical theory', and the relation is a proportionality of stress tensors, T′_αβ = (a0²/2a²)T_αβ (1601.00662). (3) A property attributed to the quanta: knowledge of Λ, infinite statistics, non-locality; 'the microscopic basis for this correlation ... may have something to do with the quanta of MDM being non-local' (1709.04388 §4). So, among Q4's options, MDM's tie is a phenomenological profile plus a property of the dark quanta. It is not a stated non-gravitational interaction, and no coupling strength is ever given. Couplings to Standard Model particles appear only as speculation: 'could be nonstandard, and perhaps even of a spin-orbit type' (2010), and a toy matrix model with a 'Yukawa' coupling g to an SM operator, 'the necessary coupling to the dark matter sector' (2012), not linked to the acceleration correlation. The strength stays open: 'how strongly coupled is MDM to baryonic matter and how does MDM self-interact?' (2016, 2017). In 2017 (1801.00160) the authors call the relation 'a coupling between DM and BM governed by a universal acceleration scale' that 'must decouple from baryons at the cosmological scale. Exactly how this de-coupling occurs is currently under investigation'. In 2024 (2405.08744) the link is gravity alone: fuzzy dark matter in equilibrium between quantum and gravitational potentials, 'with the gravitational potential providing a link between the baryonic and dark matter'. What 'correlated' and 'entangled' mean in my papers: the observed relations and the profile; in 2011, the entangled left and right Rindler vacua; in 2017, the 'two entangled particles' of the metastring zero-mode sector, offered ('perhaps') as the origin of the profile relation, and later 'in momentum space'. The explicit non-gravitational terms are in the string-side papers that adopt MDM as their observed side: Minic 2020's Berry-phase-like 'metaparticle entanglement' term α′A_μ dÃ^μ/dt, 'uniquely different from the usual effective field theory interaction terms'; Berglund–Hübsch–Minić 2021's mixing term −(λ⁸/L¹⁰)f[[A, Ã]], 'invisible in effective field theory', which they identify with the Hubble-scale correlation 'studied in the context of modified dark matter'; Edmonds–Minic–Takeuchi 2021, 'correlated to the visible sector via the dark energy sector' (their Ref. 12, the MDM papers); Hübsch–Minić 2024, 'correlated (via the minimal length' λ). Limits: the MDM correlation is meant to switch off on cluster and cosmological scales (1709.04388 §2; 1801.00160), and by my reading of 1601.00662 Eq. (37), M′ ∝ a0² ∝ Λ at fixed a; the BHM term vanishes as λ → 0 or L → ∞. Against the program: #11 P4 strict allows no coupling beyond gravity (ε ≲ 10⁻¹³). My inference is that the school's 'correlated' differs from the program's gravity-only sector through the string-side terms, not through MDM's data, which the MDM authors themselves allow to arise by gravity alone.

Evidence: 1601.00662 §1.3, §2.3 Eqs. (35)–(37); 1709.04388 §1, §2, §2.2, §4, §5; 1308.3252 §II; 1602.00055 §1; 1801.00160 §2, §3; 1005.3537 Phenomenological Implications (2), (3); 1201.2365 §4, §5 Eq. (31); 1105.2916 §4; 2005.08927 essay text; 2009.12915 §V; 2405.08744 FDM paragraph; 2003.00318 §5; 2010.15610 §2–§3; 2109.12763 §5, §6.1; 2407.06207 §3.4

#### r6-foundations: Q4 (reader confidence medium)

Not addressed as a coupling between a dark and a visible sector: there is no dark sector in these papers. What they say about links between x and x̃ and about decoupling: (1) x and x̃ are linked kinematically by [x, x̃] = 2πiλ² (1706.03305 eq. 73), independent of the radius (§3) and present in trivial backgrounds; in the metaparticle it disappears only as α′ → 0 (1812.10821 §2). (2) The dual degrees of freedom decouple in limits: R → ∞ projects p̃ → 0 and x̃ decouples (1706.03305 §2; 1707.00312 §2); ordinary spacetime physics when m ≫ m̃ (1502.08005 §7.3). (3) Interactions: the closed-string product conserves momentum and winding, with a phase exp(iπw′(n − n′)) (1707.00312 §4, eq. 41); the metaparticle's p̃ is conserved, and ordinary particles (μ = 0, p̃ = 0) form 'a consistent truncation ... preserved by the interactions' (1812.10821 §2.1); particle-number-changing metaparticle interactions are not developed (§5). (4) 'Entanglement' appears once, for the two chiral components of a single metaparticle (1812.10821 §3.1); 'correlated' is never used for a dark–visible relation. My inference: (3) makes p̃ a conserved label, so a p̃ = 0 sector does not feed p̃ ≠ 0 states through p̃-conserving interactions; this is a selection rule, not a coupling strength. None of this is #11 P4's gravity-only coupling or its opposite; the later 'correlated' dark sector the record attributes to the school (notes/literature-check.md §3) is not defined in these papers.

Evidence: 1706.03305 §2 (end), §3 after eq. (52), §4 eq. (73); 1707.00312 §2 fn. 3, §4 eq. (41); 1812.10821 §2 after eq. (7), §2.1, §3.1, §5; 1502.08005 §7.3 eq. (233)

**Gravity alone:**

The two sectors couple through a kernel W = A e^{iΘ}, at horizons ('boundary') or through a complex collar; W = 1 decouples them.

#### r9-sweep: Q4 (reader confidence high)

At zeroth order in λ the two sectors share only the metric. In S_eff = ∫√(g g̃)[R + R̃ + L_m(A) + L̃_dm(Ã)], integrating x̃ puts both on √−g(x), with the gravitational background treated as non-dynamical (Minic 2020 eqs. 49–50; BHM 2021 eq. 8). BHM 2023 IJMPD says that 'to first order in the fundamental length, we can treat them as independent'. Beyond that order: (a) a 'mixing/correlation' term from the λ² p dp̃/dτ symplectic term, λ²[[A, Ã]], with F F̃ and ∂A ∂̃Ã cross terms before the x̃-integration. After it the term is (λ⁸/L¹⁰) f [[A, Ã]] (BHM 2021) or (λ₄²/L⁴) 𝓕 [[A, Ã]] (BHM 2023), with a fermion–dual-fermion mass mixing (λ⁹/L¹⁰ or λ₄³/L⁴) that gives a seesaw. It is 'invisible in effective field theory', 'vanishes as either λ → 0 or L → ∞', yet is 'finite even in the limit of zero momenta (deep infrared)'; 'the dark matter sector provides “sources” for the visible matter sector'. (b) A Berry-phase-like entanglement, α′A dÃ/dt (Minic 2020). (c) Non-commuting visible and dual fields, forming 'an effective Heisenberg algebra'. (d) The astrophysical MDM correlation through Λ (a₀ ~ cH₀). What 'correlated' means: 'These fields are in general correlated, due to their non-commutativity' (BHM 2023 §6.3). The strength is not computed: λ₄ and L are 'left unspecified'. FKLM 2021: the dual degrees of freedom 'decouple' at late times. No paper says 'gravity only' or 'kinetic mixing' (0 hits). Contrast: #11 P4 strict, ε ≲ 10⁻¹³.

Evidence: 2003.00318 §5 eqs. (47)–(50); 2010.15610 §4 eqs. (15)–(21); §5; 2212.06086 §6.2; §6.3; §6.4 and footnote 43; 2109.12763 §6.1; 2111.14205 item 4; 2307.16712 §5; 2104.00802 abstract; 1709.04388 §4

**Verifier (r9-sweep-verify): confirmed-in-part.** 'No paper says gravity only' is wrong. HM 2024 (2407.06207, §5.3, 'Neutrinos:') says the Berry-phase-like correlation term (cited to BHM 2021) has a mass scale that 'defines the decoupling energy, below which the dark and visible matter interact as required, only gravitationally'. It identifies that scale with M_SM ≈ 4.5×10¹⁴ GeV; recomputed from its Table 1 by scratch/r9-sweep-verify/bin/msm.py, 4.48×10¹⁴ GeV. The family-(a) patterns missed 'only gravitationally'. So one school paper assigns a scale to the coupling, though it does not compute it, and puts every accessible energy in a gravity-only regime. The answer also omits the school's own counterweights to reading the terms as suppressed: 'with a particular double scaling, this term can be finite!' and λ⁸/L¹⁰ 'should not be naively considered to be trans-Planckian' (BHM 2021 §4; BHM 2023 §6.4 likewise); 'the usual visible photon/dark photon coupling is subdominant to this term' (BHM 2021 §5, BHM 2023 §6.5); and the fermion mass gap 'may well be phenomenologically relevant — even for a minuscule numerical value' (BHM 2023 §6.5).
