## Q5: Detection

#### r1-priority: Q5 (reader confidence high)

Minic 2020 makes three claims:
- Dark-matter quanta 'should be detectable through their particular metaparticle entanglement to visible matter'; no experiment and no rate are given.
- 'If the quanta of modified dark matter are metaparticles, this may explain why, so far, dark matter detection experiments have failed' (conditional).
- Through modified dark matter, an acceleration scale near cH ~ 1e-10 m/s² in rotation curves, with profiles consistent with galaxies and clusters, and a testable dispersion relation E² + μ²/E² = p² + m².

Earlier school papers explain null detection by non-locality: Chang et al. 2016 and Edmonds et al. 2017; the 2017 paper defers collider, direct and indirect tests until 'a more concrete theory'. FKGLM 2021 predicts infrared dispersion modifications, for neutrinos μ_ν ≲ 1e-2 eV². No collider prediction appears. Contrast #11 §13 row 7, 'Permanent null'.

Evidence: 2003.00318 section 5; 1605.04361 section 5; 1709.04388 abstract and conclusion; 2104.00802 eq. (17); talk:mphys10-2019-Minic slide 5

**Verifier (r1-priority-verify): confirmed.** 

#### r2-dual-sm: Q5 Detection (reader confidence high)

Colliders: 'correlated events in the accelerators, ... not products of any standard particle decays'; a dual photon correlated with ours and distinct from the EFT dark photon; a dual Z distinct from a Z′; dual-QCD effects in the deep IR (2010.15610 §5; 2212.06086 §6.5). Particle physics: neutrino masses through the dual-neutrino seesaw (2010.15610 §4–§5; 2212.06086 §6.4–§6.5); a new view of the strong-CP problem by averaging over a constant axion (both); CP phases from the dual SM (2307.16712 §4.3); the dual Higgs as an inflaton (2212.06086 §6.5); a deformed dispersion relation E² + μ²/E² = p² + m², with a neutrino bound putting μ near the dark-energy scale (2109.12763 §4; 2212.06086 §4.3, citing arXiv:2104.00802). Astrophysics and cosmology: MDM's acceleration scale cH₀ ~ 10⁻¹⁰ m/s² in galaxy and cluster fits and in the BTF and BFJ relations; a predicted (1+z) scaling of σ⁴/(GM_bar) (2109.12763 §6). The authors caveat that BFJ 'could well be explained by more conventional means' and that MDM lacks 'concrete scenarios'. Also: the H0 tension, through dual gravity (2010.15610 §4), a varying G_N (2212.06086 §6.2) or light dual states as dark radiation (§6.4); the coincidence of dark and visible abundances (2212.06086 §4.8; 2307.16712 §5); 'one should search for metaparticles' (2212.06086 §7). Direct detection: dark quanta 'should be detectable through their particular metaparticle correlation/entanglement' (2109.12763 §4). 'If the quanta of MDM are metaparticles, this may explain why, so far, dark matter detection experiments have failed' (2109.12763 §6.1): conditional, with no mechanism given. The 'usual detection analysis ... should be revisited' (2212.06086 §4.3). No paper predicts a permanent null. My inference: the school's collider and dual-photon signals are non-gravitational DM–SM couplings, which #11 §13 row 7 names as killing the program's candidates. A confirmed signal of that kind would therefore separate the two, which bears on the open question in root CLAUDE.md. The MDM acceleration-scale correlation is likewise a halo prediction the program does not make (#11 §13 row 1 is a knee in σ_T(v)).

Evidence: 2010.15610 §4, §5; 2109.12763 §4, §6.1–§6.3; 2212.06086 §4.3, §4.8, §6.2, §6.4, §6.5, §7; 2307.16712 §4.3, §5

**Verifier (r2-dual-sm-verify): confirmed-in-part.** The neutrino bound that puts μ near the dark-energy scale appears only in 2109.12763 §4 (its [45] = arXiv:2104.00802). 2212.06086 §4.3 gives the deformed dispersion relation (citing [250]) without the bound. The (1+z) scaling is 'the possible z-dependence', resting on 'this simple handwaving argument' (2109.12763 §6.2), not a firm prediction. On the nulls, the nearest the paper comes to a reason is 'The metaparticle is fundamentally non-local, and thus it should not be associated with effective local field theory' (§4), followed by 'the natural model for MDM quanta could be provided by the metaparticle realizations of the SM particles' (§6.1). #### r3-gravitized: Q5 (reader confidence high)

None of the six texts predicts a dark-matter signal in direct detection or at colliders. A grep for direct detection, collider, LHC, accelerator and detectab finds only LHC planar jets from a 2d reduction of spacetime (2604.19418 §6–§7; 2407.06207 §3.5), which is not a dark-matter signal.
No explanation of the direct-detection nulls is offered, beyond 2407.06207's 'interact as required, only gravitationally' below the decoupling energy. I read 'as required' as an appeal to the nulls; the paper does not say what requires it.
The stated prediction is astrophysical:
- the λ-correlation 'suggest[s] the origin (from dark matter) of the observed scaling (found in galaxies, clusters, superclusters) and the universal acceleration a₀ ~ cH/(2π)' (2407.06207 §3.4);
- 2604.19418 §5.1 calls it 'Milgrom's scaling', offers a mechanism ('fuzzy dark matter is in the non-relativistic description sensitive to the quantum pressure, which in turn, is directly sensitive to the vacuum energy'), and claims the observed acceleration scale 'is evidence for ... the correlation of dual fields of fuzzy dark matter to the observed Standard Model fields of visible matter'.
The laboratory tests in these papers are of gravitized quantum theory and modular spacetime, not of dark matter: triple and higher-order interference, 'entwining' (R-matrix) instead of scattering, gravitational-wave echoes.
#11, the lab notebook #1 and the literature-check note never mention Milgrom, MOND or a₀ (case-sensitive grep). My inference: a dark–visible correlation at a₀ ~ cH/(2π) is a candidate observable for telling the school's sector from the program's, which the root CLAUDE.md lists as open.

#### r4-pheno: Q5 (reader confidence medium)

None of the nine papers makes a direct-detection or collider prediction for the dual sector, and none discusses the null results or a permanent null. The phenomenology they name is of three kinds. (1) Galactic and cluster scaling relations, as an aim (Snowmass; its ref [31] is Dai and Lu 2017 on MOND-like behaviour in ΛCDM simulations). (2) Modified-dark-matter correlations with a critical acceleration related to Λ, via the references of 2202.06890 and Addazi et al. (3) For observed quanta, 'the most specific prediction': an IR-deformed metaparticle dispersion relation E_p² + μ²/E_p² = p² + m², with μ of order 10⁻² eV² for neutrinos (2202.06890). One cited source bears on the null results: the modified-dark-matter review 1709.04388 (abstract only) says non-local aspects of its quanta 'may explain why, so far, dark matter detection experiments have failed to detect dark matter particles'. That is conditional, and it is about modified-dark-matter quanta, which 2011.08852 links to the dual sector. Compare #11 §13 row 7 ('Permanent null').

Evidence: 2202.05104, 'Constraints on the nature of dark matter'; 2202.06890, 'Observations'; 2111.05659, §2.3.4; 1709.04388, abstract

**Verifier (r4-pheno-verify): confirmed-in-part.** For the nine, right. But the cited review 1709.04388, which the reader read in its abstract only, ends by expecting tests at colliders and at direct- and indirect-detection experiments once a 'more concrete theory' exists. #### r5-mdm: Q5 (reader confidence high)

Data and fits, numbers as printed. Galaxies (1308.3252): the 30 Ursa Major spirals of Sanders & Verheijen, z ≈ 0.003; a_c = 1.2×10⁻⁸ (printed 'cm s-1'); M/L is the one free parameter for MDM and MOND, against three for NFW; 'all three models fit the data well', and MDM and MOND are 'virtually indistinguishable' over ∼1–30 kpc. The prediction against MOND is a central turnover of the MDM density (r < 1 kpc for a Milky Way-sized galaxy; by code 0.1r_c = 1.08 kpc). Clusters: 93 X-ray clusters (1602.00055) give ⟨M_N/M_obs⟩ ≈ 4.4, ⟨M_MOND/M_obs⟩ ≈ 2.1 and ⟨M_MDM/M_obs⟩ ≈ 1.0 with ξ ≈ 0.5, a result the authors say is superseded. 13 Chandra clusters of Vikhlinin et al. (1601.00662): MDM 'performs as well as CDM, and considerably better than MOND', with α ∼ 100 and r_MDM ∼ 10 kpc (α ∼ 50–100 in 1709.04388), against α ≈ 1 (0.5 with r_MDM = 20 kpc) in galaxies, a change of two orders of magnitude the authors 'admit' they cannot explain. The 2017 review plots 386 points from the 30 galaxies against the RAR, with ā0 = (1.20 ± 0.02)×10⁻¹⁰ m/s² and BTFR A = (47 ± 6) M⊙ s⁴/km⁴ (by code 1/(ā0G) = 62.8, paper 63). Scaling papers: RAR a§ = (1.2 ± 0.02(stat) ± 0.24(syst))×10⁻¹⁰ m/s² from 2693 points in 153 SPARC galaxies; a BFJR with n = 4 over M_bar = 10^{4∼14} M⊙, from 62 HIFLUGCS clusters, 56 ellipticals and 56 globular clusters (2005.08927); BTFR slope (1.6 ± 0.2)a0 and a∅ = (1.5 +1.4/−0.7)a0, with clusters alone preferring n ≈ 3 and n ≈ 4 only jointly, a∅ ≈ 1.4a0 and 1.3a0 (2009.12915); a0 = (1.3 ± 0.3)×10⁻¹⁰ (BTFR) and (1.5 +0.9/−0.6)×10⁻¹⁰ m/s² (E0 galaxies), no redshift dependence to z ≈ 2.5 in 100 RC100 galaxies (2405.08744). By code, cH0/2π = 1.05×10⁻¹⁰ m/s² for H0 = 67.74, so the RAR value is 1.15 times it. Proposed discriminators: the central turnover (2013); strong lensing 'appears' sufficient because a0 = cH0 ≈ 6a_c (2016–17); an acceleration floor ∝ a0 in galaxies but none in clusters (Dec 2017); a0 in colliding clusters would show that a0 is fundamental (2020); a redshift dependence of a0 for the fuzzy-dark-matter account (2024); no dark-matter effects in the solar system, attributed to MDM's built-in External Field Effect (2013); a hope that Fermi-LAT gamma-ray maps test the profile (2013). Direct detection and colliders: 'dramatic signatures in high energy particle experiments' expected (2012); tests 'at colliders, dark matter direct detection experiments and indirect detection experiments' deferred to 'A more concrete theory' (2016, 2017); primordial-black-hole search techniques 'may be relevant' (2012). Null results: non-locality 'may explain why, so far, dark matter detection experiments have failed' (2017 abstract), and Minic 2020 and Edmonds–Minic–Takeuchi 2021 add the conditional 'If the quanta of MDM are metaparticles, this may explain why'. No MDM paper predicts a permanent null (contrast #11 §13 row 7). ## Q6: Gravity

#### r1-priority: Q6 (reader confidence high)

The dark sector gravitates on the observed spacetime x through the same metric. Minic 2020 eq. (50) puts L̃_dm(Ã(x)) under √g(x) after x̃ is integrated out, and eq. (57) puts it in the same intensive action. A dual metric g̃(x̃) exists; its curvature R̃ gives Λ > 0. Newton's constant comes from the x̃ integration: 'the weakness of gravity is determined by the size of the canonically conjugate dual space' (Minic 2020 section 6.1; BHM 2019: 'the curvature and size of the canonically conjugate dual space are the cosmological and gravitational constants'). Compare #11 §8: χ propagates on g.

Evidence: 2003.00318 section 5 eq. (50), section 6.1 eq. (52)-(54), section 6.2 eq. (57); 1905.09463 introduction; talk:mphys10-2019-Minic slide 6

**Verifier (r1-priority-verify): confirmed.** 

#### r2-dual-sm: Q6 Gravity (reader confidence high)

Newton's constant is set by the size of the dual space and Λ by its curvature. Integrating x̃ in Tseytlin's ∫∫√g√g̃[R(x) + R̃(x̃)] 'defines the gravitational constant G_N' (2010.15610 §2; 2109.12763 §5). 'The size of the dual space is the gravitational constant' (2212.06086 §7). 'G_N could vary by varying the volume of the dual spacetime' (2212.06086 §6.2). With Ṽ ∼ V⁻¹ by T-duality the action becomes intensive (2109.12763 §5). Dark matter gravitates on the observed metric: after x̃-integration, L̃_dm(x) sits beside L_m(x) under √−g(x) (2010.15610 eq. 8; 2109.12763 eqs. 24, 32; 2212.06086 eq. 6.19). There is also a dual gravity sector R̃(x̃), 'associated with a dynamical dual spacetime', which gives dark energy (2212.06086 §4.3). It is correlated with ours 'via the scale of non-commutativity' (2010.15610 §4; 2212.06086 §6.4). Compare #11 §8, where χ propagates on g, and §7, where momentum gravity is a massive spin-2 X. The school has no massive dual graviton; its dual gravity is the dual curvature (my comparison).

After integrating over x̃, both visible A(x) and dark Ã(x) are described 'in classical x spacetime'. ## Q7: Dynamics

#### r1-priority: Q7 (reader confidence medium)

No velocity-dependent or gated self-interaction appears; contrast #11 §8. The quanta are non-local. Minic 2020 mentions a 'Friedel-like bi-local static potential' and 'non-local generalizations of quantum statistics'; HMN 2012 argues for infinite statistics, and Edmonds et al. 2017 for possible '(dark) stars' of such quanta. The momenta p and p̃ 'can be ... widely separated'. 'Two entangled particles in momentum space' (Edmonds et al., December 2017) and 'Berry phase in momentum space' (Belgrade slides) are the only momentum-space language.

No localization in x̃ or in momentum is proposed: x̃ is integrated out, and Chang et al. 2016 make the quanta 'extended in both space and momentum', the opposite of #11 §12's monopoles. Self-interaction is listed as an open question (Edmonds et al. 2017). No condensate or wave form appears.

Evidence: 2003.00318 section 4.3, section 5 footnote 22; 1201.2365 abstract; 1709.04388 conclusion; 1801.00160 conclusion; 1605.04361 section 5; talk:mphys10-2019-Minic slides 4-5

**Verifier (r1-priority-verify): confirmed-in-part.** Three additions. (1) The Belgrade slide 4 writes out the static potential between metaparticles: it depends on the dual separation r̃ as well as on r (Yukawa in r − r̃, oscillating in r + r̃, a 'Friedel oscillation'), with no velocity dependence. (2) 'No condensate' needs a qualification: Jejjala, Kavic and Minic 2007 (Minic 2020's [78]) ask, as a speculation, whether Standard Model matter is a collective excitation around 'the dark matter condensate'. (3) Chang et al. 2016 predict halos whose density falls with time as the non-local excitations decay into hot daughters.

Counter-evidence: talk:mphys10-2019-Minic slide 4 (PDF p. 23), slide image: 'V(r, r̃) ~ ∫d^{D−1}p d^{D−1}p̃ e^{ipr} e^{ip̃r̃} δ(pp̃−μ)/(p²+p̃²+m²) ... (r_± = r ± r̃)' | 0705.4581 conclusion: 'Could this mean that the standard model matter is just a collective excitation around the dark matter condensate?' (FOUND) | 1605.04361 §5: 'So if the CDM halos around galaxies consist of non-local excitations, we expect their density to decrease with time.' (FOUND)

Not found in any of the papers: self-interaction, dependence on relative velocity, a momentum-overlap gate, or localization in x̃ or momentum ('self-interact' occurs 0 times; 'velocit' only in unrelated contexts).

Related dynamics: the metaparticle constraint p·p̃ = μ fixes 'the component of the momentum parallel to p̃' of each particle (1812.10821 §2.1); p̃ is conserved, and metaparticles with different p̃ have different dispersion relations and 'de facto effectively experience different notions of space-time' (§2.1); ordinary particles (p̃ = 0) form a consistent truncation preserved by interactions; particle-number-changing interactions are left to future work and only background gauge fields are coupled (§5). An INSPIRE search of titles and abstracts for 'metaparticle' returns five papers (1812.10821, Minic 2020, Barnes–Heremans–Minic 2021, two by Chouha 2026), none developing metaparticle interactions. Closed-string interactions conserve momentum and winding and carry a phase depending on both (1707.00312 eq. 41). My reading: nothing here acts as a gate in relative momentum. Conservation of p̃ restricts which total p̃ can appear; it does not suppress scattering as the relative momentum grows, and it is not #11 §8's knee at v*. Localization: compactifying x delocalizes x̃ 'consistent with the Heisenberg uncertainty principle' (1502.08005 §7.4); the modular cell is these papers' localization concept, for space, not for matter.

## Q9: Polarization and vacuum

#### r1-priority: Q9 (reader confidence medium)

Spacetime is a choice of polarization. FLM 2017: 'a choice of polarization of phase space'; 'In GR there is a preferred polarization: the spacetime one'; Born reciprocity 'broken by the choice of the principle of absolute locality'; η 'describes the choice of polarization' and is dynamical in quantum gravity. Minic 2020: 'a choice of polarization is a choice of a spacetime'; choices are 'related by ... T-duality'. Metaparticles: 'nothing special about the x^μ; we could have taken any linear subspace, such as x̃, p', so the other choice is equally available. Minic 2020 asks 'whether Born reciprocity can be used as a criterion for vacuum selection'.

FKGLM 2021 goes further: dual Lorentz symmetry breaking 'happens at the level of the vacuum sector', a 'choice of ground state fixes' the frame, and 'the expansion of the Universe can act as a mechanism that selects the decoupling frame'. That is a proposed dynamical selection. None of these papers gives a potential or an order parameter for the polarization; compare #11 P2′ and P5 (η as order parameter, spontaneous breaking).

Evidence: recid:1590124 pp. 2-4, p. 9; 2003.00318 section 2.2, section 5, section 7; 1812.10821 section 2; 2104.00802 after eq. (12)

**Verifier (r1-priority-verify): confirmed-in-part.** Four passages qualify the answer. (1) FKGLM 2021 says 'the dual symmetry is fundamentally broken at the quantum level', since the dual Lorentz action 'is not a canonical transformation'; its vacuum-level selection is not spontaneous breaking of an exact symmetry. (2) FLM 2017 builds η = ωK from a real structure K that is +1 on the spacetime Lagrangian and −1 on 'momentum space'; #11 P2′ reads this construction as an order parameter. (3) Minic 2020 §7 does sketch a speculative 'attractor mechanism' for vacuum selection, for masses, couplings and cosmological parameters, not for the polarization. (4) 1812.10821 relates the other polarizations by 'generalized Fourier transforms'.

Counter-evidence: 2104.00802, before eq. (12): 'the dual symmetry is fundamentally broken at the quantum level' and 'In other words the dual Lorentz action is not a canonical transformation.' (both FOUND) | recid:1590124 §3, p. 8: 'As we have seen above, it is η that governs the splitting of phase space into space and momentum space.' (matched locally) | 2003.00318 §7: 'might be the consequence of an attractor mechanism that makes the observed cosmology and particle physics “universal”' (FOUND) | 1812.10821 §2: 'Generally, other polarizations can be obtained from our analysis by generalized Fourier transforms and will be considered elsewhere.' (FOUND)

#### r2-dual-sm: Q9 Polarization and vacuum (reader confidence high)

The polarization is a choice, with no mechanism. 'A choice of polarization is a choice of a spacetime' (2109.12763 §2.1). 'The spacetime polarization is not fixed. Still one can pick a particular ... spacetime polarization'; 'we could have picked the dual, x̃,p polarization', with the same seesaw 'in either case' (2212.06086 §4.4). The spacetime is 'ambiguous, with different choices related by ... T-duality' (2109.12763 §4; 2212.06086 §4.3; 2307.16712 §2.4). Polarizing on the complementary half swaps T and T*, which the authors read as mirror symmetry (2111.14205; 2212.06086 §6.6). The section condition is likened to choosing the coordinate or momentum representation, 'or indeed any other (αp+βq)-polarization' (2111.14205). The other choice is thus treated as related by a symmetry and equally valid, which bears on #11 P5's 'mirror vacuum is equally lawful' (my reading). No potential or order parameter selects the polarization. H relates to 'the choice of quantum vacuum' (2212.06086 §6.1) and the vacuum depends on H (2109.12763 §2.1); η, H and ω are dynamical fields. 'Vacuum selection' is named only as a problem to be seen anew, 'along the lines of the attractor solutions' (2010.15610 §8). The one spontaneous breaking in these papers is of the second Lorentz group O(1,d−1)₂ in a sector of fixed p̃ (2212.06086 §4.3), not of Born duality. This is consistent with #11 §3, which credits the preferred polarization to Freidel, Leigh and Minic and claims only the spontaneous reading.

Evidence: 2109.12763 §2.1, §3, §4; 2212.06086 §4.3, §4.4, §6.1, §6.6; 2111.14205 Introduction, Mirror Doubling; 2307.16712 §2.4; 2010.15610 §8

**Verifier (r2-dual-sm-verify): confirmed-in-part.** 'The one spontaneous breaking in these papers' is too strong: 2212.06086 §2.2 reviews KKLT and LVS, which 'break supersymmetry spontaneously'. The point that matters stands: no paper breaks Born duality or the polarization spontaneously. The answer misses the nearest thing to a selection criterion, which is for the modular vacuum, not the polarization: 'the choice of modular vacuum which is determined by the cosmological constant', with holography and an IR condition as input (2212.06086 §4.8). It also misses that extensification can run 'along the space directions or the dual space directions' (§4.3). And 2111.14205's 'by virtue of having to polarize on one half' makes the choice of a half necessary, which is close to #11 §3's 'The breaking is mandatory' (verifier's comparison).

Counter-evidence: 2212.06086 §2.2: '(2) The LVS formalism was, besides breaking supersymmetry spontaneously and generating the' (FOUND) | 2212.06086 §4.8: 'the necessary input that holography (and IR condition) provided in the choice of modular vacuum which is determined by the cosmological constant' (FOUND) | 2212.06086 §4.3: 'We stress that there are really two ways in which we can extend the cell[245]: either we extensify modular space along the space directions or the dual space directions.' (FOUND)

#### r3-gravitized: Q9 (reader confidence high)

Classical spacetime is called a choice of polarization. The papers say what selects it, but never call the selection spontaneous.
The choice: 'Classical spacetime label x of canonical QFT corresponds to a choice of (classical spacetime) polarization in modular (quantum) spacetime with a contextuality parameter λ' (2407.06207 §3.3; repeated in 2604.19418 §5.1 and 2610.19522 §3.2).
Selection:
- 'Classical spacetime emerges from the process of extensification (imagine one unit length in dual direction and many, N, modular cells in the spacetime direction). Spacetime emerges, in the large N limit, as a natural pointer basis'; the dual labels are hidden variables 'associated with the spacetime pointer basis, selected by quantum gravity' (2407.06207 §3.4, citing FLM 2016);
- 'quantum gravity picks the natural basis for measurement and classical description - the spacetime basis' (2604.19418 §5.1); 'the emergent measurement basis is the spacetime basis' (§7).
Dynamical polarization:
- 'quantum gravity ... should have both a dynamical polarization and a dynamical geometry' (2604.19418 §1.1), where quantum mechanics' 'dynamical polarization' is ψ → Uψ;
- 2610.19522 §3.2 names η 'the polarization (or locality) metric' that 'encodes the distinction between spacetime-like and dual-spacetime-like aspects of phase space'; 'Curving η means gravitizing the quantum'; gravitized quantum theory 'makes both its polarization and its probability rule dynamical'.
Other choices: 'just one polarization allowed', 'different choices related by ... T-duality' (2407.06207 §3.5), 'just one of many, and in metastring theory, different observers see different spacetimes', in superposition (2610.19522 §3.2). These are lawful as observer-dependent frames (relative locality), not as a mirror vacuum.

#### r4-pheno: Q9 (reader confidence high)

Nothing in the nine selects the polarization through a vacuum, a potential or an order parameter, and none calls the choice spontaneous or dynamical. 2503.20854 §1 says the non-commutativity makes 'the very notion of spacetime polarization observer dependent'. In 2202.06890 and Addazi §2.2.2.2, observer-dependent spacetimes are 'slices'/'sections' of quantum spacetime, depending on the probe, for example its momentum. 2212.00901 says 'A choice of basis is equivalent to a choice of polarization of phase space' and calls the Schrödinger polarization 'singular'. 2303.17495 says 'The change of polarization is a unitary change of basis', with modular spacetime 'corresponding to a generic polarization'. The polarization metric η is a dynamical field, but so are ω and H (2011.08852 §2; 2503.20854 App. B), whereas #11 P2′ keeps ω rigid. The 'Vacuum Selection' of the Snowmass title is selection among perturbative string vacua, compactifications that yield the Standard Model, by a mechanism modeled on horizontal gene transfer in biophysics. It is not the selection of a polarization. Whether the world with the other choice is equally lawful (#11 P5) is not discussed. This agrees with the record's literature check (§3, 'The vacuum'), which found no such mechanism.

#### r6-foundations: Q9 (reader confidence high)

Definitions. A polarization is 'a choice of Lagrangian subspace of phase space' (1502.08005 §2), encoded by the neutral 'polarization metric' η, with spacetime L = ker(η + ω) and momentum space L̃ = ker(η − ω) (§2.1); quantum space is 'a choice of polarization for the Heisenberg algebra', a maximal commutative subalgebra, generically modular (1606.01829); spacetime is the η-null Lagrangian with K = +1 (1405.3949 §2). What selects it. (1) In general relativity and the usual string η is kinematical: 'space-time provides a preferred polarization that cannot be modified' (1405.3949 §1.3); 'There is a preferred basis — the space-time basis' (§1.1). (2) FLM propose making the split dynamical: η and J dynamical, 'spacetime is a derived dynamical concept' (1307.7080, 26 July 2013); 'the purely kinematical splitting ... has to be made dynamical' (1405.3949); 'the choice of space-time inside phase space as a dynamical question' (1502.08005 §7); η's deformations are collective excitations of winding modes (§8). (3) A concrete selection: worldsheet locality selects the target-space locality condition (L-projectability; quantum mechanically Λ-periodicity) that makes a Lagrangian spacetime (1502.08005 §6.4, §7). (4) For the compact string the radius decides: 'which compact coordinate, x or x̃, plays the role of a spatial coordinate depends on context' (1707.00312 §2), x at large R and x̃ at small R; T-dualities 'change the choice of polarization' (1706.03305 §5). (5) In quantum mechanics and in the metaparticle the choice is a basis or analysis choice: 'A choice of quantum polarization is a choice of basis, unitarily equivalent to any other' (1606.01829 §3); the metaparticle is studied in the (x, p̃) polarization, with 'nothing special about the x^μ' (1812.10821 §2). Spontaneous? No: 'spontan' and 'order parameter' have zero matches in all seven texts; there is no potential for η and no symmetry-breaking vacuum. 'Vacuum' in 1606.01829 is the ground state of a kinetic 'Hamiltonian' built from H, squeezed when H changes (§1.7); it does not select η. The η compatible with a fixed ω and a fixed vacuum form U(d)/O(d) (1606.01829 §2), the Lagrangian Grassmannian Λ(d) (a standard identification), the manifold #11 §10 (Λ(1), τ's circle) and §14 item 12 (Λ(4)) use; FLM treat it as a set of inequivalent choices, not as a vacuum manifold. Is the other choice equally lawful? In the flat theory, yes: T-duality J is a symmetry of the metastring action and exchanges the two Lagrangians (1502.08005 §2.4.1, §8); the dual description carries 'precisely the same information' (1707.00312 §3), and the string product is consistent with T-duality (§4). This matches #11 P5's 'the mirror vacuum is equally lawful'. Relative locality: different probes may carry different spacetimes ('different strings propagate in different space-time backgrounds', 1405.3949 §2; 'each string can carry a different space-time', 1502.08005 §2.3; metaparticles with different p̃ 'effectively experience different notions of space-time', 1812.10821 §2.1). My reading: this is the closest these papers come to #11 P4's matter in the other polarization, but it is a property of all probes, not a separate sector of matter, and nothing assigns ordinary matter to one Lagrangian and dark matter to the other. FLM's 'kinematical' describes the breaking by general relativity; their own proposal is a dynamical η without a selection mechanism. The program's spontaneous reading, with η as order parameter (#11 §3, P2′), has no counterpart here.

#### r7-vacuum: Q9 (reader confidence high)

No school text read gives a potential, an order parameter or Goldstone modes for the choice of polarization, and none calls that choice spontaneous. But the school does not treat the choice only as kinematical, and one paper puts its breaking in the vacuum.
1. GR's breaking is called kinematical, after Born. 1405.3949 §1.1 (2014): 'this kinematical breaking is different from the dynamical breaking ... due to the choice of a particular Hamiltonian'. J. Phys. Conf. Ser. 804 012032 §2 (2017): 'Born reciprocity is broken by the choice of the principle of absolute locality'. Quantum gravity 'ought to restore the kinematical duality principle' (1405.3949 §1.1).
2. Their proposal makes the splitting dynamical. 'the purely kinematical splitting of phase space into space and momentum space has to be made dynamical' (1405.3949 §1.3). 'in the context of quantum gravity, both H and η have to be dynamical' (1502.08005 §2.1, p. 11). 'the choice of space-time inside phase space as a dynamical question' (1502.08005 §7, p. 47). 'spacetime is a derived dynamical concept' (1307.7080). Quantum gravity 'should have both a dynamical polarization and a dynamical geometry' (2604.19418 §1.1), where a 'dynamical polarization' is ψ → Uψ, a unitary change of basis.
3. The splitting sits in solutions, not in the law. 'This formulation does not require the presence of a bi-Lagrangian structure K. However when we look at solutions of string theory, we find this extra structure' (1405.3949 §2). Solutions 'are labelled by bi-Lagrangians' (1307.7080). Linearized background equations from a double RG say H 'can depend only on X or only on P' (1405.3949 §2.2), and 804 012032 §7 gives flow equations for η. My inference: a law without K and solutions with K is the logical form of spontaneous breaking, but FLM neither name it so nor compare vacua.
4. The one vacuum-level statement is Freidel, Kowalski-Glikman, Leigh and Minic 2021 (2104.00802, v1 1 April 2021, checked in the v1 PDF). The dual Lorentz symmetry, which moves the commuting spacetime coordinate from x to x + αx̃, 'is broken. The symmetry breaking happens at the level of the vacuum sector, and thus a choice of ground state fixes a value of δ'. They 'propose that the expansion of the Universe can act as a mechanism that selects the decoupling frame', and work it in a simple model: at late times only states without dual momentum survive. 'the symmetry is restored as one goes back in time', and at a → 0 'a dual universe made of dual particles is accessed'. They tie this to dark matter/energy ('states of class II being remnants').
   Limits of this statement:
   - the same essay says the dual symmetry 'is fundamentally broken at the quantum level' by λ, so it does not separate explicit from vacuum breaking;
   - there is no potential, order parameter, Goldstone mode or the word 'spontaneous';
   - the broken group is the non-compact dual Lorentz group, not ρ;
   - the doubled space is (x, x̃) at λ² = ℏα′, with separate momenta p and p̃, not the program's (x, p) at ℏ.
5. Modular vacua: the vacuum is the ground state of a chosen 'Hamiltonian' E_H and depends on H (1606.01829 §1.6; 2003.00318 §2.2). In extensified vacua 'The choice of Lagrangian is controlled by the choice of complex structure that enters the definition of the “Hamiltonian”' (1606.01829 §2.1), and that 'Hamiltonian' is 'a tool' (fn. 8). H 'is related to the choice of quantum vacuum' (2212.06086 §6.1). So a chosen H fixes the vacuum and the Lagrangian, and nothing dynamical chooses H.
6. Other selectors the school names:
   - consistency: worldsheet locality (1502.08005 §6.1, §7); a section condition 'imposed' for integrability (2111.14205; 2212.06086 §6.6);
   - observer or context: 'each observer makes his/her own choice' (804 012032 §6); 'tied to the contextual nature of quantum measurement' (2407.06207 §3.5); 'observer dependent' (2503.20854 §1); a lattice 'set contextually by some experimental apparatus' (1606.01829 §3);
   - scale: large modes probe X and stringy-small modes probe its mirror (1902.08617); the decoupling limit a → ∞ (2104.00802); a 'thermodynamical limit' (1606.01829); a 'singular limit' (2003.00318 §6.3);
   - the authors' convenience: 'nothing special about the x^μ' (1812.10821 §2); 'one can pick' (2212.06086 §4.4).
7. 'Vacuum selection' in the school's titles and texts means the string-vacuum problem, not the polarization. 2003.00318 §7 asks, as a question, 'whether Born reciprocity can be used as a criterion for vacuum selection in quantum gravity, which selects “maximally symmetric solutions” both in spacetime (de Sitter space) and in the matter sector (Standard Model and its dual, describing the dark matter sector)'. The 'vacuum selection principle' of 2202.05104 is 'the origin of the SM', modelled on horizontal gene transfer. 1502.08005 §8, 2010.15610 §8 and 1307.7080's 'vacuum problem' use the same sense.
8. The other choice is equally lawful throughout:
   - T-duality exchanges L with its complement (1502.08005 §8; 1706.03305 §5; 1405.3949 §2.1);
   - the two are 'on equal footing' (1405.3949 §2.1; 2407.06207 §3; 804 012032 §1);
   - the spectrum is invariant under p ↔ p̃ (2104.00802);
   - 'we could have picked the dual, x̃,p polarization', and 'in either case' the same seesaw formula follows (2212.06086 §4.4);
   - swapping the polarization is mirror symmetry (2111.14205);
   - 'neither of the two factors is a preferred base' (1902.08617).
   This is, in substance, P5's 'the mirror vacuum is equally lawful', held as a symmetry of the theory.
9. What is rigid differs. The school makes ω dynamical too (1502.08005 §2.3; 2407.06207 §3.5, §4.1) and puts 'the rigidity of the quantum theory' in 'the flatness of the polarization metric η' (1502.08005 §1). P2′ keeps ω rigid and makes η emergent. For FLM a dynamical η 'necessarily leads to a relaxation of locality' (1405.3949 §1.3); in the program, selecting a polarization is what makes local physics possible (#11 §3).
Test of #11 §3:
- 'They treat the breaking as kinematical' fits what FLM say of GR, not what they propose (points 2 and 3). [26S] itself makes η dynamical (p. 11), which #11 P2′ already credits.
- 'call the choice "a dynamical question"' is accurate: 1502.08005 §7, p. 47. The §3 quotation is on p. 3, as cited.
- 'This program's addition is to read it as spontaneous breaking, with η as the order parameter' holds for the word 'spontaneous', for η as order parameter, for a potential, for the U(1) Goldstone angle and for the K-sign lock (P4). It does not hold for the vacuum as the place where the breaking occurs: FKLM 2021 state that, in the doubled (x, x̃) setting, before the program.

**Verifier (r7-vacuum-verify): confirmed-in-part.** These parts hold. No school text gives a potential, an order parameter or Goldstone modes for the choice of polarization. FLM call GR's breaking kinematical and propose a dynamical splitting. 'Vacuum selection' means the string-vacuum problem. The other choice is equally lawful. 2104.00802 (v1 2021-04-01; the passage is in the v1 PDF, pp. 5–6) puts a breaking tied to the choice of polarization 'at the level of the vacuum sector', and no record file quotes it. Corrections: (1) 'The symmetry is restored as one goes back in time' concerns the Lorentz symmetry violated by the infrared dispersion relation (17) in the cosmological frame, not the dual Lorentz symmetry. The essay calls the dual symmetry 'fundamentally broken at the quantum level'. (2) The essay gives its own reason for that breaking: 'the dual Lorentz action is not a canonical transformation' (eq. 12). It does not preserve [x̂, x̂̃] = iπλ², so it is not a symmetry of the essay's quantum theory. The program's ρ and its U(1) preserve ω (P2′, P3). So 2104.00802 anticipates the phrase 'symmetry breaking ... at the level of the vacuum sector', said of a non-canonical symmetry of a doubled (x, x̃) space, together with 'a choice of ground state' and the expansion as selector. It does not anticipate the program's structure, an exact ω-preserving symmetry broken by the vacuum. The reader's test of #11 §3 ('does not hold for the vacuum as the place where the breaking occurs') is too strong. (3) 'None calls that choice spontaneous', and the test's 'holds for the word spontaneous', need qualifying. Berglund, Hübsch and Minic 2022 (2212.06086 §4.3, citing 2104.00802 as [250]) call O(1,d−1)₂ 'effectively spontaneously broken' within the sector of fixed nonzero p̃. That is the same dual Lorentz group, whose orbits δ labels. It is the symmetry tied to the polarization, though not ρ, and it comes with no order parameter or potential. (4) Missed: 1606.01829 §2 (2016; also J. Phys. Conf. Ser. 804 012032 §6) names the coset over which the program's order parameter ranges: 'The space U(d)/O(d) represents the choice of inequivalent lifts η that preserve the symplectic structure and the vacuum state'. It is named as a space of choices, with no potential. The same section argues that choosing a modular polarization does not break a canonical symmetry, since 'any other modular space is related to the original one by a change of polarization, i.e., a canonical transformation'. (5) 'The one school text' that places the breaking in the vacuum: add 2212.06086 §4.3 as its restatement in 'spontaneous' terms.

#### r8-outside: Q9 (reader confidence medium)

No outside paper treats the choice of spacetime as a spontaneous vacuum selection of a phase-space polarization with an order parameter, as #11 P5 does.

- **Chouha.** Spacetime 'arises from a choice of polarization of modular (doubled) phase space. Different polarizations related by O(d,d) transformations describe the same underlying physical state'. The polarization is a choice and every choice is equally lawful; η is 'a polarization metric'.
- **Lovelady and Wheeler, and Hazboun and Wheeler.** The Lorentzian metric falls on one Lagrangian submanifold, and 'we interpret this submanifold as spacetime': a geometric selection, 'the origin of time'.
- **Yargic.** The Schrödinger and momentum limits 'require two distinct fixings of the modular gauge symmetry'.
- **Nishimura.** 'After the dual symmetry breaks spontaneously': spontaneous, but of a double Lorentz symmetry, not of Born duality.
- **Ran.** Both sectors are dynamical; nothing is selected.
- **Bolognesi.** Inflation hides the dual sector.

**Verifier (r8-outside-verify): confirmed-in-part.** Literally true: no outside paper calls the selection spontaneous or a vacuum, or gives it a potential or order parameter. But the nearest outside statement was missed. Yargic's Waterloo thesis (INSPIRE imprint 2021-08-26; supervisor Smolin, with Freidel on the committee, so school-adjacent) speculates in §2.8 on breaking a symmetry. The different gauge fixings 'might be useful for developing quantum theories that break the Born reciprocity. If the modular U(1) gauge symmetry is somehow broken, this may be used as a mechanism to fix the quantum configuration space'. A far-infrared modular scale 'would break the Born reciprocity in favor of the position variables'. η is 'the geometric construct that singles out the position and momentum spaces'. This is a speculative symmetry-breaking selection of the position polarization, with η in the role it has in #11 P2′. It bears on #11 §3's claim to the spontaneous reading as the program's own. Yargic 2020 (2002.01604, introduction) also records the view that 'polarizations are observer-dependent properties and do not carry a fundamental meaning for Nature'.

#### r9-sweep: Q9 (reader confidence high)

## Q10: Dark energy

#### r1-priority: Q10 (reader confidence high)

Dark energy is a positive cosmological constant from the curvature of the dual spacetime: 'the smallness of the cosmological constant is given by its curvature'. It is made radiatively stable by sequestering and non-commutativity, with a see-saw M_Λ ~ M²/M_P and M at the TeV scale. 'Higher order terms in λ produce various forms of dark energy'. No w(z) is predicted; the leading order is w = −1. That is compatible with #11 §10's w ≥ −1 but is not thawing quintessence. Modified dark matter is said to be 'sensitive' to Λ.

On C5, 'their collapse is our acceleration' (App. A item 9): no collapsing dual sector drives acceleration in these papers. FKGLM 2021's rescaling x → ax, x̃ → a⁻¹x̃ ('the dual dimension shrinks to zero' as a → ∞; 'a dual universe made of dual particles is accessed' as a → 0) has the reciprocal form of #11 §10's a·ã = 1 and App. A items 2, 6 and 7 (my inference), without C5's causal claim.

#### r2-dual-sm: Q10 Dark energy (reader confidence high)

Dark energy is a positive cosmological constant from the curvature of the dual spacetime. 'The curvature of the dual space is the cosmological constant in the observed spacetime' (2212.06086 §7; 2010.15610 §2; 2109.12763 §5; 2307.16712 §5), with a seesaw M_Λ M_P ∼ M_H². The equation of state is 'p = −ρ' (2212.06086 §5.3), so w = −1. Quintessence alternatives appear 'rather unnatural' (2212.06086 §7) and 'tend to induce too low a value for the Hubble constant' (§2). Two pointers go to dynamical dark energy: 'a dynamical form of dark energy [51]' (2109.12763 §5) and 'predictive power ... in particular the equation of state for the dark energy' ([285], 2212.06086 §4.8). Neither paper gives a w. 2109.01122 gives a positive Λ on a de Sitter bubble-world, also w = −1. Against #11 §10: the school's main line is a constant Λ, where the program's τ is thawing quintessence with w ≥ −1. Both lines would come under pressure from w ≠ −1 data, in different directions (my comparison). The school maps the dual space's curvature to our Λ; #11 §10's corrected dictionary maps dual curvature to negative dark radiation and dual stiff matter to Λ. The two constructions differ: Tseytlin's intensive action with Ṽ ∼ V⁻¹, against a shared lapse with a·ã = 1 (my comparison). On C5 ('Their collapse is our acceleration', App. A item 9): nothing in these papers has the dual spacetime contract or collapse. It is static and Planck-sized ('the big spacetime is balanced by a Planckian dual spacetime, and vice versa', 2212.06086 §4.4). Its degrees of freedom dominate early and freeze late (2212.06086 §4.3, §5.3), and Ṽ ∼ V⁻¹ (2109.12763 §5) parallels a·ã = 1 (App. A item 6). The acceleration comes from the dual curvature, not from a collapse.

Those fits lie at wₐ/(1 + w₀) = −3.0 to −3.8, off #11's corridor wₐ ≈ −1.5(1 + w₀) (my computation). #### r9-sweep: Q10 (reader confidence high)

At leading order dark energy is a positive cosmological constant, w = −1, from the curvature of the dual spacetime, with a seesaw M_Λ ~ M²/M_P (BHM 2019; Minic 2020 §6; BHM 2021). Here w is pressure over energy density. Beyond leading order it is time-dependent, from the 'dynamical geometry of the dual spacetime' (JKMT 2020). The sign of the deviation changed. JKMT 2022 gave 'w_eff < −1 in this framework' (bounded so that w_eff ≤ −1), a phantom against #11 §10. Hur et al. 2025 call this 'our first, naive guess'. Their adopted UV–IR relation gives 'w(a) ≥ −1 in all cases' with wₐ < 0 and 'w(a) > −1', compared favourably with DESI. No paper has a contracting or collapsing dual spacetime that drives our acceleration (C5, App. A item 9). Related passages: the cell rescaling x → ax, x̃ → a⁻¹x̃ ('the dual dimension shrinks') (FKLM 2021; Minic 2023, there with x → ax, p → a⁻¹p), which is App. A item 6's a·ã = 1; 'in the early (small) universe the dual (“dark”) degrees of freedom dominate' (BHM 2023 §4.3); and a 'dual universe made of dual particles' as a → 0 (FKLM 2021).

## Q11: Priority

#### r1-priority: Q11 (reader confidence medium)

The dual-Standard-Model form of the identification first appears publicly in the slides of Minic's talk at the 10th Mathematical Physics Meeting, Belgrade, on 9 Sep 2019 (slide PDF created 29 Aug 2019, on the conference server since 10 Sep 2019). Slide 5 reads 'Each particle of the Standard Model has a "dual" particle ... dual particles → dual Standard Model (hidden sector?)' and 'Metaparticles φ(x,x̃) → dark matter quanta correlated to visible (baryonic/Standard Model) quanta'; slide 6 equates L_m(x̃) with dark matter. That is 173 days (computed) before arXiv:2003.00318 (29 Feb 2020), the first written statement I could read ('could be interpreted', section 5; 'The proposal here is', section 6.2).

An earlier written statement may be in Freidel, Leigh and Minic, IJMPA 34, 1941004 (published 15 Oct 2019, from a February 2018 talk). It is closed access. It cites six modified-dark-matter papers, and Minic 2020 cites it for 'dark matter ... represented by metaparticles'.

What the school wrote about dark matter before 2020, dated:
- Ho, Minic and Ng 2010-2012: modified dark matter. 'Duality' there is between cold dark matter and MOND, with non-local, infinite-statistics quanta and no dual sector.
- Jejjala, Kavic and Minic 2007: asks whether dark energy and dark matter come 'in one go'.
- Chang, Minic, Sun and Takeuchi, arXiv 14 May 2016: every Standard Model field should have extended non-local excitations; the chargeless ones are dark-matter candidates, and non-locality explains null detection.
- Freidel, Leigh and Minic, JPCS 804 (talk June 2016, published 3 Apr 2017): 'implications of the metastring for the problems of dark matter' (speculation).
- Miami slides, December 2016: 'non-particle (metaparticle) excitations ⇒ (modified dark matter)'.
- Edmonds et al., arXiv 13 Sep 2017: metaparticles 'could be viewed as a model for modified dark matter quanta'.
- Edmonds et al., arXiv 30 Dec 2017: modified-dark-matter quanta 'might be modeled as metaparticles'.
- Wojanów slides, 26 Sep 2018: 'non-particle dark matter with Milgrom's scaling'.
- 'Theory of metaparticles', 27 Dec 2018: dual particles, no dark matter.
- Berglund, Hübsch and Minić, 20 and 22 May 2019: 'relates to' modified dark matter.

So the claim of Minic 2020's abstract (metaparticle quanta as dark-matter quanta) was in print in 2017, while 'the dual sector is the dark sector' appears first in September 2019.

The talk's six venues:
- Polish Society on Relativity: Wojanów, 26 Sep 2018, under the title 'Manifest quantum non-locality...'; the 2019 Szczecin programme has no Minic.
- Bangkok: 9 Jan 2019, same title; no slides posted.
- Penn State IGC@25: 27 Jun 2019, 'Manifest Quantum Non-Locality in String Theory and Quantum Gravity'; only plenary slides are posted.
- Belgrade: 9 Sep 2019; slides public.
- Miami: listed among the Miami 2019 abstracts (12-18 Dec 2019); Miami 2018 unknown; host unreachable, no archive.
- Granada (COST CA18108): 10-13 Mar 2020, after the posting; Minic is absent from the public programme.

Minic 2020 cites no source outside the school for the identification ([9], [10], [12], [89]). Everything here precedes the program's notebook (September 2026) by years.

Evidence: talk:mphys10-2019-Minic slides 5-6 (PDF pp. 24-25); timetable 09.09.2019; Last-Modified 2019-09-10; Wayback 2019-09-19 prefix; 2003.00318 section 5, section 6.2, section 7, Acknowledgements; recid:1760046 abstract and INSPIRE reference list; 1605.04361 section 5; 1709.04388 conclusion; 1801.00160 conclusion; talk:potor5-2018-Minic slide 15; talk:miami2016-Minic slide 9; recid:1590124 section 8 p. 19; 1905.08269 conclusion; 1905.09463 end; 1812.10821 section 3.2; Bangkok 2019 schedule (BKK2019HEPTH_schedule.txt); IGC@25 programme (Google Doc); Granada Indico event 2

**Verifier (r1-priority-verify): confirmed-in-part.** The substance holds. The dual-Standard-Model form first appears publicly in the Belgrade slides (talk Mon 9 Sep 2019; file Last-Modified 10 Sep 2019; PDF created 29 Aug 2019). It first appears in writing in Minic 2020 (29 Feb 2020), which cites only the school for it. The 2016-2017 statements are precursors of a different claim. Six corrections: (1) slide 5 puts the dual Standard Model forward with a question mark ('HIDDEN SECTOR?') and names metaparticles, not the duals, as the dark-matter quanta; slide 6 comes closest to 'matter on x̃ is dark matter'. (2) The INSPIRE reference list of FLM 2019 has 65 numbered references (69 entries), not 46; 46 is the number of linked records. (3) FLM 2019 was published online 15 Oct 2019 (issue dated 10 Oct), after the Belgrade talk, so it could only be an earlier written statement than Minic 2020, not an earlier public one. (4) The 2016 essay's excitations are extended non-local excitations of the same fields (NCFT-type dipoles), not dual partners. (5) Minic's Miami 2019 listing (#31) is confirmed, but its text is unavailable. (6) The 'one go' sentence of Jejjala, Kavic and Minic 2007 is verified; the same paper also speculates about 'the dark matter condensate'.

Counter-evidence: talk:mphys10-2019-Minic, slide 5 (PDF p. 24), read from the slide image: 'DUAL PARTICLES → DUAL STANDARD MODEL (HIDDEN SECTOR?)' | 1605.04361 §3: 'represent open-string-like dipole excitations' (FOUND); §5: 'Thus, those would not be “dark” and would have had to decay early on in the history of the universe' (FOUND) | recid:1760046, INSPIRE record: reference labels 1-65 in 69 entries; tools/lit.py refs prints '46 references of recid:1760046 found in INSPIRE' | 0705.4581, conclusion: 'Could this mean that the standard model matter is just a collective excitation around the dark matter condensate?' (FOUND)

#### r2-dual-sm: Q11 Priority (reader confidence medium)

My six papers all postdate Minic 2020 (29 Feb 2020; not among my papers). The earliest of them is 2010.15610, v1 posted 29 Oct 2020; I checked its v1 PDF, which holds the identification, the correlation and the dual photon. 2010.15610 presents the identification as its own step ('In this paper we consider'). It credits 'the explicit appearance of dark matter' to its [14], the Freidel–Leigh–Minic series 2014–2019 plus Minic arXiv:2003.00318, and the visible–dark correlation to MDM [18]. 2109.12763 carries an arXiv admin note of 'substantial text overlap with arXiv:2003.00318'. 2212.06086 cites [254, 255] (Freidel–Leigh–Minic, IJMPA 34 (2019) 1941004; Minic 2020) for dual particles as dark-matter candidates, and [242] (Freidel–Leigh–Minic PLB 2014) for 'the explicit appearance of dark matter'. I ran tools/lit.py text with --grep on seven arXiv papers of the series: 1307.7080, 1405.3949, 1502.08005, 1606.01829, 1706.03305, 1707.00312 and 1812.10821 ('Theory of metaparticles'). Each gave 0 matches for 'dark matter' and 0 for 'dark sector|dark energy', so these citations are to the formalism. The 2019 Berglund–Hübsch–Minic papers 1905.08269 and 1905.09463 mention dark matter only as a pointer to MDM. Ho–Minic–Ng 2010–2011 (abstracts only) propose 'a duality between cold dark matter and modified Newtonian dynamics': a different duality. No statement outside the school is cited. Unread: Freidel–Leigh–Minic IJMPA 34 (2019) 1941004 (not on arXiv; its abstract mentions metaparticles but not dark matter) and J. Phys. Conf. Ser. 804 (2017) 012032 (IOP bot check). Within what I could read, Minic 2020 stays the earliest statement, as the record says.

Evidence: 2010.15610 §1, §4 first sentence, references [14], [18]; 2010.15610 v1 PDF (29 Oct 2020); 2109.12763 arXiv API comment; 2212.06086 §4.3 [254, 255], §6.4 [242]; 1905.08269 concluding paragraph; 1905.09463 concluding paragraph; 1307.7080, 1405.3949, 1502.08005, 1606.01829, 1706.03305, 1707.00312, 1812.10821: lit.py text --grep, 0 matches; recid:1760046 abstract (INSPIRE); 1005.3537, 1105.2916 abstracts (INSPIRE)

**Verifier (r2-dual-sm-verify): confirmed-in-part.** The conclusion stands: of the papers read, Minic 2020 is the earliest statement of the identification. Three corrections. (1) A second attempt retrieved Freidel, Leigh and Minic, J. Phys. Conf. Ser. 804 (2017) 012032, from the copy INSPIRE hosts (talk at ISQS-24, Prague, 14–18 June 2016; INSPIRE imprint 2017-04-03). Its only dark-matter sentence is 'Finally, we think that there are implications of the metastring for the problems of dark matter and the origin of the standard model of particle physics or the physics of the early universe.' It has no metaparticles, no dual fields and no identification, so it does not move the priority. (2) The phrase 'a duality between cold dark matter and modified Newtonian dynamics' is in the abstract of 1005.3537 only; it is NOT FOUND in that of 1105.2916. (3) In its reading of 2010.15610 §4 the reader wrote that 'the arXiv papers of [14] contain no dark matter', but [14] includes arXiv:2003.00318, which has 28 matches. IJMPA 34 (2019) 1941004 remains unread.

#### r4-pheno: Q11 (reader confidence high)

None of the nine is the earliest statement. The earliest of them to state the identification is 2011.08852 (17 November 2020), after Minic 2020 (29 February 2020) and Berglund–Hübsch–Minic (arXiv, October 2020). The Snowmass paper credits the idea to Minic 2020 and BHM 2021 ('has been recently proposed [29, 30]'). 2202.06890 and Addazi et al. credit Minic 2020 ([11], [408]). 2011.08852 cites the modified-dark-matter papers (2010–2020) for a dark-matter sector 'sensitive to dark energy'. No statement outside the school is cited. All nine predate the program's notebook (September 2026), so the four that state the identification are prior art for C1, each in a weaker form. For dark energy, they credit the dual-curvature origin to Berglund–Hübsch–Minic 2019 (arXiv:1905.08269, 1905.09463; also 1902.08617), and the Wien-spectrum dark energy goes back to Jejjala, Kavic and Minic 2007 (arXiv:0705.4581).

#### r5-mdm: Q11 (reader confidence medium)

MDM begins on 19 May 2010 (1005.3537v1, titled 'MONDian Dark Matter'). It credits its formula to earlier work: Milgrom 1999 for the de Sitter Unruh-temperature difference √(a²+a0²) − a0 ('Milgrom has suggested in [13] ...'), Verlinde 2010 and Jacobson 1995 for the thermodynamic route, Deser–Levin 1997 for the de Sitter Unruh temperature, and Kaplinghat & Turner 2002 as the CDM account of cH0. None of the MDM papers cites an earlier statement, by anyone, that dark matter is the dual or conjugate counterpart of visible matter. Within the school, the precursors are: the first pointer from MDM to the Freidel–Leigh–Minic reformulations, 4 Jan 2016 (1601.00662), before which the 2010–2013 MDM texts never mention Freidel, metastrings or metaparticles (count 0); the metastring zero-mode sector as a model of MDM quanta, 13 Sep 2017 (1709.04388); 'metaparticles ... two entangled particles in momentum space', 30 Dec 2017 (1801.00160; the cited Freidel–Leigh–Minic papers 1707.00312 and 1706.03305 never mention dark matter); and MDM quanta as the metaparticle (dual) cousins of Standard Model fields, 29 Feb 2020 (Minic 2003.00318). All precede the program's first written statement of C1 (lab notebook v0.1, September 2026). On my papers, the priority for the identification stays with Minic 2020; the 2016–2017 MDM papers are earlier steps toward it, not statements of it.

Evidence: 1005.3537 v1 PDF and v3 text, refs. [6], [12], [13], [5]; 1601.00662 §4; 1709.04388 §5; 1801.00160 §5, refs. [57], [58]; 1707.00312 and 1706.03305 full texts (grep: dark matter, 0 matches); 2003.00318 §5; grep counts over 1005.3537, 1105.2916, 1201.2365, 1308.3252

**Verifier (r5-mdm-verify): confirmed.** No correction to the priority finding. Additions: (1) MDM has a precursor inside the school. 1201.2365 ref. [35] cites 'Previous work on toy models of infinite statistics and dark energy/dark matter': Ng 2007 and Jejjala, Kavic and Minic 2007 (arXiv:0705.4581, posted 31 May 2007). The latter already asks whether infinite statistics gives 'both dark energy and dark matter in one go', citing dark-matter scaling relations 'apparently sensitive to the vacuum parameters, such as the cosmological constant'. It is not a dual-sector identification: 'dual' occurs only as 'dual quantum and classical nature' and 'dualism'. (2) Milgrom 1999 (astro-ph/9805346, posted 28 May 1998), which MDM credits, already has T(a) − T(0) with T ∝ (a² + a0²)^{1/2}, a0 = (Λ/3)^{1/2}. Its abstract already ties a0 to cosmology through the vacuum. (3) The metaparticle paper behind 'two entangled particles' (1812.10821) was posted after 1801.00160 and does not mention dark matter. (4) 1801.00160 is listed among the MDM papers in BHM 2021's ref. [18] ('Bulg. J. Phys. 45 (2), (2018) 138–151'), yet the record nowhere mentions it (my grep: no '1801.00160', and no 'Bulg' except for a different 2024 Minic paper). The count of 0 for 'Freidel', 'metastring' and 'metaparticle' in the 2010–2013 texts is confirmed.

#### r6-foundations: Q11 (reader confidence high)

These papers do not state the identification and cite no earlier statement of it (no 'dark' in any text). As framework they all precede the program's first statement (September 2026): Born geometry, Born reciprocity as a choice of Lagrangian, and dynamical η (1307.7080, 26 July 2013); spacetime as a preferred basis broken by gravity, and dynamical η as 'our addition to Born's argument' (1405.3949, 15 May 2014); 'broken Born duality' and 'a dynamical question' (1502.08005, 27 February 2015); quantum space as a polarization, with x̃ = p/ε in quantum mechanics (1606.01829, 6 June 2016); [x, x̃] = 2πiλ² (1706.03305, 11 June 2017; 1707.00312, 2 July 2017); the metaparticle and its dual particle pair (1812.10821, 27 December 2018). For the program's own attributions, #11 §3 and P2′ cite 1502.08005; the 2013 letter and the 2014 essay are earlier statements of dynamical η and of a preferred polarization. Earlier sources FLM cite for the framework: Born (Nature 136, 1935; Proc. R. Soc. A165, 1938; Rev. Mod. Phys. 21, 1949), Snyder 1947 and Gol'fand 1960 (curved momentum space), Veneziano 1986 (Born reciprocity in string theory), Tseytlin 1990–91 (the action), Amelino-Camelia, Freidel, Kowalski-Glikman and Smolin 2011 (relative locality), and Aharonov's modular variables. None concerns dark matter.

Evidence: arXiv API v1 dates (scratch/r6-foundations/arxiv_dates.out); 1307.7080 'Conclusion' and refs. [1], [2], [4], [18]; 1405.3949 §1.1, §1.3 and refs. [3]–[5]; 1502.08005 §1, §2.1, §7 and ref. [32]

**Verifier (r6-foundations-verify): confirmed-in-part.** The dates (arXiv API, rerun) and the framework list (reference lists of 1307.7080, 1405.3949 and 1502.08005; Veneziano 1986 is 1307.7080 ref. One is the observation that gravity breaks the reciprocity symmetry (1405.3949 sec. 1.1). The other is curved momentum space (1502.08005 sec. 1). This bears on #11 sec. 3, which credits 'the idea that a preferred polarization breaks Born duality' to FLM and traces curved momentum space from Snyder.

- Minic 2020 §7 also cites the unread FLM 2019 paper, alone ([10] = IJMPA 34 (2019) 1941004), for dark matter 'represented by metaparticles'.
- FLM 2019's first public date is its publication, 2019-10-10 (print) or 2019-10-15 (online). The Julian Schwinger Centennial talk (Singapore, 7–12 February 2018) is earlier, but the text was not public before October 2019. It is closed access and was not retrieved; see 'retried'.
- BHM 2021 credits 'the explicit appearance of dark matter in the general formulation of string theory' to [14]; EMT 2020's metaparticle candidate cites [50]. Both are composite citations of the whole FLM series, FLM 2019 and Minic 2020 included, so neither dates the identification.
- Outside the 41, FLM's open-access J. Phys. Conf. Ser. 804 (2017) 012032 (online 2017-04-03) has one dark-matter sentence, a pointer with no identification.
- 1601.00662 and 1709.04388 are already in the record's findings.json as precursors; 1801.00160 is not in any record file.

Counter-evidence: 2003.00318, §7: 'dark matter correlated to visible matter and dark energy [12] and represented by metaparticles [10]' (FOUND); reference list: '[10] L. Freidel, R. G. Leigh and D. Minic, Int. J. Mod. Phys. A 34, no. 28, 1941004 (2019)' | 2010.15610, §4 opening: 'In this section we elaborate on the dual matter degrees of freedom and the explicit appearance of dark matter in the general formulation of string theory [14].' (FOUND); [14] lists PLB 2014 … IJMPA 34 (2019) 1941004 … PRD 99 (2019) 066011, and arXiv:2003.00318 | FLM, J. Phys. Conf. Ser. 804 (2017) 012032, closing remarks: 'Finally, we think that there are implications of the metastring for the problems of dark matter and the origin of the standard model of particle physics or the physics of the early universe.' (not code-checkable: read via the WebFetch-saved CC-BY PDF and pypdf; exact string match on the extracted text)


## Other: Other findings

#### r2-dual-sm: How strong the dark–visible mixing is, computed (reader confidence high)

The school does not fix the strength, and its own range spans many orders of magnitude. 2010.15610's coefficients λ⁸/L¹⁰ (vectors, scalars) and λ⁹/L¹⁰ (fermions) equal λ⁻²(λ/L)¹⁰ and λ⁻¹(λ/L)¹⁰. For the paper's 'within one or two orders of magnitude', the suppression is 1.0e-10 (L/λ = 10) to 1.0e-20 (L/λ = 100). For λ the Planck length and L the Hubble length (1.372e26 m for H₀ = 67.4) it is 10^-609.3, a reading the paper rejects. With λ = 1e-19 m, an illustration taken from 2212.06086 fn 38's bound in another context, ħc/λ = 1.97 TeV and the fermion mass mixing is 197 eV at L/λ = 10 and 1.97e-8 eV at L/λ = 100. 2212.06086's 4d coefficients λ₄²/L⁴ and λ₄³/L⁴ cannot be evaluated, since it leaves λ₄ and L 'unspecified'. Script: /home/paul/git/reinholdtsen/conjugate-matter/work/dual-sector/scratch/r2-dual-sm/scripts/mixing_scale.py; output: /home/paul/git/reinholdtsen/conjugate-matter/work/dual-sector/scratch/r2-dual-sm/mixing_scale.out.

Evidence: 2010.15610 §4 eqs. (19)–(21), §6; 2212.06086 §6.1 fn 38, §6.4 fn 43; scratch/r2-dual-sm/mixing_scale.out

**Verifier (r2-dual-sm-verify): confirmed-in-part.** An independent script reproduced every number (scratch/r2-dual-sm-verify/scripts/mixing_check.py, output mixing_check.out): c/H₀ = 1.3725e26 m; 1.0e-10; 1.0e-20; 10^-609.3; 1.973 TeV; 197.3 eV; 1.973e-8 eV. The unstated assumption is that the flux factors f^a_b in eqs. (19)–(21) equal 1, whereas 2010.15610 says only that they are 'naturally at the fundamental scale λ'. The answer is right that 2212.06086 leaves λ₄ and L 'unspecified', so its coefficients cannot be evaluated.

Counter-evidence: 2010.15610 after eq. (19): 'encode “background fluxes,” naturally at the fundamental scale \lambda from (3).' (FOUND)

#### r3-gravitized: Evolution 2022–2026 (reader confidence high)

How the statement develops across these papers:
- March 2022 (GRF essay): nothing on dark matter.
- March/June 2023 (CQG): nothing on dark matter; x̃ only as non-commuting dual coordinates.
- May 2023 (Minic talk; journal 2024): one parenthesis. Metaparticles are correlated visible/dual pairs, 'natural candidates'; the dual sector drops out in the b → ∞ decoupling limit.
- July 2024 (Hübsch–Minic): the fullest account:
  - dark fields as backgrounds of the dual momentum p̃, fuzzy through [φ, φ̃] ≠ 0;
  - an effective action in which the x̃-integration gives G_N and Λ;
  - the λ-correlation proposed as the origin of a₀ ~ cH/(2π);
  - a dual SM from the other E8, CP phases from it, and reciprocal indeterminacy of its parameters;
  - a decoupling energy M_SM ≈ 4.5×10^14 GeV, below which dark and visible matter interact only gravitationally;
  - the spacetime polarization selected by quantum gravity through extensification.
- April 2026: §3 repeated, adding the dual Higgs as Starobinsky-like inflation and the axion's dual partner as fuzzy dark matter (speculative); the quantum-pressure mechanism for a₀ and the claim that a₀ is evidence; time-varying dark energy; x 'canonically conjugate' to x̃; quantum gravity picking the spacetime basis. The decoupling scale is not repeated.
- June/July 2026 (book): not read.
- October 2026: the identification hedged ('a candidate', 'the potential to describe'); X̃ derived as the string's momentum-space coordinate; η named the polarization metric; neutrinos as portals to dark energy; nothing new on the dark sector's dynamics.
Across the period: no self-interaction, no velocity dependence, no monopoles, no localization in momentum, no spontaneous selection of the polarization.

Evidence: 2203.17137 and 2303.15645: 'dark' 0 occurrences; 2305.12593 §2; 2407.06207 §3, §5.1, §5.3, §5.4, §6; 2604.19418 §1.1, §5.1, §5.2, §7; 2610.19522 §3.2, §5

**Verifier (r3-gravitized-verify): confirmed.** 

#### r4-pheno: ω rigid or dynamical (#11 P2′) (reader confidence high)

The school's worldsheet action makes all three Born-geometry structures dynamical: 'The mutually compatible dynamical fields' ω_AB(X), η_AB(X), H_AB(X) (2011.08852 §2; 2503.20854 App. B, 'a (dynamical) Born geometry'). 2202.06890 describes the program of 'gravitization' as 'quantum mechanical structures that are traditionally fixed become dynamical'. #11 P2′ holds only ω rigid (the constancy of ℏ) and makes η emergent, crediting metastring theory only with η being dynamical. So the school goes further than P2′, making ω dynamical too. That is a difference to state where the two are compared, not a conflict with the record's attribution.

Evidence: 2011.08852, §2 after eq. (1); 2503.20854, App. B after eq. (94); §4; 2202.06890, 'Introduction and Summary'

**Verifier (r4-pheno-verify): confirmed-in-part.** The core holds: ω, η and H are 'mutually compatible dynamical fields' in 2011.08852 and 2503.20854, and 2202.06890 makes traditionally fixed structures dynamical. One piece of evidence is misread. The 2503.20854 Discussion sentence 'This raises intriguing questions about the dynamical nature of fundamental constants' follows the result on time-dependent dark energy and the UV–IR relation, and does not refer to ω or ℏ.

Counter-evidence: 2503.20854 §4: 'Infinite statistics, plus a particular form the relation between the UV and IR physics leads us to demonstrate quite generically, that the dark energy is dynamical, i.e., time-dependent, and that its effective equation-of-state parameter satisfies w(a)>-1 . This raises intriguing questions about the dynamical nature of fundamental constants' (FOUND)

#### r4-pheno: Reciprocal observed and dual scales (a·ã = 1 analog) (reader confidence medium)

The dark-energy papers pair an observed scale with a dual one reciprocally. In 2011.08852 eq. 17 and 2503.20854 eq. 16, Ẽ_UV E_IR = μ, so with E_IR = E₀/a the dual cutoff grows as Ẽ_UV = μa/E₀. 2503.20854 §3.4 uses E_IR Ẽ_IR = μ_IR and E_UV Ẽ_UV = μ_UV, from the metaparticle's p p̃ = μ. 2503.20854 §2 adds δx δx̃ ∼ λ² between our volume and the dual volume. 2212.00901 eq. 15 has LM/(2πℏ) = N^{1/4} at fixed N. My comparison: the first three are at the string scale and between cutoffs, and the papers call them assumptions ('as a first, naive, attempt, we simply assume'). #11 §10's a·ã = 1 instead follows from a rigid ω at ℏ. Only 2212.00901's relation is at ℏ, and the record's diameter note already cites it.

Evidence: 2011.08852, §4 eqs. (17)–(19); 2503.20854, §2; §3.2 eq. (16); §3.4 eq. (45); 2212.00901, eq. (15)

**Verifier (r4-pheno-verify): confirmed-in-part.** 'the first three are at the string scale' is imprecise. The cutoff relations Ẽ_UV E_IR = μ and E Ẽ = μ_IR, μ_UV are set by μ, not λ: √μ ≈ 5.23 TeV in 2011.08852's special choice, which it calls 'only a very special choice' and a parameter to fit. Only δx δx̃ ∼ λ² is at the string scale. Also, 2011.08852 presents its relation as following from 'the UV/IR correspondence'. The 'assumption' wording belongs to 2202.05266 ('an additional assumption') and 2503.20854 ('as a first, naive, attempt, we simply assume').

Counter-evidence: 2011.08852 §4: 'Due to the UV/IR correspondence between the two spacetimes, we have \tilde{E}_{UV}E_{IR}\;=\;\mu\;,' (FOUND) | 2011.08852 §4: 'However, this is only a very special choice, and in general,' (FOUND; continues 'μ is a parameter we should fit')

#### r6-foundations: Other: which structure is rigid (#11 P2′ against the metastring) (reader confidence medium)

#11 P2′: 'Only ω is rigid (symplectic rigidity ≡ constancy of ℏ). The neutral metric η is emergent', and it credits 'η dynamical' to metastring theory. The sources agree that η is dynamical but put quantum rigidity elsewhere: 'the rigidity of the quantum theory is encoded into the flatness of the polarization metric η', to be relaxed on general backgrounds (1502.08005 §1); the metastring promotes η, H and ω all to functions on phase space, keeping ω closed (§2.3); the string's symplectic form 'only depends on the neutral metric η' (§4.2), while ω is the symplectic form of the zero modes (1706.03305 §4.1). In ordinary quantum mechanics ω 'defines the Heisenberg algebra' (1606.01829), as in P2′. My reading: the program's rigid ω plays, for a point particle, the canonical role that η plays for the metastring's string modes; P2′'s 'η dynamical, as in metastring theory' is accurate for η, but the two frameworks hold different structures rigid. Whether a closed but position-dependent ω changes ℏ is not discussed in the papers (by Darboux's theorem such an ω is locally constant; my remark).

Evidence: 1502.08005 §1, §2.3, §4.2 after eq. (109); 1706.03305 §4.1; 1606.01829 introduction

**Verifier (r6-foundations-verify): confirmed.** 

#### r6-foundations: Other: modular polarizations and #11 §3's mandatory breaking (reader confidence medium)

#11 §3: '[x,p] = iℏ forbids joint locality, so any world with local physics must select a Lagrangian polarization. The magnet must point.' FLM show that the Heisenberg algebra also has polarizations that are not Lagrangian: maximal commutative subalgebras generated by modular variables, which 'commute with one another, and thus their eigenvalues can be specified precisely' (1502.08005 §7.2); 'generically, such polarizations contain a fundamental length scale', and the Schrödinger representation is 'a highly singular one' (1606.01829 abstract, §1.4). They also say modular observables evolve non-locally (1502.08005 §7.2) and that 'The concept of locality that we use in physical theories is tied to the choice of a preferred basis, the Schrödinger polarization' (1606.01829 §1.4). My reading: the premise holds for locality in the usual sense, but the alternatives are not only a Lagrangian polarization or joint locality: FLM's modular polarizations, with a finite cell of area ℏ, are a third option, which they propose as the quantum notion of space. #11 §3 does not discuss them. This bears on the program's framing, not on C1.

Evidence: 1502.08005 §7.2; 1606.01829 abstract, §1.4; #11 §3 (work/dual-sector/inputs/program.md)

**Verifier (r6-foundations-verify): confirmed-in-part.** The modular-polarization point holds. Missing: FLM do not derive a preferred polarization from [x, p] = i hbar. In ordinary quantum mechanics they call the choice 'immaterial', and the breaking appears only when 'all probes agree' on spacetime in quantum gravity (1502.08005 sec. 1, the context of the sentence #11 sec. 3 quotes). FLM's own premise therefore differs from #11 sec. 3's 'mandatory' breaking, though 1606.01829 sec. 1.4 ties locality to the Schroedinger polarization, as the answer says.

Counter-evidence: 1502.08005 sec. 1: 'In ordinary quantum mechanics we may, depending on convenience, choose a position or momentum basis of states; it is a fundamental property of quantum theory that this choice of polarization is immaterial.' (FOUND)
