## Record points: where the primary sources contradict or qualify the record

#### r1-priority record point 0

Record says: "All nine come from one group and carry one priority, Minic 2020, dated no later than its posting; two earlier items were not read, the September 2019 talk behind it, whose content is unknown, and a 2019 paper of Freidel, Leigh and Minic not on arXiv, whose abstract mentions metaparticles but not dark matter." (notes/literature-check.md section 3, 'What is published')

Source says: Belgrade talk slides, 10th Mathematical Physics Meeting, 9 Sep 2019 (www.mphys10.ipb.ac.rs/slides/Minic.pdf, server Last-Modified 10 Sep 2019), slide 5 (PDF p. 24): "1) EACH PARTICLE OF THE STANDARD MODEL HAS A "DUAL" PARTICLE ... DUAL PARTICLES → DUAL STANDARD MODEL (HIDDEN SECTOR?)" and "3) METAPARTICLES φ(x,x̃) → DARK MATTER QUANTA CORRELATED TO VISIBLE (BARYONIC/STANDARD MODEL) QUANTA"

Why (reader): The talk's content is public and known. It states the dual-Standard-Model identification on 9-10 Sep 2019, 173 days before the arXiv posting (computed). The priority date of that form therefore moves from 29 Feb 2020 to 9 Sep 2019, and is at least as early as the talk.

**Verifier: confirmed.** Re-checked independently. The slides at www.mphys10.ipb.ac.rs/slides/Minic.pdf have server Last-Modified 10 Sep 2019 14:52 GMT and PDF Creation/ModDate 29 Aug 2019; the file is linearized, with no incremental update. They state the identification. Slide 5: 'EACH PARTICLE OF THE STANDARD MODEL HAS A "DUAL" PARTICLE ... DUAL STANDARD MODEL (HIDDEN SECTOR?)' and 'METAPARTICLES φ(x,x̃) → DARK MATTER QUANTA CORRELATED TO VISIBLE (BARYONIC/STANDARD MODEL) QUANTA'. Slide 6: L_m(x̃) ≈ L̃_m(x), braced 'DARK MATTER SENSITIVE TO Λ AND L_m(x)'. The timetable (created 3 Sep 2019) puts the talk on Mon 9 Sep 2019, 11:15-12:00, and the abstract (Wayback, 25 Aug 2019) announces 'a new picture of dark matter and dark energy'. The only 2019 archive copy (19 Sep 2019) is the first 1,048,576 bytes, byte-identical to today's file. It holds pages 1-7, including 2′B's 'METAPARTICLES ⇒ (DARK MATTER & DARK ENERGY)', but not pages 24-25, so slides 5-6 are dated by the file's own dates. The record's 'whose content is unknown' is out of date; 'dated no later than its posting' stays true. The dual-SM form is public no later than 9-10 Sep 2019, 173 days (computed) before the posting, in wording as hedged as the paper's.

#### r1-priority record point 1

Record says: "Minic and coauthors have identified dark matter with the degrees of freedom dual to visible matter in Born-reciprocal (metastring) string theory since 2020 (Minic 2020; Berglund, Hübsch and Minić 2021; ...)" (root CLAUDE.md, 'Since the handoff', 2026-11-02 entry)

Source says: Edmonds, Farrah, Minic, Ng, Takeuchi, arXiv:1709.04388 (13 Sep 2017), conclusion: "Roughly, the zero mode sector looks like two entangled particles, and it could be viewed as a model for modified dark matter quanta discussed in this review". Chang, Minic, Sun, Takeuchi, arXiv:1605.04361 (14 May 2016), section 5: "Universality of gravity also suggests that all quantum fields, including those in the SM, should have extended non-local excitations of the type discussed here, in addition to the standard particle-like excitations."

Why (reader): 'Since 2020' understates. The dual-Standard-Model form was public in September 2019. The claim the record quotes from Minic 2020's abstract, metaparticle quanta as dark-matter quanta, was in print in September 2017. A non-local partner for every Standard Model field, with the chargeless ones as dark matter, was in print in May 2016. The record's count of nine papers starts with Minic 2020 and misses these earlier school statements.

**Verifier: confirmed-in-part.** 'Since 2020' is understated by the Belgrade talk (public 9-10 Sep 2019) and accurate for written papers: Minic 2020 is the first written statement read, and FLM 2019 (published 15 Oct 2019) is unread. The 2017 statements (1709.04388, 1801.00160) are verified, but they make metaparticles a model for modified-dark-matter quanta, not 'the degrees of freedom dual to visible matter'. The 2016 essay (1605.04361) concerns extended non-local excitations of the same fields, NCFT-type dipoles ('represent open-string-like dipole excitations'), whose charged members 'would not be “dark”'. They are not dual partners; the reader's 'non-local partner' is its own gloss. The point about the count of nine holds only on the record's own reading that the Minic 2020 abstract sentence states the identification (flag 64). On that reading 1709.04388 states the same thing in 2017.

#### r1-priority record point 2

Record says: "... and a 2019 paper of Freidel, Leigh and Minic not on arXiv, whose abstract mentions metaparticles but not dark matter." (notes/literature-check.md section 3)

Source says: Minic 2020, arXiv:2003.00318, section 7: "some of the implications for the real world are dark energy as curvature of the dual spacetime [11], and dark matter correlated to visible matter and dark energy [12] and represented by metaparticles [10]." Here [10] is Freidel, Leigh and Minic, Int. J. Mod. Phys. A 34, 1941004 (2019). INSPIRE's reference list for that paper (recid:1760046) includes Ho, Minic and Ng 2010, 2011 and 2012 and Edmonds et al. 2013, 2016 and 2017.

Why (reader): Describing that paper by its abstract probably understates it (my inference). It cites six modified-dark-matter papers, and Minic 2020 cites it for dark matter represented by metaparticles. It came from a February 2018 talk and was published 15 Oct 2019, before the arXiv posting. Its text is closed access, so whether it states the dual-sector form is unknown. This qualifies the record; it does not settle the question.

**Verifier: confirmed-in-part.** The record's description is literally accurate. Minic 2020 §7 does cite the paper for 'dark matter ... represented by metaparticles [10]': [10] is IJMPA 34, 1941004, verified in the reference list. Its INSPIRE reference list includes the six MDM papers (refs 54-59: Ho-Minic-Ng 2010, 2011, 2012; Edmonds et al. 2014, 2017, 2017), Grosse-Wulkenhaar (62), Kaloper-Padilla (63) and 'The theory of metaparticles, to appear' (64). Correction to the reader: the list has 65 numbered references (69 entries), of which 46 are linked INSPIRE records, not '46 references'. My second retrieval attempt also failed (closed access; the publisher returned 403), so the content remains unknown. It was published online 15 Oct 2019, after the Belgrade talk.

#### r1-priority record point 3

Record says: "Minic and coauthors identify dark matter with the degrees of freedom dual to visible matter in Born-reciprocal (metastring) string theory. Minic 2020 (arXiv:2003.00318): "metaparticle quanta (the zero modes of the metastring) as the natural quanta of dark matter in this approach"." (review/wiki-build-flags.md, item 64)

Source says: Minic 2020, section 4.3: "the metaparticle looks like two particles that are entangled through a Berry phase-like"; section 5: "the effective action for Standard Model matter Lagrangian ( L_{m} ) and their duals (that could be interpreted as dark matter Lagrangian L_{dm} )"; section 6.2: "The proposal here is that the dual sector (as already indicated in the previous section) should be interpreted as the dark matter sector"

Why (reader): The abstract phrase the flag quotes makes the metaparticle, a particle and its dual entangled, dark matter's quantum. That is a different and earlier claim, made in 2017, than 'the degrees of freedom dual to visible matter are dark matter'. The dual-sector identification is in section 5 and section 6.2 and is worded as a proposal. The flag's quotation does not carry the claim it is attached to; this is a qualification.

**Verifier: confirmed.** The abstract sentence makes metaparticle quanta dark matter's quanta. §4.3 describes the metaparticle as 'two particles that are entangled', and in this framework every Standard Model particle is a metaparticle (slide 3: 'ANY STANDARD MODEL PARTICLE → METAPARTICLE'). So the sentence does not say that the dual half is the dark matter, and 1709.04388 made the same claim in 2017. The dual-sector identification is in §5 (eq. 49, 'could be interpreted as dark matter Lagrangian') and §6.2 ('The proposal here is that the dual sector ... should be interpreted as the dark matter sector'). The flag's second quotation does carry the claim: Berglund, Hübsch and Minić 2021 (2010.15610 abstract), 'dark matter, on the other hand, comes from the degrees of freedom dual to the visible matter' (FOUND). Only the Minic 2020 quotation needs replacing, by §6.2.

#### r1-priority record point 4

(3) [x̂^a, x̃̂_b] = 2πiλ²δ, "where \lambda denotes the fundamental length scale, such as the Planck scale"

Why (reader): In the same authors' 2019 usage the phrase names the T-dual (left-minus-right) coordinate space, whose non-commutation with x is at λ². It is not momentum at ħ, so the phrase alone does not support 'the momentum side'. Minic 2020 section 2 does define x̃ = p/ε at ħ = λε in quantum mechanics, so the momentum reading has other support. But the dark-matter sections use the string-scale x̃.

**Verifier: confirmed-in-part.** The phrase names x̃ = x_L − x_R, conjugate to x at the string scale. That holds in the 2019 essay (footnote 4) and in the 2021 paper the record cites: 2010.15610 §2 says 𝕏 'combine the sum ( x^{a} ) and the difference ... of the left- and right-moving chiral bosons on the string', with 'doubled “phase-space” operators' and [x̂, x̃̂] = 2πiλ² (FOUND). So x̃ is not the momentum p at ħ, and the phrase alone cannot show 'the momentum side' in the program's sense. But 'canonically conjugate' is meant literally: the authors call (x̂/λ, x̃̂/λ) phase-space operators (their quotation marks) and cite Born, Heisenberg and Jordan for the commutator. The record's sentence also rests on foundations that do say momentum. FLM 2017: 'momentum space is just another Lagrangian submanifold'; η 'labels its decomposition into space and momentum'; 'spacetime and energy-momentum space are put on equal footing in a unified phase space formulation'. Minic 2020 §4: 𝕏^A ≡ (X^μ/λ, P_μ/ε) on phase space. The citation of BHM 2021 is weak support; the sentence itself stands.

**Verifier: confirmed-in-part.** FKGLM 2021 (v1, 1 Apr 2021; the passages are in v1) proposes that 'the expansion of the Universe can act as a mechanism that selects the decoupling frame'. **Verifier: confirmed.** Confirmed, and stronger than the reader states. In 2010.15610 §2 the phrase follows eq. (3), [x̂^a, x̃̂_b] = 2πiλ²δ, with x̃ the difference of the left- and right-movers. It first appears in 2019, in the abstract of 1905.08269 ('the curvature of the canonically conjugate dual space provides for a positive cosmological constant'). 1905.09463 defines it outright: 'the canonically conjugate dual space, spanned by the difference of the left- and right-movers x̃_a=x_L−x_R, is often completely omitted' (lit.py quote: FOUND). The phrase thus names the winding space, not the momentum side. #### r2-dual-sm record point 2

Read together (my inference), the school's later wording places its dark gauge sector in the electric–magnetic dual factor, which contests the verifier's conclusion. So does §2's summary. The phrase '(and its dual Standard Model of the dark sector, coming from the other E8 …)' in 2307.16712 §4.3 is in tension with this and could be recorded, but it does not put the dark sector in the electric–magnetic factor. Part (b) is confirmed: the review adds 'the Born geometry of the metastring formalism, besides being inherently T-dual, also involves S-duality' and footnote 24 on S-duality patching, neither in 2111.14205, so 'repeats the passage' understates the review for flag 67. Part (c) holds only on a strict reading: the record's same paragraph names the correlation term, so 'no … couplings' must mean coupling constants.

**Verifier: confirmed.** A qualification, not an error. #### r3-gravitized record point 0

Record says: notes/literature-check.md §3, 'What differs': 'All three judges for C1 read this as predicting couplings between dark and visible matter beyond gravity' and 'The scope judge answers that the school still expects a detection, through the entanglement, and states no permanent null.' Why (reader): This qualifies the record, from a paper its own finding cites.
- The non-gravitational term exists, but 2407.06207 puts it above a decoupling energy of about 4.5×10^14 GeV (4.48×10^14 GeV from the paper's definition M_CH^{1/14} M_P^{13/14} and its Table 1 inputs; executed, scratch/r3-gravitized/scripts/msm_check.out).
- Below that energy the sectors interact 'only gravitationally', which at laboratory energies is #11's strict-P4 situation.

The only observable named for the correlation is astrophysical: Milgrom's scaling and a₀ ~ cH/(2π) (2407.06207 §3.4; 2604.19418 §5.1, which calls the observed acceleration scale 'evidence'). 'Expects it to be detectable' holds for these papers only in that astrophysical sense.
- 'States no permanent null' is literally true: no paper uses the words.
- The decoupling statement appears only in 2407.06207: not in 2604.19418, 2610.19522 or 2307.16712 v2 (which names Vafa only in its references). The LHEP 2021 paper it cites (arXiv:2010.15610) has the Berry-phase-like [[A, Ã]] term but no decoupling energy. Neither later paper retracts it.

The verifier recomputed 4.484×10^14 GeV; the same paragraph also calls M_SM a 'would-be unification scale ... ∼10^{15-16} GeV'. The statement is absent from 2604.19418, 2610.19522 and 2307.16712 v2 (verifier's greps).
This validly qualifies findings.json's difference (2) as applied to 2407.06207. In that paper the non-gravitational term sits above M_SM, and the only dark-sector observable named is astrophysical (a₀).
It does not refute the note's account of the 2020–2021 papers. LHEP 2021, which 2407.06207 cites for this very term, says the dual photon's correlation 'is finite even in the limit of zero momenta (deep infrared)' and that such correlations 'might be found in correlated events in the accelerators'. It also says the correlation 'becomes invisible in effective field theory' (all FOUND in 2010.15610).
The school's papers disagree with one another on low-energy coupling, and the record should state both positions.

#### r3-gravitized record point 1

Record says: wiki/summary.md line 34: 'Its core is published (Minic and coauthors, 2020), their sector correlated with ours; neither form was found.' review/wiki-build-flags.md item 64: 'Their dual sector is correlated with ours; #11's couples by gravity alone under strict P4 (row 7)'. Root CLAUDE.md, 2026-11-02 entry: 'Their dual sector is correlated with ours. The program's couples by gravity alone (P4 strict, row 7 ...)'.

Source says: 2407.06207 §5.3: 'below which the dark and visible matter interact as required, only gravitationally.' 2407.06207 §3.3: 'However, the visible \phi and invisible (dark matter) {\tilde{\phi}} do not commute. Therefore, one could say that dark matter is fuzzy from the point of view of classical spacetime.' 2604.19418 §5.1: 'visible and dark matter degrees of freedom are correlated (via the minimal length \lambda ) which might serve as the physical origin (arising from fuzzy dark matter and its correlation to visible matter) of the so-called Milgrom’s scaling'.

Why (reader): This qualifies the contrast the summary draws. In these papers 'correlated' means three things:
- the dark field does not commute with its visible partner, at order λ;
- each particle is paired with its dual in one metaparticle ('rigid length-ℓ_s strings that correlate each Standard Model particle with its dual', 2407.06207 §5.1);
- a Berry-phase-like mixing term whose mass scale is a decoupling energy.
Below ~10^14 GeV the 2024 restatement has gravity-only interaction, like #11's P4. What remains as a difference is the non-commutative pairing, the dual SM's contribution to visible CP phases, and the claimed galaxy-scale correlation at a₀, which #11 does not have (grep: no Milgrom, MOND or a₀ in #11, #1 or the literature-check note). It is not a non-gravitational force at accessible energies. A reader of 'correlated with ours … by gravity alone' would infer a difference in forces that the 2024 restatement excludes below about 4.5×10^14 GeV.

**Verifier: confirmed-in-part.** The qualification is real and the record lacks it: one 2024 restatement (Hübsch and Minic 2024, §5.3) has gravity-only interaction below M_SM. But the contrast is not refuted, for three reasons.
(1) 'Correlated' is the school's own word, used in these same papers for a relation it claims at galactic scales: 'visible and dark matter degrees of freedom are correlated (via the minimal length λ)' (2407.06207 §3.4; 2604.19418 §5.1). 2604.19418 §5.1 calls the observed a₀ 'evidence for ... the correlation of dual fields of fuzzy dark matter to the observed Standard Model fields'.
(2) The source of the mixing term, LHEP 2021, keeps a correlation in the deep infrared and at accelerators.
(3) 2407.06207 §5.4 itself lets the dual SM contribute CP-violating phases to visible mixing.
The summary's wording is faithful to the school. What it lacks is the 2024 decoupling sentence, and the fact that the school's papers do not agree on whether low-energy coupling is gravitational only.

- The doubled phase space is right for the metaparticle. Executed: the brackets of eq. 3.15 have rank 4 per dimension, so x̃ is not a function of x and p (scratch/r3-gravitized/scripts/bracket_rank.out).
- But the same §3 also uses x̃ for the rescaled canonical momentum of one phase space (x̃ ≡ p/ε, [x, x̃] = i at ħ = λε), which is the program's (x, p). It moves between the two uses without a separating statement, and so does 2604.19418 §5.1 (eqs. 5.4–5.5).
- The paper does not fix the scale at the string length. λ is 'a contextuality parameter', set to ℓ_s, l_P or an effective l_eff in different places, with normalizations iλ², 2πiℓ_s² and πα′ in this one paper (iπλ² in Minic 2023; 2πiλ² in 2610.19522 eq. 3.3). 'At the string scale' is one of the paper's settings, not a statement it makes.
- The dark fields are backgrounds of the dual momentum p̃ ('p\to p+\phi and \tilde{p}\to\tilde{p}+\tilde{\phi}', §3.3). The record does not mention this, and it bears on how the school's 'dual' compares with #11's p-polarized χ.

**Verifier: confirmed-in-part.** Confirmed:
- the metaparticle phase space is doubled (rank 4, derived here from the paper's Lagrangian);
- the same §3 also uses x̃ ≡ p/ε on one phase space;
- the dark fields are backgrounds of p̃ (§3.3), which the record omits.
Not confirmed: the reader's ''At the string scale' is one of the paper's settings, not a statement it makes'. For the dark-matter setting the paper does state the string scale. §3.5: 'every Standard Model field φ(x) is doubled as φ(x,x̃) and φ̃(x,x̃), with doubled and non-commutative arguments [x^a, x̃_b] = iℓ_s²δ'. §5.1: the zero modes 'are rigid length-ℓ_s strings that correlate each Standard Model particle with its dual'. λ is contextual in §3.3, and l_P (§3.2, §4.3) and l_eff (§3.5) appear elsewhere. The record's pairing of 'string scale' with §3.3's iλ² is loose but defensible.
Both the record and the reader omit that the papers define x̃ = x_L − x_R, the closed string's T-dual coordinate (2407.06207 eq. 3.11; 2604.19418 eq. 5.10; 2610.19522 eq. 3.7).

#### r3-gravitized record point 3

Record says: notes/literature-check.md §3, 'What differs', on the vacuum: 'The check found no such mechanism in the school's papers, where the classical spacetime label "corresponds to a choice" of polarization.' Also review/literature-check/findings.json, C1 findings for 2407.06207 and 2604.19418, 'difference' (1): 'reciprocity is built into the formulation, not an exact symmetry broken spontaneously by a vacuum-selected polarization with η as order parameter (#11 P2′, P5)'.

Why (reader): This qualifies the record.

- But the papers say more than 'corresponds to a choice'. The spacetime polarization is selected by quantum gravity as the emergent pointer basis, through extensification at large N, and gravitized quantum theory makes the polarization dynamical.
- In October 2026, η is named 'the polarization (or locality) metric' that distinguishes spacetime-like from dual-spacetime-like directions, and curving η is 'gravitizing the quantum'. This is the school's statement closest to #11 P2′'s η as order parameter. It postdates September 2026 and cites FLM 2014 for curved η. Whether FLM's earlier papers already call η a polarization metric was not checked (lead).

**Verifier: confirmed.** The source passages are verified (lit.py FOUND for the extensification, pointer-basis, 'picks the natural basis' and η 'polarization (or locality) metric' passages). 'No spontaneous mechanism' stands, and the papers do say more than 'corresponds to a choice'.
One point the reader missed: 2610.19522 §3.2 has #11 P2′'s formula. It defines J = η⁻¹H, so η = HJ, with (η, J) 'a chiral structure' credited to FLM 2014 [63], arXiv:1307.7080, and it writes 'η = ωK, with K² = 1, so K is the real structure'. The structure predates the program; only its spontaneous choice is absent.

#### r4-pheno record point 0

(2202.06890, paragraph 'Observations'), where [45] is Ho, Minic, Ng, 'Cold Dark Matter with MOND Scaling', PLB 693 (2010) and [46] is Edmonds, Farrah, Minic, Ng, Takeuchi, 'Modified Dark Matter: Relating Dark Energy, Dark Matter and Baryonic Matter', IJMPD 27 (2017). The abstract of [46] (arXiv:1709.04388): "Such a critical acceleration is an effective phenomenological manifestation of MDM, and it is found in correlations between dark matter and baryonic matter in galaxy rotation curves." The Snowmass paper (2202.05104, 'Constraints on the nature of dark matter') likewise: "Various astrophysical observations point to intriguing correlations between dark matter, dark energy, and visible matter on the galactic, and galaxy cluster scales [8, 9]."

Why (reader): This qualifies the record. As its references show, the quoted sentence refers to the observed MOND-like scaling of halos with baryons and Λ that the modified-dark-matter model fits, a correlation between distributions. The paper writes no interaction term and no coupling strength, and nothing that makes the correlation hold 'by construction'. In the Snowmass paper the correlations are observations the dual-SM picture is meant to explain ('we would like to understand whether…'). For these two sources, 'correlated by construction' and 'couplings beyond gravity' are the record's and the judges' readings, not the papers' words. The record's other sources for the coupling (Berglund–Hübsch–Minic 2023 Symmetry, Minic 2020, BHM 2021) are not among my papers, and this does not test them.

**Verifier: confirmed-in-part.** Right for 2202.06890's sentence. Its sources [45, 46] are modified-dark-matter papers, and [46]'s full text (1709.04388 §2) defines the correlation as the dark-matter mass profile 'tied to the baryonic mass profile' through a₀ (FOUND); the paper writes no coupling term. Two qualifications. (i) [45] itself speculates beyond gravity: '(2) The couplings of MONDian dark matter to the Standard Model particles could be nonstandard, and perhaps even of a spin-orbit type' (1005.3537, FOUND). So the reader's 'For these two sources, … couplings beyond gravity are the record's and the judges' readings, not the papers' words' is true of 2202.06890's text but not of its source. (ii) The record's 'by construction' has a direct source in the school, outside the reader's set. Berglund, Hübsch and Minic 2021 (arXiv:2010.15610) write 'the known Standard Model fields and their dark matter duals are dynamically correlated' (abstract). Their coupling (λ⁸/L¹⁰)[φ, φ̃] is 'as predicted by the doubled/non-commutative set-up, and provides an explicit correlation between the dark matter sector and a visible sector' (§5), and 'This correlation becomes invisible in effective field theory, and it vanishes as either λ→0 or L→∞' (§4; all FOUND). The record already cites BHM 2021 for the limit. It should rest 'by construction' on BHM 2021's coupling term, not on 2202.06890's parenthesis, and should say the term is suppressed by λ⁸/L¹⁰ and invisible in effective field theory.

#### r4-pheno record point 1

Record says: "In the literature… Related [S]: … Hur et al. 2025, "In our model, dark energy stems from the curvature of the dual spacetime"; … None has a collapsing partner driving our present acceleration." (notes/literature-check.md §6, C5, 'Related')

Source says: "The volume of spacetime x depends on a , and if we convert (via the “uncertainty” relation implied by the fundamental non-commutativity of x and \tilde{x} , \delta x\delta\tilde{x}\sim\lambda^{2} ) the volume of spacetime x to the volume of spacetime \tilde{x} , and this in turn, via the usual Heisenberg uncertainty relation to the energy in the dual spacetime \tilde{x} , we obtain the above equation" and "because the covariant spacetime labels x and \tilde{x} do not commute, they are not independent and thus, the change of the curvature in \tilde{x} , which is the origin of \Lambda , would, in principle, imply that \Lambda does change with the volume of x , of alternatively, with the scale factor a of the x spacetime, as well." (2503.20854 §2; both FOUND)

Why (reader): The record's one-line summary understates the paper. Hur et al. tie the dual spacetime's size reciprocally to ours (δx δx̃ ∼ λ²) and make our Λ evolve with our scale factor because of it. In their second model the dual-energy band closes at a future a (1.59–2.26 at the DESI best fits, my computation), where the dark energy vanishes. 'None has a collapsing partner' still holds: the paper never says the dual space contracts or collapses, gives it no dynamics of its own ('only a motivation'), and works at the string scale λ² rather than ℏ. Still, this is the nearest published element in my papers to #11 §10's a·ã = 1 setting our dark energy, and it deserves a sentence where C5's related work is listed. The same paper also bears directly on #11 §13 row 4: its second model has w ≥ −1 and wₐ < 0 and reaches DESI DR2's best fits. The root CLAUDE.md records it only as a lead that 'bears on reading DESI's CPL quadrant against strict w ≥ −1'.

**Verifier: confirmed-in-part.** The understatement in the literature-check note is real. The reader's quotations (δx δx̃ ∼ λ²; Λ changing with our scale factor because x and x̃ do not commute) are verbatim, and my code reproduces the band's future closing (a = 1.589–2.262). 'None has a collapsing partner' still holds. But the claim that the record has the paper 'only as a lead' is incomplete. papers/phase-space-diameter.md §7 already states: 'A dual-spacetime model with ultraviolet–infrared mixing, in the Born-geometry lineage, gives a dark energy that varies in time with w > −1, which its authors find compatible with DESI [S: Hur, Jejjala, Kavic, Minic and Takeuchi 2025]', and wiki/gravity/spin-2-fields/cosmological-constant-problem.md repeats it. That wording has its own gap (fourth item).

#### r5-mdm record point 0

Record says: review/literature-check/findings.json, C1 finding for 2005.08927, field 'difference': "Its dark matter knows about the baryons through a0, a non-gravitational correlation against strict P4."

**Verifier: confirmed-in-part.** The reader is right that 'non-gravitational' is the record's word, not the essay's. My count of 'non-gravit' is 0. Where the essay names MDM's channel it says 'In MDM, the CDM quanta is informed of the cosmological constant Λ via gravitational thermodynamics' (FOUND). Its abstract speaks of 'a fundamental acceleration scale governing the observed gravitational physics' (FOUND). The record's word is presumably an inference from refs [51], [52] (BHM 2019; Minic 2020), where the string-side terms are non-EFT. The record's 'proposes metaparticles ... as dark matter' is also stronger than the essay's 'Let us speculate ... A possible candidate'. But the reader's counter-reading, that the essay 'prefers for now its emergence within gravity-only ΛCDM', is too strong too. The Occam preference concerns galaxies. It is followed at once by 'However, what if the same acceleration scale appeared in structures at vastly larger length-scales as well?'. The cluster result is called 'difficult to imagine' as a memory of the galaxies' a0, and the abstract concludes the data 'strongly supports the existence of a fundamental acceleration scale'. All of these are in v1 (18 May 2020).

#### r5-mdm record point 1

Record says: review/literature-check/findings.json, the C1 findings' 'difference' field (for example the finding for 2407.06207): "(2) Coupling: the dual (dark) sector is 'correlated'/'entangled' with visible matter through non-gravitational terms and is tied to modified dark matter's acceleration-scale correlation with baryons; the school expects it to be detectable."

(18) does not modify General Relativity (GR) since the derivation of the right-hand-side is based on GR." and the information on cH0 "is conveyed to the dark-matter via the temperature of the de Sitter horizon"; "This choice is, of course, arbitrary and a case of reverse engineering." (1601.00662 §1.3); "how strongly coupled is MDM to baryonic matter and how does MDM self-interact?" (1602.00055 §1); "Whether such a dark matter profile can be realized dynamically will depend on the detailed nature of the MDM quanta and their interactions with ordinary matter." (1308.3252 §II); "with the gravitational potential providing a link between the baryonic and dark matter" (2405.08744). The tie is made on the string side: "Such a correlation between visible and dark matter involving an IR scale (in this case, the Hubble scale) has been observed in astronomical data and has been studied in the context of modified dark matter in [18]." (2010.15610 §3).

Why (reader): This qualifies the record. The non-gravitational terms exist, but in the string-side papers: Minic 2020's Berry-phase-like term and Berglund–Hübsch–Minić 2021's mixing term ∝ λ⁸/L¹⁰. The MDM correlation they are tied to has no interaction term in its own papers. It is an observed regularity plus a reverse-engineered, Λ-dependent mass profile in unmodified GR, with the coupling to baryons an open question; in the 2024 fuzzy-dark-matter account the link is gravitational. The tie between the two is the school's proposal of 2020–2021 (Minic 2020; BHM 2021; Edmonds, Minic and Takeuchi 2021), not a result of MDM. The record's sentence can be read as if MDM's correlation were evidence for the non-gravitational terms; on MDM's own papers it is not.

**Verifier: confirmed-in-part.** As a description of the string-side papers the record is accurate. Minic 2020 has the Berry-phase-like term α′A dÃ/dt and BHM 2021 the mixing term ∝ λ⁸/L¹⁰, and both tie them to MDM (their refs [12] and [18], verified). The reader is right that MDM's own papers write no interaction between dark matter and baryons. My count of 'coupl' and 'interact' finds only open questions, plus 1201.2365's matrix-model g V(𝕄) O_SM, which is unconnected to the correlation. The reader is also right that the 2024 fuzzy-dark-matter essay links baryons and dark matter through the gravitational potential. But the qualification goes too far when it implies MDM treats the correlation as gravity-only. 1709.04388 §1 and 1801.00160 §3 say the data 'strongly suggests that DM may not simply be extra, independent degrees of freedom'. 1709.04388 §1 says the dependence 'has to go beyond the classical Einstein theory and the usual local effective quantum field theory', which anticipates BHM's 'invisible in effective field theory'. So 'on MDM's own papers it is not [evidence]' holds for an interaction term, not for how the MDM authors read their data. BHM also add 'with a particular double scaling, this term can be finite!'.

#### r5-mdm record point 2

Record says: notes/literature-check.md §3, 'What differs', the coupling: "All three judges for C1 read this as predicting couplings between dark and visible matter beyond gravity: Minic 2020 expects dark quanta to be detectable through a Berry-phase-like entanglement with visible matter, and Berglund, Hübsch and Minić 2021 a dual photon correlated with ours, correlated events at accelerators and a dark–visible correlation at the Hubble scale, which they tie to modified dark matter."

Source says: 2005.08927: "A correlation between the total dark matter and total baryonic matter in a region surrounding a galaxy is to be expected in the" ΛCDM scenario, and "At this point in time, Occam's razor would favor the first option"; 2009.12915 §V: "The simplest one is, of course, that it is emergent in the standard" ΛCDM model; 1709.04388 §1: "Nevertheless, it may be possible to reconcile this result with CDM models".

Why (reader): This qualifies the record. The Hubble-scale dark–visible correlation that BHM tie to MDM is, by the MDM authors' own account, possibly reproduced by gravity-only ΛCDM. So the observable the school offers does not by itself separate a coupling beyond gravity from the program's gravity-only sector (#11 P4 strict, §13 row 7). The judges' reading holds for the string-side mixing and entanglement terms, not for the MDM correlation as evidence. This bears on the record's open question of whether observation can tell the two dark sectors apart. The discriminators the school proposes are a0 in colliding clusters (2005.08927), a redshift dependence of a0 (2405.08744), an acceleration floor in galaxies but not clusters (1801.00160) and a central turnover of the MDM density (1308.3252).

**Verifier: confirmed-in-part.** The record accurately reports the judges' reading of the string-side papers. The same paragraph already records the round-1 verifier's narrower reading, and the record calls the observational question open. The reader's point is documented: the MDM authors allow a ΛCDM origin of the correlation (1709.04388 §1; 1801.00160 §2; 2005.08927; 2009.12915 §V). But the same authors also call Kaplinghat–Turner's CDM route 'more of a numerical accident' and demand knowledge of cH0 'via some fundamental principle and not by accident' (1601.00662 §1.3). In 2018 they argue that a cluster-scale relation 'should have significance beyond coincidences of galactic dynamics within a DM halo composed of WIMPs' (1801.00160 §2). The 2020 abstract says the data 'strongly supports the existence of a fundamental acceleration scale'. Two of the four discriminators the reader lists do not separate the school's tie from gravity-only dark matter by themselves. A redshift change of a0 is predicted by 'CDM simulations' too (2405.08744). The galactic acceleration floor is also reported from 'ΛCDM numerical simulations (Frenk)' (1801.00160 §5); only its absence in clusters is MDM-specific. The colliding-cluster test (2005.08927) is the one the authors frame as separating an emergent a0 from a fundamental one.

**Verifier: confirmed.** Confirmed. 2405.08744 has no 'dual', 'metaparticle', 'metastring', 'Born', 'non-commut', 'MDM' or 'modified dark matter' (my counts: 0 each). #### r6-foundations record point 0

B 730, 302 (2014): "so we will refer to Y as coordinates in momentum space")." Source says: 1706.03305 §4, eq. (69): "The proper interpretation is that (p,x) and (\tilde{p},\tilde{x}-\pi\alpha^{\prime}p) are canonically conjugate pairs, and in particular, we should have [\hat{x},\hat{\tilde{x}}-\pi\alpha^{\prime}\hat{p}]=0 ." 1706.03305 end of §3: "the operator that is canonically conjugate to \hat{\tilde{p}} is not \hat{\tilde{x}} , but instead \hat{\tilde{q}}:=\hat{\tilde{x}}-\pi\alpha^{\prime}\hat{p} . Why (reader): Contradicts the wording, qualifies the substance. In the school's string papers the canonical conjugate of x is the ordinary momentum p ([x̂, p̂] = iℏ, 1706.03305 eq. 73). The letter's 'momentum space coordinate' Y is named for its period around the string, which is the momentum. Y is the T-dual field: its σ-derivative is the momentum density, and Y = X̃/α′ up to a function of τ, X̃ being 1706.03305's dual field (checked in scratch/r6-foundations/dual_field.py). Y's zero mode x̃ is a further variable: x̃ = q̃ + πα′p, with q̃ conjugate to the winding p̃ and commuting with x, so [x, x̃] = iπℏα′ (units.py). x̃ is momentum-like: it contains πα′ times p, in Y's momentum units [x, Y₀] = iπℏ, and FLM read (x, x̃) as a Heisenberg pair and the doubled space as a phase space. But it is not the canonical conjugate of x, and it is not the program's p, which stays a separate variable in these papers. Only FLM's paper on ordinary quantum mechanics, 1606.01829 §1.1, sets x̃ ≡ p/ε, the momentum of the same particle. Date: the letter was first posted 26 July 2013 (arXiv v1); 2014 is its journal year.

**Verifier: confirmed-in-part.** Record wording checked: flag 64 and sec. 3 say this. The reader is right on three points. The cited sentence names Y for its period (p = loop integral of dY). FLM's canonically conjugate pairs are (p, x) and (pt, xt - pi alpha' p) (1706.03305 eq. 69; also 1502.08005 sec. 7.4, 'the momenta dual to x (resp. xt)', and 1707.00312 sec. 2, 'the conjugate operators P_A'). And xt is independent of p. But the reader's 'it is not the canonical conjugate of x' omits 1706.03305 sec. 4.2: 'xt/{2 pi lambda^2} plays the role of the variable conjugate to x', on the reduced (x, xt) space that the topological coupling makes 'a phase space'. It also omits that in 1307.7080 Y enters as the loop-space Fourier variable of X (d_sigma Y is X's momentum density). Flag 64 is therefore right for the reduced (x, xt) space, in a paper it does not cite. It is wrong for the full zero-mode phase space, where x's canonical partner is p, and the program's p stays a separate variable. My computation: hbar xt/(2 pi lambda^2) = xt/(pi alpha') = p + qt/(pi alpha'), with [x, qt] = 0, so xt's conjugacy to x comes from its p-content (verify_numbers.py B). The reader's 'contradicts the wording, qualifies the substance' should read: the wording has partial support, at the string scale, from a source the flag does not cite.

(13): "to unify both X^{\mu} and Y_{\mu} in one space {\cal{P}} (that we often refer to as phase space) and introduce a dimensionless coordinate \mathbb{X} on {\cal{P}}". 1706.03305 §2: "In particular, the zero modes (x,\tilde{x},p,\tilde{p}) should be considered independent, being linear combinations of (x_{L},p_{L},x_{R},p_{R}) ." 1707.00312 §2: "\omega_{AB} can be thought of as a symplectic form on the reduced space coordinatized by \mathbb{X}^{A} , a subspace of the full phase space." 1606.01829 §1.1: "introduce dimensionless coordinates x\equiv q/\lambda and \tilde{x}\equiv p/\varepsilon measuring position and momentum in natural units." 1812.10821 §2: "\{\tilde{x}_{\mu},x^{\nu}\}=\pi\alpha^{\prime}\delta_{\mu}^{\nu}".

The only FLM paper here in which the dual coordinate is the momentum of one undoubled phase space is 1606.01829, on ordinary quantum mechanics, not strings or dark matter. So the record's contrast, foundations (momentum side, matching C1) against model (doubled phase space), is in the sources a contrast between that one paper and the string papers. On the coefficient: the metaparticle paper's own bracket gives [x, x̃] = iπℏα′, which is 2πiλ² in 1706.03305's λ = √(ℏα′/2) and iπλ² in 1502.08005's λ² = α′ℏ; the record's iλ² (from Hübsch and Minic 2024, not read here) holds only in a normalization with λ² = πℏα′ (normalizations.py). #### r6-foundations record point 2

Record says: papers/broken-born-checkpoint-v2.md §3 (as in work/dual-sector/inputs/program.md): "They treat the breaking as kinematical and call the choice "a dynamical question"." Repeated in wiki/phase-space/reciprocity/broken-born-duality.md (opening): "they wrote no potential for η and treated the breaking as kinematical, not as spontaneous symmetry breaking."

Source says: 1405.3949 §1.3: "Moreover, the purely kinematical splitting of phase space into space and momentum space has to be made dynamical, and thus both the phase space metric H_{AB} and the neutral metric \eta_{AB} have to be dynamical in the context of quantum gravity." 1405.3949 §1.1: "It is important to appreciate that this kinematical breaking is different from the dynamical breaking that arises in Schrödinger’s quantum mechanics, due to the choice of a particular Hamiltonian." 1502.08005 §8: "In the metastring formulation new CFT deformation modes are allowed, including the winding modes, whose collective excitations correspond to deformations of the \eta background."

Why (reader): Qualifies. 'Kinematical' is FLM's word for the breaking by general relativity (and by the usual string, in which all probes share one spacetime). Their proposal is to make the split dynamical, with η a dynamical background deformed by string modes. So in their own framework they do not treat the breaking as kinematical; what they do not do is treat it as spontaneous: no potential for η, no order parameter, no vacuum selection (zero matches for 'spontan' and 'order parameter' in all seven full texts). The program's claimed addition, the spontaneous reading with η as order parameter, still has no counterpart in these papers; the description of what FLM did would read correctly as 'dynamical but not spontaneous'. The wiki page's other claims (η dynamical, no potential, no symmetry-breaking analysis) agree with the sources.

**Verifier: confirmed.** Record wording checked in both files. I checked every 'kinematical' in 1405.3949 (12 uses) and 1502.08005 (4 uses). Each describes the breaking by gravity, or eta in gravity; FLM's own proposal is that 'the purely kinematical splitting ... has to be made dynamical'. #11's sentence matches FLM's word for general relativity's breaking ('this kinematical breaking', 1405.3949 sec. 1.1), so it is incomplete rather than misquoted. For FLM's own framework, 'dynamical, not spontaneous' is accurate: 'spontan', 'order parameter' and 'Goldstone' have zero matches in all seven papers, and no potential for eta appears. FLM also aim to restore the duality: 'a theory of quantum gravity ought to restore the kinematical duality principle' (1405.3949 sec. 1.1).

#### r6-foundations record point 3

FLM put the rigidity of quantum theory in the flatness of η and promote η, H and ω all to functions on phase space (1502.08005 §2.3), while P2′ keeps ω as the one rigid structure (ℏ) and makes η emergent; in the metastring the string's symplectic form is built from η, not ω (§4.2). The two frameworks hold different structures rigid.

[3], which is 1307.7080 (reference list checked). 1307.7080 (v1 2013-07-26) says '(eta,J) ... dynamical'. 1405.3949 (v1 2014-05-15) says 'This is our addition to Born's argument [1]' ([1] = 1307.7080, checked) and 'There is a preferred basis — the space-time basis.' The interval from 2013-07-26 to 2015-02-27 is 19 months, recomputed. The rigidity contrast also holds: FLM put quantum rigidity in eta's flatness, P2′ in omega. One addition: FLM attribute to Born the observation that gravity breaks the symmetry (1405.3949 sec. 1.1, 'One of the central insights of Born, 75 years ago [3], ...'; [3] = Born 1935, 1938, 1949). #11 sec. 3's credit to FLM is right for the polarization wording; by their own account the observation is Born's.

#### r6-foundations record point 4

Record says: notes/literature-check.md §3, 'What differs': "The vacuum. #11's polarization is selected spontaneously (P5). ... The check found no such mechanism in the school's papers, where the classical spacetime label "corresponds to a choice" of polarization."

Source says: 1502.08005 §7: "What is new about this viewpoint is that it allows us to think about the choice of space-time inside phase space as a dynamical question." and "The condition of L -projectability is a target space locality condition specifying which submanifold of {\cal{P}} can be understood as a space-time. What is conceptually remarkable here is that this target space locality condition is selected by demanding worldsheet locality." 1707.00312 §2: "Consequently, which compact coordinate, x or \tilde{x} , plays the role of a spatial coordinate depends on context." 1606.01829 §3: "A choice of quantum polarization is a choice of basis, unitarily equivalent to any other."

Why (reader): Agrees that there is no spontaneous mechanism, but understates what the foundations say about the choice. (1) η is a dynamical field with string excitations (1502.08005 §1, §8). (2) Worldsheet locality selects the locality condition (projectability; quantum mechanically Λ-periodicity) that singles out a Lagrangian as spacetime (§6.4, §7). (3) For a compact string the compactification radius decides: x at large R, x̃ at small R (1707.00312 §2–§3; 1706.03305 §5). (4) In quantum mechanics a polarization is a basis choice, unitarily equivalent to any other (1606.01829). None is a vacuum that breaks a symmetry spontaneously: (2) is a consistency condition and (3) a modulus. Under (3) the selected polarization depends on a background parameter, unlike #11 P5's vacuum selection; under (4) the choice is not physical at all, unlike #11 §3's 'the magnet must point'.

**Verifier: confirmed-in-part.** The quoted phrase is from Hubsch and Minic 2024 sec. 3 (arXiv:2407.06207), code-checked: 'Classical spacetime label x of canonical QFT corresponds to a choice of (classical spacetime) polarization in modular (quantum) spacetime with a contextuality parameter \lambda .' The reader's four points hold, and there is no spontaneous mechanism. Two corrections. (a) Point (4)'s 'not physical at all' overstates: 1606.01829 sec. 1.4 calls the Stone-von Neumann theorem 'fundamentally misleading in the present context' because 'different polarizations do have very different geometry, notions of regularity, and sets of C^infinity vectors'. (b) The reader omits 1405.3949 sec. 2, where the space-momentum split K is not required by the formulation and is found 'when we look at solutions', each string selecting its own Lagrangian. That is a selection by solutions, though not a single vacuum. Supporting the record: 1812.10821 sec. 1 poses 'the mechanisms for localization of strings (or their corresponding particle-like states) on such submanifolds' as an open question.

#### r6-foundations record point 5

Record says: papers/broken-born-checkpoint-v2.md §5, compatibility theorem: "Positive H satisfying both (η⁻¹H)² = 1 and (ω⁻¹H)² = −1 are exactly H = diag(g, g⁻¹), g ∈ Sym⁺(d)." This is no longer true if B does not vanish." 1502.08005 §2, footnote 9: "Here neutral means that \eta is of signature (d,d) , while H is of signature (2,2(d-1)) ." 1307.7080: "\eta is a neutral metric and H a generalized Lorentzian one".

Why (reader): Minor qualification. The attributed content is confirmed verbatim, but in a different setting: FLM's H is Lorentzian, built from a Lorentzian G and B, while #11 §5 states the theorem for positive H with g ∈ Sym⁺(d). The algebra carries over, but FLM did not state it for positive H; the positive-H version is the program's adaptation of their statement.

**Verifier: confirmed-in-part.** Confirmed: 1502.08005 sec. 2.1 states the attributed sentence verbatim, for a Lorentzian H (fn. 9: signature (2,2(d-1)); eq. 14: h Lorentzian; 1307.7080: 'a generalized Lorentzian one'). Refuted: the reader's 'FLM did not state it for positive H; the positive-H version is the program's adaptation'. 1606.01829 sec. 2 gives GL(d) = Sp(2d) ∩ O(d,d) as the group 'determining the vacuum metric H(x,xt) = x q x + xt q^-1 xt', with q = e delta e^T positive, and says 'the space of vacuum metrics associated with a given Hilbert space is encoded in GL(d)/O(d)'. Its sec. 3 names 'a positive definite form H'. My code (verify_numbers.py E, gl_intersection.py) confirms three things. This H satisfies (eta^-1 H)^2 = 1 and (omega^-1 H)^2 = -1 with the paper's own eta (eq. 17) and omega (eq. 7). Sp(2d) ∩ O(d,d) = {diag(A, A^-T)}. Its orbit of H0 = 1 is diag(A^T A, (A^T A)^-1), which is diag(g, g^-1) with g in Sym+(d). FLM assert this in group form without proving the 'exactly' direction, so #11's block proof and machine check remain the program's. #11 sec. 5 could cite 1606.01829 sec. 2 for the positive form.

#### r7-vacuum record point 0

Record says: papers/broken-born-checkpoint-v2.md (#11 v2.5) §3: "They treat the breaking as kinematical and call the choice \"a dynamical question\"." However when we look at solutions of string theory, we find this extra structure.' And [26S] itself, 1502.08005 §2.1, p. 11: 'When we introduce quantum theory into gravity, we have to make the neutral metric \eta dynamical, and thus in the context of quantum gravity, both H and \eta have to be dynamical.'

Why (reader): 'Kinematical' is FLM's word for the breaking by general relativity, which they credit to Born and want quantum gravity to undo. Their own proposal makes the splitting dynamical: K is absent from the action and present in solutions. 'They treat the breaking as kinematical' therefore describes what they say of GR, not what they propose, and understates them. The second clause is accurate (1502.08005 §7, p. 47). #11 P2′ already credits [26S] with a dynamical η, so §3 and P2′ say different things about the same paper.

**Verifier: confirmed-in-part.** The sources are as quoted (1405.3949 §1.1, §1.3, §2; 1502.08005 §2.1 p. 11; all FOUND). 'Kinematical' is FLM's word for GR's breaking, and their proposal makes the splitting dynamical. So the wiki's 'treated the breaking as kinematical', beside its own 'made η dynamical', misdescribes the proposal. Against #11 §3 the item is weaker than the reader says. (i) The same sentence reports the dynamical side, accurately ('a dynamical question', p. 47). (ii) The selector [26S] itself exhibits is a consistency condition: worldsheet locality selects L-projectability (§6.1 p. 39; §7 p. 47). The reader's own reading of that passage calls it 'a kinematical selection, as #11 §3 says'. (iii) 'Kinematical breaking' is 1405.3949's phrase; [26S] uses 'kinematical' only for η in GR. (iv) §3 and P2′ do not conflict: one reports FLM's view of the breaking, the other credits [26S] with a dynamical η. The worklog's sentence is scoped to 1307.7080 and 1405.3949 and is accurate for them.

#### r7-vacuum record point 1

Record says: papers/broken-born-checkpoint-v2.md §3: "This program's addition is to read it as *spontaneous* breaking, with η as the order parameter". wiki/phase-space/reciprocity/broken-born-duality.md (description): "they gave no potential and no symmetry-breaking analysis". notes/toy-lagrangian.md 'Chunk 1 results': "They write no potential and do no symmetry-breaking analysis".

Source says: 2104.00802 (Freidel, Kowalski-Glikman, Leigh, Minic; v1 1 April 2021, the passage present in the v1 PDF): 'Now since the modular fields and their decoupling limit are obtained by a choice of polarization it is clear that they do not transform covariantly under the dual Lorentz symmetry. In other words the dual Lorentz symmetry that acts on the free one metaparticle state is broken. The symmetry breaking happens at the level of the vacuum sector, and thus a choice of ground state fixes a value of \delta .' Also: 'Once a dual boost is performed the new commuting decoupling coordinate is no longer x , but x^{\mu}+\alpha^{\mu\nu}\tilde{x}_{\nu}+\cdots' and 'the infrared Lorentz violation appears only for an old Universe, as the symmetry is restored as one goes back in time.'

Why (reader): The school does analyse a symmetry breaking tied to the choice of polarization, published before the program. A symmetry that moves which phase-space coordinate is the commuting spacetime coordinate is broken 'at the level of the vacuum sector', the ground state fixes the frame, and the symmetry is restored in the past. 'No symmetry-breaking analysis' is therefore too strong.
What holds: there is no potential, the word 'spontaneous' is absent, nothing is called an order parameter (δ is what the ground state fixes, but they do not name it so), and no Goldstone modes appear. The essay also says the dual symmetry 'is fundamentally broken at the quantum level' by λ, so it does not separate explicit from vacuum breaking. The broken group is the non-compact dual Lorentz group of the doubled (x, x̃) space at λ², not ρ on (x, p) at ℏ.
The program's own addition narrows to the spontaneous formulation proper: the name, η as order parameter, the potential, the toy models' U(1) Goldstone angle and the K-sign lock. The record nowhere quotes these passages; the earlier check read 2104.00802 only for C1.

**Verifier: confirmed-in-part.** Confirmed: 2104.00802 (v1 2021-04-01, v1 PDF pp. 5–6) ties a symmetry breaking to the choice of polarization and places it 'at the level of the vacuum sector'. No record file quotes it: a repository grep for 'vacuum sector', 'decoupling frame' and 'choice of ground state' gives 0 hits, and the literature check cites the essay only for C1. Corrections: (1) 'the symmetry is restored as one goes back in time' is the Lorentz symmetry of dispersion relation (17), not the dual symmetry. (2) The essay's reason for the breaking is 'In other words the dual Lorentz action is not a canonical transformation': the broken symmetry does not preserve the commutator, whereas #11's ρ preserves ω. Two paragraphs with no vacua compared and no potential are a statement, not a symmetry-breaking analysis. So 'no symmetry-breaking analysis' survives narrowly; 'nothing on a breaking tied to the polarization' would not. (3) 'The word spontaneous is absent' holds for 2104.00802 only. 2212.06086 §4.3 (2022, citing 2104.00802 as [250]) calls the same group O(1,d−1)₂ 'effectively spontaneously broken'. So the program's own addition is narrower than the reader's list: an exact ω-preserving symmetry (ρ inside its U(1)) broken by the vacuum, η as order parameter, the potential, the Goldstone angle and the K-sign lock. The bare word 'spontaneous' has a school precedent. The coset U(d)/O(d) of η-choices at fixed ω and vacuum is named, as a space of choices, in 1606.01829 §2. (4) The worklog's sentence is scoped to 1307.7080 and 1405.3949 and stays accurate. What 2104.00802 and 2212.06086 contradict is the wiki's generalization to FLM's metastring work.

#### r7-vacuum record point 2

Record says: notes/literature-check.md §3, 'What differs', 'The vacuum': "The check found no such mechanism in the school's papers, where the classical spacetime label \"corresponds to a choice\" of polarization." 'No such mechanism' therefore holds only for a spontaneous-breaking mechanism with an order parameter and a potential. 'A vacuum that selects a polarization' joined to a dual dark sector is published, in this hedged form.
The quotation 'corresponds to a choice' is accurate (2407.06207 §3.3), but the sentence ends 'with a contextuality parameter λ', and §3.5 ties the polarization to 'the contextual nature of quantum measurement'. That is what this school paper says does the choosing.

But the reader overstates when it says 'a vacuum that selects a polarization' joined to a dual dark sector is published. The reader is right that 'corresponds to a choice' ends 'with a contextuality parameter λ' (2407.06207 §3.3).

'when the scale factor goes to zero the dual decoupling limit means that a dual universe made of dual particles is accessed.'

Why (reader): FKLM 2021 work a simple model, not only words. In the metaparticle dispersion relation in an expanding background (their eqs. 15–17), states with dual momentum decouple at late times, so the expansion picks the x frame over its T-dual partner, and the a → 0 limit gives the partner.
In the worklog's terms this uses outside input (the scale factor sets which modes are light), so its 'open' verdict stands only with that proviso. The wiki's paraphrase drops the proviso and says the mechanism is 'open in the literature', which overstates. The worklog does not cite 2104.00802.

**Verifier: confirmed-in-part.** Confirmed: the worklog does not cite 2104.00802 (repository grep), and its 'Claimed only in words' list omits FKLM 2021, which works a simple model (eqs. 13–17: dual-momentum states decouple as a_c → ∞). Not confirmed: the charge that the wiki overstates. FKLM's mechanism falls in the worklog's own class 'uses outside input: which modes are light', so the worklog's 'Open' stands as written. The wiki's paraphrase matches the worklog, whose S5 conclusion says of the strong form 'It is also open in the literature'. The wiki drops information, that outside-input candidates exist; it does not overstate.

#### r8-outside record point 0

Record says: notes/literature-check.md §3, 'What is published': "No statement independent of the group was found; Finkelstein 2008 (arXiv 2006) only speculates, in one sentence, that the momentum-energy coordinates of quantum events may contribute to dark matter (related, one agent's label)." The round-1 search log (review/literature-check/round1/g1-conjugate-matter.web_queries.json) records '"Dark Matter from Dual-Manifold Coupling" Sheng Ran' with "hits": "10 results; report not found", and review/literature-check/workflows/workflow1-result.json has 'Sheng Ran not retrieved'.

Source says: Sheng Ran, 'Dark Matter from Dual-Manifold Coupling' (doi:10.55277/researchhub.unu7fof1.1, first posted 2025-12-24; v2 .2, CC-BY PDF), abstract: "A recently proposed framework enlarges the Hilbert space of quantum theory by allowing a single quantum state to admit two conjugate dynamical descriptions—one on the familiar space–time manifold and one on a complementary momentum–energy manifold—thereby restoring a missing dynamical symmetry between the two. Here we show that dark matter arises naturally as a structural consequence of the coupling between these two dynamical sectors." §V: "it arises as an evanescent projection of a single quantum state across two dynamically conjugate manifolds."

Why (reader): This is a statement independent of the group: its author is at Washington University in St. Louis, and it cites neither the school nor Born. It puts the origin of dark matter in the ordinary Fourier-conjugate (k, E) description at ℏ, given dynamics of its own. It was posted before the program's September 2026 notebook, and an open copy exists (OpenAlex lists the CC-BY PDF). It is not either of the program's forms: no χ particles, no velocity gate, no magnetic monopoles. Its dark matter is a projected state, not a counterpart copy of ordinary matter. So whether it is C1's core depends on the readings the record lists in its §2. On the reading in which ordinary momentum at ħ belongs to C1, it is nearer to C1 than the school's metaparticle, whose x̃ is at the string scale.

**Verifier: confirmed-in-part.** Quotations of source and record are verbatim (Ran's PDF; note lines 169–171; round1/g1-conjugate-matter.web_queries.json; workflow1-result.json). The source says what the reader says, and v1 says it too: the v1 PDF (dated 15 December 2025, posted 24 December 2025) differs from v2 only in its date line and reference formatting. But the record did not miss the report. Round 3 retrieved it (PDF and Wayback abstract), verified four quotes and labeled it C1 'partial'. Its verifier downgraded the label to 'related' (round3/oa-c1-c6-c7.verify.json; findings.json key doi:10.55277/researchhub.unu7fof1.1, basis 'verified'), because the report 'never says dark matter is the counterpart of ordinary matter' and because of the rule for non-reviewed venues. The round-3 sweep's statement, carried into panel/C1.evidence.json, even says that 'no source other than Sheng Ran's removed ResearchHub preprint states that dark matter is the matter of momentum space, the conjugate or reciprocal sector, the dual half of phase space'. What survives is narrower than the reader claims. By the record's own labels the note's sentence is defensible, since every anticipates is the school's. It is incomplete: it names Finkelstein's one-sentence speculation (related) as the outside near-miss and omits Ran's report (related, verified), which states its result as a finding. The record's 'removed' is also inexact. The landing pages 404, but both CC-BY PDFs still download from storage.prod.researchhub.com, and OpenAlex lists v2's PDF. That the report is C1's core is the reader's judgement, against the record's verified label; it rests on the §2 reading in which ordinary momentum at ℏ belongs to C1.

#### r8-outside record point 1

Record says: wiki/method/literature-check.md, C1 row, 'Not found': "the identification joined to a sector coupled to ours by gravity alone (mirror and twin matter couple by gravity, without reciprocity)". review/wiki-build-flags.md item 64: "Copies of ordinary matter coupled to ours mainly or only by gravity are published without reciprocity (mirror and twin matter); joined to the identification they were not found." notes/literature-check.md §3: "All nine come from one group and carry one priority, Minic 2020".

Source says: Häuser and Dröscher, Z. Naturforsch. A 74, 387 (online 2019-04-10), abstract: "Dark matter is assumed to reside in D d S 1 , 3 $DdS^{1,3}$ and therefore is principally invisible. On the other hand, its gravitational interaction with ordinary matter ( m ≥ 0) in spacetime d S 1 , 3 $dS^{1,3}$ is directly perceptible." Part III (online 2021-11-04), abstract: "With this construction visible and dark matter both represent positive energies, and hence gravitation makes no distinction between these two types of matter." Nishimura, arXiv:2212.13131 (2022-12-16): "The negative norm sector of the universe |\Psi_{-}\rangle , which has no gauge interactions with the positive norm universe |\Psi_{+}\rangle , will interact with |\Psi_{+}\rangle through gravity after symmetry breaking" and "is to describe dark side of the universe responsible for the dark matter and the dark energy in cosmology".

Why (reader): This qualifies the record rather than contradicting it. Outside the school, dark matter placed in a 'dual spacetime' and coupled to ours by gravity alone is published twice. One instance (Häuser–Dröscher, 325 days before Minic 2020) also holds it to be in principle undetectable, a permanent direct-detection null like #11 §13 row 7. Nishimura's dark sector comes with the spontaneous breaking of a dual symmetry. Neither duality is position–momentum: one is an imaginary-time dual de Sitter space that shares our spatial coordinates, the other an internal isospin spacetime. So the record's 'not found' holds for the Born-reciprocal identification, but not for gravity-only dark matter in a dual spacetime in general, and not for priority of the phrase 'dark matter resides in the dual spacetime'. Neither paper is in the record. Häuser–Dröscher Part II is not on INSPIRE; I found it through OpenAlex and read it in its abstract only.

**Verifier: confirmed-in-part.** Source and record wording are quoted correctly (Crossref abstracts of Häuser–Dröscher Parts II and III; Nishimura full text; wiki row line 45; flag 64; note line 166). A grep of the repository outside work/ finds neither paper. As the reader says, this qualifies the record rather than contradicting it. The 'Not found' cell is about the identification joined to gravity-only coupling, and neither paper states the identification: their dual spacetimes are an imaginary-time dual de Sitter space and an internal isospin spacetime, not momentum space. Two corrections. (1) For Häuser–Dröscher, 'by gravity alone' is an inference from abstracts ('principally invisible', 'principally undetectable', gravitational interaction 'directly perceptible'); no 'only' is stated. (2) Nishimura goes further than reported. As a conditional, his negative-norm sector, assigned to the dual isospin spacetime, coupled by gravity alone and proposed as the dark side, would hold 'almost all the antiparticles'. That makes it a counterpart of visible matter in a dual space, though not a Born dual. The record's examples of published gravity-only copies could add these dual-spacetime cases. Part II (online 2019-04-10) predates Minic 2020 by 325 days (scripts/day_gaps.py).

#### r9-sweep record point 0

Record says: notes/literature-check.md §3, 'What their dual is', first bullet: 'In the foundations it is the momentum side. Freidel, Leigh and Minic 2014 (Phys. Lett. B): … Berglund, Hübsch and Minić 2021 speak of the “canonically conjugate dual space”.'

Source says: 2010.15610 §2, eq. (3): '[x̂^a, x̂̃_b] = 2πiλ²δ^a_b … where \lambda denotes the fundamental length scale, such as the fundamental Planck scale', then 'Hence, the weakness of gravity is determined by the size of the canonically conjugate dual space, while the smallness of the cosmological constant is given by its curvature.' 1905.09463 footnote 4 defines the phrase: 'the canonically conjugate dual space, spanned by the difference of the left- and right-movers' (x̃_a = x^a_L − x^a_R).

Why (reader): In the BHM papers 'canonically conjugate dual space' names the doubled coordinate x̃ = x_L − x_R. It is conjugate to x at the string scale λ², which is the same x̃ the record's next bullet places 'at the string scale', and it is not momentum at ℏ. The foundations claim has other support: FLM 2014's 'coordinates in momentum space', and x̃ ≡ p/b in Minic 2020 §3, EMT 2021 §2.2, FLM 2016 §1.1 and HM 2024 §3.3. This citation does not support it, so the record's wording overstates what BHM 2021 says.

**Verifier: confirmed.** Checked against the source. In BHM 2021 itself (2010.15610 §2) the coordinates X^A 'combine the sum ( x^{a} ) and the difference ( \mathord{\tilde{x}}_{a} ) of the left- and right-moving chiral bosons on the string', with [x̂, x̂̃] = 2πiλ² (eq. 3). The 'canonically conjugate dual space' sentence follows Tseytlin's leading limit, obtained 'effectively neglecting \omega_{AB} in (2) by assuming that [\hat{x},\mathord{\hat{\tilde{x}}}]=0' (both FOUND). 1905.09463 footnote 4 defines the phrase the same way. So that citation does not support 'the momentum side'. FLM 2014's 'coordinates in momentum space' and x̃ ≡ p/b (Minic 2020 §3, EMT 2021 §2.2, FLM 2016 §1.1, HM 2024 §3.3) do support it. Flag 64 cites only FLM 2014 for the foundations, so the overstatement is in the note's §3 alone.

#### r9-sweep record point 1

Record says: notes/literature-check.md §3, 'What differs', 'The vacuum': 'The check found no such mechanism in the school's papers, where the classical spacetime label “corresponds to a choice” of polarization.' Also wiki/phase-space/reciprocity/broken-born-duality.md (description): Freidel, Leigh and Minic 'gave no potential and no symmetry-breaking analysis'; wiki/glossary.md, 'Born duality' entry, the same.

Source says: 2104.00802 (Freidel, Kowalski-Glikman, Leigh, Minic 2021), at eqs. (11)–(12): the presence of λ 'means that the dual symmetry is fundamentally broken at the quantum level'; 'The symmetry breaking happens at the level of the vacuum sector, and thus a choice of ground state fixes a value of' δ; 'We now propose that the expansion of the Universe can act as a mechanism that selects the decoupling frame'. 2212.06086 §4.3, of O(1,d−1)₂ within a sector of fixed non-zero p̃: 'is effectively spontaneously broken. Here the novel feature is that the internal symmetry is non-compact'. 1502.08005 §7: 'this target space locality condition is selected by demanding worldsheet locality.'

Why (reader): The school does propose a selection mechanism. Cosmic expansion selects the decoupling frame, and so which commuting combination (x, or x + αx̃ + …) serves as spacetime, with the breaking placed in the vacuum sector and fixed by a choice of ground state. BHM also call the dual Lorentz group 'effectively spontaneously broken'. None of this is spontaneous breaking of Born reciprocity ρ with η as order parameter, and none writes a potential, so #11 §3's narrower claim of its own addition may stand. But 'no such mechanism' and 'no symmetry-breaking analysis' are too broad for the school. The wiki sentence is accurate for the three FLM papers (1307.7080, 1405.3949, 1502.08005), where the sweep found no 'spontaneous', but FKLM 2021 shares those three authors.

**Verifier: confirmed-in-part.** The quotations are right, and no record file has them: no 'vacuum sector' or 'decoupling frame' in findings.json or panel/C1.evidence.json.
- What the school does say: FKLM 2021 breaks a symmetry 'at the level of the vacuum sector', fixes the decoupling coordinate by 'a choice of ground state', and proposes cosmic expansion as 'a mechanism that selects the decoupling frame'. BHM 2023 §4.3 calls O(1,d−1)₂ 'effectively spontaneously broken'.
- So 'no such mechanism' is too broad if 'such' means any vacuum mechanism that selects which commuting coordinates serve as spacetime. 'No symmetry-breaking analysis' is too broad for FKLM, who include all three FLM authors.
- Where the reader overreaches: 'spontaneous in substance' is its inference. FKLM say the dual symmetry is 'fundamentally broken at the quantum level' because 'the dual Lorentz action is not a canonical transformation', i.e. broken by the λ² bracket. The symmetry is dual Lorentz, not ρ. The coordinates selected (x, x + αx̃ + …) live in the λ²-doubled space. The expansion yields 'a preferred frame' only late, 'as the symmetry is restored as one goes back in time'.
- No potential and no order parameter appear (Goldstone: 0 hits). #11 §3's narrower claim, spontaneous breaking of ρ with η as the order parameter, stands.

#### r9-sweep record point 3

Record says: notes/literature-check.md §3 plain-language passage: the school's dark sector 'is correlated with ours by construction, where the program's couples to ours by gravity alone'; 'What differs': 'Their dual sector is correlated with ours by construction'; wiki/summary.md line 34: 'their sector correlated with ours'.

Source says: 2212.06086 §6.3: 'These fields are in general correlated, due to their non-commutativity. However, to first order in the fundamental length, we can treat them as independent.' §6.2: 'In general the observed spacetime and its dual are correlated, which should be important for phenomenology, but to first order in the fundamental length we will treat them as independent sectors.' §6.4: the mixing 'is invisible to effective field theory, and it vanishes as either' λ₄ → 0 or L → ∞; footnote 43: 'so here we leave them unspecified'. 2003.00318 eqs. (49)–(50): after the x̃-integration L_m(A(x)) and L̃_dm(Ã(x)) sit on one √g(x) '(where we have included the gravitational background as non-dynamical)'.

Why (reader): 'Correlated' is the school's own word and follows from its construction, but the school puts the correlation beyond leading order. At zeroth order, and in BHM's words to first order in the fundamental length, the visible and dual sectors are independent and share only the metric, as in #11 P4. The non-gravitational pieces are suppressed by powers of λ, invisible in EFT, and vanish as λ → 0 or L → ∞: mixing ∝ λ⁸/L¹⁰ or λ₄²/L⁴, a fermion–dual-fermion mass mixing, and non-commuting fields. Their size is never computed. The contrast with 'gravity alone' is therefore a contrast at sub-leading order, which the record's wording does not say. It understates how close the two are at leading order.

**Verifier: confirmed-in-part.** Correct that BHM 2023 §6.2–§6.3 treat the two sectors as independent to first order in the fundamental length, and that the mixing is invisible in EFT and vanishes as λ → 0 or L → ∞.
- The record does say part of this: note §3 reports the round-1 verifier's finding that the 2021 paper calls the correlation 'invisible in effective field theory, vanishing as λ → 0 or L → ∞'. 'Which the record's wording does not say' is therefore too strong.
- The school does not present the correlation as negligible: 'with a particular double scaling, this term can be finite!' and λ⁸/L¹⁰ 'should not be naively considered to be trans-Planckian' (BHM 2021 §4); 'the usual visible photon/dark photon coupling is subdominant to this term' (BHM 2021 §5, BHM 2023 §6.5); the mass gap 'may well be phenomenologically relevant — even for a minuscule numerical value' (BHM 2023 §6.5); and such a correlation 'has been observed in astronomical data' (BHM 2021 §4). 'Suppressed by powers of λ' is therefore the reader's characterization, not the school's.
- The strongest support for the reader's direction is a passage the reader missed: HM 2024 §5.3, gravity-only coupling below a decoupling energy (next entry).

#### r9-sweep record point 4

Record says: notes/literature-check.md §3, 'What their dual is', second bullet: 'The identification matches C1 in their foundations; their model doubles phase space where the program polarizes one.'

Source says: 2212.06086 §6.4: 'First the fields and their duals are “canonically conjugate” like q and p' … 'the dual particles are conjugate to the Standard Model particles in analogy with the canonical fact that momenta p are conjugate to coordinates q' … 'That is, the dual fields do not commute with Standard Model fields, and the two form an effective Heisenberg algebra.' 2003.00318 §3: 'So even though one has formally doubled the number of variables, the chiral nature of the theory, keeps the total number of degrees of freedom the same as in the purely electric formulation.'

Why (reader): The record finds the match with C1 only in the foundations (x̃ ≡ p/b) and describes the model as a doubling. Yet the section just before the one the record cites for the identification (BHM 2023 IJMPD §6.5) states a conjugacy inside the model itself: the dark (dual) fields are canonically conjugate to the visible fields, as p to q, induced from [x, x̃]. That is a third sense of 'conjugate', in field space, and closer in wording to 'conjugate matter' than anything the record quotes. Minic 2020 §3 adds that the doubled formulation does not double the degrees of freedom. My reading: the record understates the overlap in wording; whether field-space conjugacy is the program's exchange of polarizations is open.

**Verifier: confirmed-in-part.** The §6.4 passage exists, one section before the §6.5 sentence the record cites, and no record file has it ('canonically conjugate like': no hits).

It is a conjugacy of field values inside the doubled model, and the record's 'their model doubles phase space' stays true.
- What changes is the overlap in wording: 'the dual particles are conjugate to the Standard Model particles' is closer to 'conjugate matter' than anything the record quotes.
- A further qualification of 'matches C1 in their foundations': even in EMT 2021 §2.2, where x̃ ≡ p/b, the dual fields come from 'the dual momenta p and \tilde{p} (to x and \tilde{x} respectively)', i.e. from the doubled phase space.
